  are done (2026-10-08, §9). A household out of food weighs leaving against what its own food,
  its neighbours' spare and a store it knows of would carry it through, and the harvest its
  fields should bring (content API 32). The first office is a storekeeper: a law naming its
  holder, under whose roof the store keeps and at whose home relief is asked; when they die or
  leave, the law lapses and a new one must be proposed (saves 30, wire 1.28, content API 33).
  This is ADR-0013 §2's office in a first form, without its general primitive (§9). Its fifth
  step is done: each polity is labelled from its saved history by a pure classifier that nothing
  in the world reads, with its reasons, in the Government panel (wire 1.29). Its sixth step is
  done: the notables' gate passes, after its first failure showed the tier built short of ADR-0014
  §4, and a sponsor now remembers how a gathering voted (content API 34). The demo lives one
  village through its lean seasons (§9). **M4a is implemented (2026-10-08):** the dashboard passes
  on its last build (§9). Not built, and carried forward: office pay and land tenure held as a
  law (the pipeline carries a levy, relief and one office); ADR-0013 §2's general Office primitive
  (the first office is a law naming its holder); slice X's speed goal (a year of 1,000 people at
  Max takes about eight minutes, not two); and word of a gathering (every adult hears of it, until
  M4c). M4b, crime and order, is designed ([ADR-0015](decisions/0015-incidents-cases-obligations.md),
  four slices); all four are implemented (§9): AA, theft and what was seen; AB, cases and their
  decision; AC, the watch and corruption; and AD, curfews, the crime row and the demo. M4c,
  factions and unrest, is next.

**M4b: Crime and order.**
- *Contents:*
  - Theft as a person's choice, scored like any other; incidents, and what was seen.
  - Cases: who is suspected, who tells, what is decided, by an office or the gathering (no
    courts).
  - The watch: an office whose hours are its enforcement capacity, with a backlog.
  - Punishments as obligations: restitution, compensation, a fine, labour, exile. No corporal
    punishment or execution in v0.
  - Corruption v0: an officer's choice to take, or to look away, scored like theft.
  - Curfew and prohibitions in the law pipeline.
  - The dashboard's crime row, graded by its direction.
- *Demo:* a theft from the act to its end: whether anyone saw it, who was told, the case, what
  was decided, the obligation and whether it was met.
- *Design (2026-10-08):* one ADR, [ADR-0015](decisions/0015-incidents-cases-obligations.md)
  (incidents, cases and obligations), from the M4 crime brief (`docs/briefs/m4-crime.md`). Four
  slices:
  - **AA**: theft and what was seen. `Take` as a scored choice behind a moral filter; arrival
    settles whether anyone is home or in sight; incidents as the kernel's truth; each person's
    objection to taking and perceived risk; victims notice a loss and learn who took only from
    those who saw; telling along ties; refusing known takers; a demand for restitution as the
    first obligation. Saves, the `take` and `restitution` channels, and an observer that shows
    what happened beside what people believe. *Implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* saves schema 31,
    wire 1.30 (the Takings panel), content API 35. Not built: an objection moved by what the
    person lives beyond their upbringing (M4c's norms), and reporting (AB).
  - **AB**: cases and their decision. A law against taking, from a content template with a
    sanction bundle at levels people propose; a report opens a case with its sourced leads; the
    gathering (or an office the law names) decides it as it decides a law; restitution,
    compensation, a fine to the store, labour-days and exile as obligations, with arrears and
    default rules. *Implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* saves schema 32, wire 1.31 (the case in the
    Takings panel; a gathering's cases in the Government panel), content API 36. Not built:
    labour-days (no polity work exists to owe), an office that hears cases (the gathering hears
    them all), and a default rule beyond the arrear (refusing what a finding imposes has no
    further sanction until a watch exists, AC).
  - **AC**: the watch and corruption v0. A watch named by a law, as the storekeeper is, whose
    holders walk night rounds and see only where they are; its hours and backlog reported; an
    officer's choice to take a payment or look away, on a `bribe` channel. *Implemented
    (2026-10-08, §9):* saves schema 33, wire 1.32 (the watch's record in the Government panel;
    what it did with a taking in the Takings panel's truth layer), content API 37. Not built: a
    watch of several, a rotating household duty and office pay; exposure of a watcher's silence
    (nobody learns of it in v0); and removal from office.
  - **AD**: curfew and prohibitions in the law pipeline, the dashboard's crime row graded by its
    direction (true takings by household food days, recorded ones beside them), and the demo.
    *Implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* saves schema 34, wire 1.33 (a curfew's breaches in the
    Government panel), content API 38; the M4b demo (`web/e2e/m4b-demo.spec.ts`, with civ-sim's
    `theft_world` example). Not built: closing a place (a grove, a fishing ground), which a
    household could not forecast without a record of what it gathers; any sanction for breaking a
    curfew (the watch neither stops nor reports those out at night).

**M4c: Factions and unrest.**
- *Contents:*
  - Grievance (harm and blame) and information spreading through ties, hearsay as evidence.
  - Opinion, ideology and norms.
  - Factions with treasuries on the ledger.
  - Episodes (protest, riot, coup, revolt) as each person's choices; the winners rebuilding the
    constitution.
  - God tools: whisper, bless or curse, ideology, agitator.
  - The dashboard's regimes row.
- *Demo:* M4's: the same start, five seeds lived forty years, their regimes shown side by side,
  and one law's full history. If fewer than two regimes arise, that is reported, not forced.
- *Design (2026-10-08):* two ADRs, [ADR-0016](decisions/0016-grievances-claims-opinions.md)
  (grievances, claims and opinions, and the interventions that touch them) and
  [ADR-0017](decisions/0017-factions-episodes-regime-change.md) (factions, episodes and changes
  of regime), from the M4 factions brief (`docs/briefs/m4-factions.md`). Six slices:
  - **AE**: claims and grievances. News as shared claims heard through contact (a gathering
    called, a law passed or turned down, a case found), replacing every adult's hearing of a
    gathering; grievances (harm, the party blamed, the expectation broken, the unresolved part,
    a fading activation) from the polity's own acts: a levy taken in a lean year, relief refused
    from a store holding food, a finding, a case not found; the inspector shows a person's
    grievances and what they have heard.
    *Implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* word of a gathering by contact, only those who heard coming;
    grievances from an empty store, a levy taken in a lean year that leaves a household short of
    a year's food, a finding, a case not found or unheard, and a finding's obligation refused,
    told at the hearth and made good by relief; saves schema 35, wire 1.34 (a person's grievances and news in the
    inspector), content API 39. Not built: news of a law or a finding as claims (who knows a law
    stays on the law, as since M4a, and what people believe of a taking stays ADR-0015's), and
    relief refused from a store that holds food (the store gives what it holds, so nobody
    refuses).
  - **AF**: amending the custom. Templates that change who belongs to the body (all adults,
    household elders, landholders) and its rule (quorum, how many must be for), proposed by those
    who forecast their household's voice and gain under the new rule, or who hold grievances
    against the body; the gathering decides them by its present rule; constitution versions with
    the law that made each; the label follows.
    *Implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* the `amend_body` template (members: adults, elders,
    landholders; a quarter or a half; more for or two-thirds), weighed one change at a time by
    those a gathering overruled or who hold a grievance against it, judged by how the new rule
    would have decided the laws and cases they saw, and only when it would have served the
    sponsor's household; the present custom decides it; members alone come, stand and count;
    custom versions with the amendment that made each, the chronicle's amendment entry, and the
    label reading the share the body admits. Saves schema 36, wire 1.35 (the custom's versions
    and how many may decide, in the Government panel), content API 40.
  - **AG**: opinion, ideology and norms. Positions with anchors from the household's material
    position, slow values from upbringing, norm templates (sharing in need, the gathering binds)
    with endorsement and expectations; ideologies as content whose proposals people weigh among
    their moves; positions moving stances and norms supplying grievances' expectations.
    *Step one, opinion, implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* a position on each question a policy
    template asks, anchored on the first of each month in the forecast of the household's lot
    and pulled toward it with a two-year half-life; companions at the hearth say where they
    stand, and a listener moves a little toward a trusted one, less the further apart; at a
    gathering, how far talk has moved someone from their household's lot weighs in their stance
    and the record says so. Saves schema 37, wire 1.36 (positions in the inspector, talk in a
    stance's reason), content API 41.
    *Step two, norms, implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* the `norm` content kind and the core norm
    that what the gathering decides binds; each person's endorsement (drawn by a key, pulled
    toward the parents'), belief of how many households abide and threshold; the points they add
    for paying a levy or keeping a curfew, in place of the flat custom; beliefs moved only by
    companions telling of their own household's last levy. Saves schema 38, wire 1.37 (norms in
    the inspector), content API 42.
    *Step three, values, implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* the `value` content kind and three core
    values (safety from want and harm, a household's say over what is its own, giving back what
    was given or taken); each person's, drawn by a key and pulled toward the parents', fixed for
    life; templates' `[bears]`; what a law does to what someone holds dear weighs in their anchor,
    their stance (and the record says so) and their weighing of proposing it. Everyone takes their
    values and norms the first midnight they are here. Saves schema 39, wire 1.38 (values in the
    inspector, their part in a stance), content API 43.
    *Step four, ideologies, implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* the `ideology` content kind (problem,
    commitments, program, legitimacy, how it travels) and three core ideologies; holdings with
    whom each was had from, brought by some founders, taken up from a parent or from a trusted
    companion at the hearth by fit; commitments weighing beside values; holders weighing their
    program among their moves when the problem it explains is before the village; a law's creed
    in its record. Saves schema 40, wire 1.39 (ideologies in the inspector, the creed in a law's
    issue), content API 44. Slice AG is implemented.
  - **AH**: factions and episodes. Factions founded and joined as scored choices, treasuries on
    the ledger; petitions, assemblies and refusals of a levy called by organizers and attended as
    a scored activity; the gathering's or an office's answer; damage and violence as separate
    choices, recorded apart.
    *Design (2026-10-08; research 04-10 §1.1–§1.8, §5.1–§5.4, 09-12 §1.5, §2.2; ADR-0017
    §2–§3):* three steps.
    *Step one, factions, implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* a faction organizes those who hold
    grievances against one party, the gathering or an office. Someone who feels theirs keenly and
    has heard that others they trust hold one against it too may found one (not within a year of
    one of theirs ending); each adult reviews
    where they belong on a day of their own each month, weighing their grievance, their regard for
    its organizer, how many of those they know belong and its dues against a keyed threshold, and
    leaves only when that falls a margin below it. Membership is kept apart from belief. Its store
    is a holder on the ledger, fed by dues from members' threshing (channel `dues`) up to a reserve
    of their food, and giving to members' households short of food; its longest-standing member takes up its organizing when
    the organizer is gone, and one with no members ends. Saves schema 41 (`factions`), wire 1.40
    (a person's faction and why, the Government panel's factions), content API 45 (`[faction]`).
    *Step two, petitions, implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* a faction's organizer, at their monthly
    review, weighs calling a petition at the hearth once it has three members, none of its
    petitions waits and its last was half a year ago or more. Its demand answers the party its
    members blame (04-10 §1.1): against the gathering, the common store at another of its shares,
    or at none, in place of the one in force, whichever its members' households forecast most
    for; against an office, another holder in place of the one in it, whom the organizer would
    name as any proposer names a keeper. Calling is worth the members' mean grievance and how
    many of those the organizer knows belong, less its cost, against their threshold. Word goes
    out from the organizer to its members and on by word of mouth. Coming is a scored activity on
    the evening it sits, for an adult who heard of it, with 04-10 §5.3's terms from the person's
    own records: their grievance, belonging (or regard for the organizer), and the share of those
    they know who belong, which for a keyed fifth of people adds less once half do (free-riding,
    04-10 §1.4). At the next midnight a gathering is free, the organizer proposes the demand as it
    was called (issue `petition`), the gathering decides it as any law (for a new officeholder,
    weighing regard for the one named against regard for the one replaced, which a grievance
    against the office lowers), and passing it
    supersedes the law in force; a petition turned down, split or without a quorum is a new
    grievance to each who came. A petition records those who came (unique participants); it sits one evening.
    Saves schema 42, wire 1.41 (the Government panel's petitions), content API 46.
    Assemblies with other demands (another template, an amendment of the custom) are not built.
    *Step three, refusals of a levy, implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* where no petition can be
    called, an organizer whose members blame the gathering or the store's keeper may call on them
    to keep back the store's levy together, openly, for a year; it is worth what a petition is
    less what the norms the organizer holds weigh for abiding by what the gathering decided, so a
    petition is always preferred where one can be called. Each who heard of it weighs joining it
    at their threshing as they weigh coming to a petition; the law records levies kept back in a
    refusal apart from those kept back unannounced. Saves schema 43, wire 1.42, content API 47.
    *Damage and violence: moved to AI (2026-10-08, §9).* Violence is an interaction process that
    answers encounters (04-10 §1.8), and no one in these villages yet collects, disperses or
    represses: built now, the choice would never fire or would fire without cause.
  - **AI**: seizure and founding. A watch of several as the first office of force; coups among
    its holders, revolts by factions whose programs replace the body; founding by bargaining from
    the inherited constitution; succession, amendment and replacement told apart. With the office
    of force come the encounters that damage and violence answer (moved from AH): damage to
    property and violence against people as separate per-person choices within an episode,
    recorded apart from peaceful action and from violence by officeholders, with every death or
    injury resolved to a record and a cause (06-10), under 06-10 §4's presentation rules.
    *Design (2026-10-08):* the revolt comes first, as it needs no force (ADR-0017 §4: it succeeds
    through officeholders' own choices), and early farming villages' breakdowns are disputes over
    the stored surplus ending in new councils, not armies (09-11 §3.1). Four steps:
    *Step one, a faction's revolt, implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* a faction whose members blame
    the gathering holds a program: of the bodies the content's amendments offer, the one under
    which the decisions its members saw would have gone most their way (AF's test), if any would.
    Where it can call neither a petition nor a refusal, its organizer may weigh declaring that
    this body decides from now on, without the custom's procedure, at what a petition is worth
    less `revolt_cost` and less the norms they hold (breaking the custom itself). Word goes out as
    a petition's does. Each adult who has heard of it chooses each day to stand with it, with the
    gathering, or with neither (04-10 §5.3's terms, the share of those they know who stood with it
    the day before, and a cost for one the new body would leave out; 09-11 §1.6's exclusion
    risk), and the keeper and the watch choose too: theirs are the store and the watch (09-11
    §1.5: administration and coercion). It holds when every officeholder stands with it and more
    adults stand with it than with the gathering for seven days (09-11 §2.2's coding); then the
    body becomes its program, a version of the custom *taken*, not amended, and the chronicle says
    so. It fails when its organizer or faction is gone or `revolt_days` pass. Losers keep their
    wealth, ties and grievances. Saves schema 44, wire 1.43, content API 48.
    *Step two, founding, implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* the new body, as its first business,
    decides whether the laws in force and the offices stand (provisional authority, 09-11 §1.6),
    each member weighing them as any law, so a seizure ends in a bargain from the inherited
    constitution rather than a blank. As built: for `founding_days` after the custom is taken,
    every member of the new body weighs proposing an end to each law the old custom made (a
    store's levy at none, as a petition would; any other law repealed, the repeal carried), each
    household weighing an end as what the law, kept, would bring it, turned about; each law is put
    once, and what the body leaves stands. Saves schema 45, wire 1.44, content API 49.
    *Step three, a watch of several and coups, implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* a further watcher
    may be proposed while takings continue; among several, one who blames the gathering may
    weigh taking the deciding for the watch, each watcher choosing to back them, the gathering,
    neither, or to leave (09-11 §1.4: a coordination contest, attempt and success apart). As
    built: a watch may always have one more, each adding what the others leave unguarded; a
    watcher of several who blames the gathering weighs a coup at their monthly review; the
    watchers alone take sides each day, and it holds once more back it than the gathering for a
    week, the body then being those who keep the watch, a founding following. Leaving the watch
    in a coup waits for step four, where staying has a cost. Saves schema 46, wire 1.45, content
    API 50.
    *Step four, force, damage and violence, implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* what an office of
    force may do (enforce a finding, keep a seized store) and the encounters it meets, with
    injuries and deaths resolved to records and causes under 06-10. As built: a watcher may go to
    take what a refused finding owed; each adult of the household lets it be taken, stands in the
    way or strikes; where any resisted, the watcher takes it by force or turns back; every blow
    is a record (who struck whom, how long it kept them from work, or that it killed them), a
    blow keeps the one struck from work, and a death by it is a death by violence. Keeping a
    seized store, and damage to property, are not built. Saves schema 47, wire 1.46, content API
    51.
  - **AJ**: the god tools (whisper, bless or curse, ideology, agitator), the dashboard's regimes
    row and the M4 demo.
    *Design (2026-10-08, §9):* ADR-0016 §5 decides the tools, so no new ADR. Each use is an
    intervention record, saved with its number, its target and what it submitted; the chronicle
    logs it as one recorded influence, and the records it touches carry its number. The
    inspector shows whether it was perceived, weighed, acted on and passed on, read from the
    records people keep, consuming no draws. Three steps:
    *Step one, a whisper and an ideology heard of:* a whisper places one true claim the
    settlement's word already holds (a gathering or a petition called, a refusal or a revolt
    declared, another's grievance) in one person's hearing, as if heard, its salience the
    claim's own; an ideology is heard of by one person from no one they know, and weighed as one
    heard at the hearth is, by how well it fits what they hold dear, with a draw keyed to the
    person and the ideology alone, so a repeat changes nothing, and weighed again at their
    reviews while it is fresh, so only a change in them can change the answer.
    *Implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* intervention records (saves schema 48), the chronicle's one
    recorded influence, the whisper of a claim from the person's news, and an ideology told of,
    weighed at once and at their monthly reviews while fresh (`news_days`); the inspector shows
    the news that may be whispered and what came of each influence. Wire 1.47.
    *Step two, the agitator, blessings and curses:* an agitator is one adult newcomer sent by
    the path that sends a family, holding the ideology chosen, bound by every law and starting
    with a stranger's ties; a blessing or a curse moves a person's own keyed draws for illness or
    accident and for finding things out by a stated share of their quantile for a stated period,
    never a decision's draw, a vote, a tie, detection or punishment, and records each outcome it
    turned.
    *Implemented (2026-10-08, §9):* the agitator, one adult with a household of their own,
    holding the ideology chosen and knowing nobody, sent with a map tool; a blessing or a curse
    for a month to five years, a little, a quarter or half the way, scaling the person's own
    chance of illness or accident (never hunger's) and of finding things out, each draw it turned
    counted on its record and told in the chronicle. Saves schema 49, wire 1.48.
    *Step three, the regimes row and the M4 demo:* the dashboard's regimes row grades whether
    two worlds' labels differ in who may decide or who leads (the label's own reasons naming the
    difference), with the classifier frozen before the first run; the M4 demo lives five seeds
    of one start forty years, shows their regimes side by side and one law's full history, and
    reports fewer than two regimes if that is what happened.
    *Implemented (2026-10-09, §9):* the regimes row, graded on each world's label at the end and
    showing each tenth year's (green: two regimes over fifty years); the M4 demo
    (`web/e2e/m4-demo.spec.ts`, from the dashboard's kept saves of year 40, with a coda at year
    50 for any world whose regime changed). At forty years all five worlds were council
    communities under the founding custom, which the demo reports; by fifty, one had become an
    oligarchy. The dashboard's food prices row was red in one world (§9). A decision's tally now
    names the members of the body that decided it.

**M5: Neighbors**, split (2026-10-09, §9) into three milestones, each meeting the usable bar
with its own demo. M5's original contents were:
- 2–3 settlements: world-setup founding groups plus **splinter founding**.
- Inter-settlement roads, merchants and caravans.
- Per-polity currencies once minting exists.
- Diffusion of technology and style through contact.
- Diplomacy states and treaties with ratification.
- Tributary relations.
- Fortification kit.
- **Bridges** on the new roads, with span and load limits per technique
  (timber beam, rope, truss; the stone arch once discovered).
- God tool: migration wave.

Its demo (two settlements trade, their prices converge, one adopts the other's roof style, and a
treaty fails ratification) is shared: M5b's shows the first three parts and M5c's the treaty. It
*proves* multi-settlement simulation within budget (M5a) and diplomacy through each side's own
institutions (M5c), and *defers* war and sovereignty changes.

**M5a: Several settlements.**
- *Contents:*
  - 2–3 settlements founded at setup, placed together so that order cannot choose; settlement and
    polity identities; each settlement's hearth its own.
  - Residence histories, the migration accounts and the off-map account.
  - Knowing other places by contact; visits; marriage between settlements.
  - Moving between settlements: leaving becomes a choice of destination.
  - Splinter founding as a coalition's staged project.
  - God tool: migration wave.
  - Scale: 3,000 people in three settlements.
- *Demo:* three founding groups settle apart and live thirty years. People visit, marry and move
  between them, each move on record with its reason, and every settlement's accounts balance. A
  wave arrives and its households choose where to go. A splinter founding, if one happens, is
  inspectable from its first recruit to its fate; if none happens, that is reported.
- *Design (2026-10-09):* one ADR, [ADR-0018](decisions/0018-settlements-residence-movement.md)
  (several settlements: identities, residence and movement), from the four M5 briefs
  (`docs/briefs/m5-*.md`). Five slices:
  - **AK**: several settlements at setup. A new world takes 1–3 founding groups, each a band of
    its own (its own founding way of building, kin only within it), their sites drawn jointly
    over assignments whose fields' reaches do not overlap; whether they know one another's sites
    is a setup choice. The settlement record gains its parent, how it was founded and when it was
    abandoned; the hearth target names its settlement; each person's residence history, written
    by births, founding, leaving, exile, kin taking in children, sent families and the agitator;
    each settlement's yearly accounts (births, deaths, arrivals by origin, departures by
    destination) and the off-map account, derived from those histories. Every per-settlement path (the gathering, the market, asking, land, the watch,
    factions, word) checked with two settlements: nothing crosses without contact over ten
    years. The dashboard founds 2–3 groups a world, with a population-accounting row (a hard
    check) and settlement sizes reported, not graded (10-01 §4; 10-02 §5). Gate A follows a
    second settlement byte-identically.
    *Implemented (2026-10-09, §9):* a new world takes up to two neighbouring groups (the
    new-world dialog's "Neighbouring groups", `NewWorld.neighbours`), placed by one joint draw
    over a shared pool of sites so that their fields' reaches (about 3 km between hearths) do not
    overlap, a group that finds no room left out and the problem told; a world of one group draws
    as before. Settlements keep their parent, how they were founded and when they were
    abandoned; the hearth target names its settlement; everyone's residence history is kept and
    the accounts are derived from it, checked every year in the smoke and the dashboard. Two
    settlements founded together share nothing over two years without contact (tested), and save,
    load and cut byte-identically. The dashboard founds two groups a world (`--groups 1` for the
    dashboard of M3c to M4), with a graded population-accounting row and settlement sizes
    reported. Saves schema 50, wire 1.49. Not built here: whether the groups know one another's
    sites (it needs known places, slice AM).
  - **AL**: scale for several settlements. Measured first: three settlements of 1,000 against
    one of 1,000 and one of 3,000. Then, exactly where possible: per-household searches within
    the settlement; the route cache sized by population; landmark lower bounds for route
    searches (01-08 §2A), rebuilt at each monthly survey, with a one-time change of digests
    checked for equal route times; parallel preparation (01-03 §4.4) only if exact and only after
    the rest. *Budget (a design prior):* a year of 3,000 people in three settlements in at most
    ten minutes at Max on the development container; 8,000 measured, not targeted. If it is not
    met, that is recorded, as slice X's was.
    *Implemented (2026-10-09, §9):* what a household looks at for its own sake (ground for a
    field, company at the hearth, the places it has found food, its own fields) is kept to its
    own ground or its own settlement, each exactly: a save lived on by the builds before and
    after gives the same digests. Route searches are bounded by landmarks round the ground people
    walk, built on four threads after each monthly survey: the same route times, about a sixth
    of the cells searched, and a one-time change of digests where two routes tie. A year of
    3,000 people in three settlements takes 1,179 s, against 273 s for one of 1,000: the budget
    is not met, and is recorded. 8,000 were measured over 30 days, at 14.6 s a day. Not built: a
    route cache sized by population, reverse travel fields and parallel preparation (§9 says
    why).
  - **AM**: knowing other places, and visits. Each household's known places (founded together,
    seen within sight on a walk, kin there, visited, told), with impressions only from what a
    member saw or heard; whether the founding groups know one another's sites is the new-world
    choice ADR-0018 §6 names, moved here from AK because it needs known places. A visit is a
    leisure choice to keep company at a known settlement's hearth, worth what ties and kin there
    and loneliness make it, costing the walk, there and back within a day (a tuning value);
    visitors are company like anyone, and carry claims, ties, opinions, norms and ideologies by
    the existing paths. Partner search includes unpartnered adults of other settlements the
    person holds a tie with; where the couple's household lives follows the two households' land
    and room, never sex. Contacts per pair of settlements a year (visits, person-days, marriages)
    are counted.
    *Implemented (2026-10-09, §9), in three steps.* Step one, known places: each household keeps
    the other settlements it knows, how it came to know each (founded alongside, seen on a walk,
    kin, visited, told) and when, saved and shown in the inspector; a walk passing within
    `sight_m` of another settlement's hearth makes it known, and at the hearth someone from
    elsewhere says where they are from and a known place is passed on with `share_told` (content
    API 52); the new-world dialog's "The groups know where the others camped" is ADR-0018 §6's
    choice. Saves schema 51, wire 1.50. Step two, visits, is implemented: an adult may walk to
    the hearth of a settlement their household knows, keep company there and walk home the same
    day (`core:activity/visit`), reached before dark and within two hours' walk; it is worth
    the company it gives, as any hearth, and those there: close kin (`w_kin`), those they know
    (`w_ties`, saturating), and for one who looked for a partner at home and found nobody, the
    hope of meeting someone (`w_seek`), the wish to go again growing back over `revisit_days`
    after a member of the household was there, against the walk there and back. Visitors are
    company like anyone and carry ties, claims, opinions and ideologies by the existing paths;
    laws and what households pay of a levy are told only among those of one settlement. The
    visitor's household learns what they saw of the place's food, and each visit and its hours
    are counted per pair of settlements a year, saved, shown in the settlements list and
    reported on the dashboard. Saves schema 52, wire 1.51, content API 53. Step three, marriage
    between settlements: the partner search takes in unpartnered adults of other settlements the
    seeker holds a tie with; a couple of two settlements settles with or beside the household
    with more land worked per member, then the better housed, then by lot, never by sex; each
    marriage is counted, both households come to know the other place as where kin live, and
    the chronicle names where they settled. Over five years two bands of 30 that knew each other
    made 78, 0 and 370 visits and 1, 0 and 0 marriages across (seeds 1, 2, 3): contact is
    possible, not assured.
  - **AN**: moving between settlements. Leaving becomes a choice among the settlements a
    household knows and the map's edge, still reviewed daily when out of food; a yearly review on
    a keyed day and at events compares staying with joining a known settlement in the household's
    own units, the advantage holding over two reviews outside an emergency (10-01 §2.3 gives
    1–3; a tuning value). Arrivals camp and build as sent families do and get land through the
    property regime; an exile goes where they choose. The migration wave: 5–50 households of one
    band walk in from the nearest map edge over 1–7 days, sharing a way of building and kin,
    carrying stated months of provisions and knowing the settlements near where they are sent;
    one intervention record (ADR-0016 §5); ordinary people once there. The dashboard gains moves
    per 100 residents a year (amber outside 0.5–10, never red: 05-06 §5.4's output benchmark)
    and turnover beside net change; Gate B gains moves.
    *Step one implemented (2026-10-09, §9):* a household reviews where it lives once a year on
    a day of its own and when it forms, weighing each settlement it knows against staying in
    points (`[moving]`): kin and those known there against here, the food a member saw there
    against home, the grievances its members hold, against the harvest its fields here should
    bring, the work of a new home and fields, and the walk once; a place must win two reviews
    running. A household out of food that gives up goes to the known place whose kin, ties and
    fed look draw it most, and beyond the map otherwise, as before. A household that moves
    forms anew beside the other hearth with what it holds and builds as a sent family does; the
    ground, home and workshop it leaves stand as a household's that left, so the regime decides
    who works the ground next, and its offices lapse. Each move is counted per pair of
    settlements a year, in each person's residence history and in the chronicle. Saves schema
    53, content API 54. *Step two implemented (2026-10-09, §9), closing AN:* an exile goes
    where kin or those they know draw them, alone in a household beside that hearth, else beyond
    the map. The migration wave: the observer sends 5–50 households of one band to a point, those
    due coming at once and the rest at the midnights of up to a week after, from the map's edge
    nearest it (their walk is not lived; they appear where sent, knowing the settlements they
    passed within sight of and those within 5 km); each carries the months of food chosen (0–12)
    with seed and tools, all build alike, and in each run of three households one of each couple
    is a child of the same parents, so the households are kin; one intervention record holds
    everyone it brought. The dashboard grades moves between settlements per 100 residents a
    year (amber outside 0.5–10, never red; exiles left out), with turnover beside net change,
    and its first-month check now expects a band for each founding group (since slice AK it
    expected one, failing every two-group world). Gate B's moves are not built: its fixtures are
    worlds of one settlement. Saves schema 54, wire 1.52.
  - **AO**: splinter founding, and the M5a demo. A household whose plan to found passes its
    threshold becomes an organizer and recruits kin and households it regards, and a faction's
    organizer gains "leave together" beside petitioning. A coalition goes only if it holds food
    and seed to its first harvest plus 1–3 months (10-01 §2.3), tools, and adults enough to break
    its land; its site is one a member has seen or heard of, clear of others' land, with yields
    forecast at a low percentile (10-01 §2.3). It moves in stages and can fail; its settlement
    founds a polity under a copy of the founders' constitution. *Time box:* splinter founding is
    expected to be rare at village sizes (05-06 §1.3's arithmetic); after a week without one,
    nudge a prior and log a `NUDGE:`, never script one.
    *Step one implemented (2026-10-09, §9):* at its yearly review a household weighs founding a
    settlement with others beside staying and moving, in moving's points (`[founding]`): the
    share of their yearly need a site's cropland would bring at 0.7 of the believed yield,
    against the share its own fields and home's vacant cropland would; its grievances; its close
    kin among the households going; against moving's cost and founding's, and the walk. Sites
    are patches its people have walked, on dry, gentle ground two field walks from every
    settlement lived in. The household whose plan wins organizes a coalition of its kin's
    households and those its members regard, each going only if founding with them beats its own
    best plan; when the plan has won two reviews running it goes if it holds food to its first
    harvest and two months beyond, and seed, and else waits. The settlement is written with its
    parent, founds its polity under a copy of its founders' body, and each household moves there
    as a moving household does. Coalitions are saved, told in the chronicle and listed with their
    settlement. Saves schema 55, wire 1.53, content API 55. A faction's "leave together" and the
    M5a demo are step two.
    *Step two implemented (2026-10-09, §9), closing AO and M5a:* when the gathering turns a
    faction's petition down, its organizer's household weighs leaving that midnight, and a plan
    to found counts the members of a faction one of its people organizes as tied to it, beside
    kin and those regarded. A household is taken on only if the coalition with it would still
    hold food and seed enough to go, the closest tied asked first, and a household counted with
    one coalition neither organizes nor joins another (both found by the demo). The M5a demo is
    `civ-host neighbours` (three groups that know where the others camped live thirty years, a
    wave of ten households is sent among them at the end of year 10, every year's end checked and
    reported: each settlement's accounts, the contact between them, every move with its reason,
    the coalitions and where the wave's people live) with `web/e2e/m5a-demo.spec.ts`
    (`TCE_DEMO=1`), which shows years 10 and 30 in the observer. In seed 1, every year's accounts
    balanced; people moved between the settlements 252 times (4.3 per 100 residents a year: with
    their household, on marrying, taken in by kin, or to found); the wave's households founded a
    settlement of their own and within a year had all moved to a neighbour; and one coalition of
    13 households (51 people) founded a settlement in year 13 that lived to year 30, after two
    famines, with 18 people. No saves or wire change.

**M5b: Trade and diffusion.**
- *Contents:*
  - Goods carried between settlements: buyers fetching for themselves, households fetching to
    resell, then merchant firms; caravans as a view of trips that share a route and a day.
  - Price reports as households' dated beliefs; the price convergence row.
  - Diffusion of techniques and style through contact: buildings seen elsewhere, awareness by
    sight, provenance that crosses settlements, and a neighbours row.
- *Demo:* M5's first three parts. Two settlements trade and their prices close to a band, set
  against a twin of the same save with purchases between settlements switched off as a test
  harness; one settlement adopts the other's roof style by the diffusion brief's definition (§3.4:
  the mean pitch of its new buildings nearer the other's founding way, a followed link that
  crosses, and the contact that carried it on record). What does not happen is reported.
- *Design (2026-10-09):* one ADR, [ADR-0019](decisions/0019-exchange-between-settlements.md)
  (exchange between settlements: reach by reports, goods changing hands at the seller's door,
  trades tallied where they settle), from `docs/briefs/m5-trade.md` and `m5-diffusion.md`; the
  diffusion work extends ADR-0008 and ADR-0009 through their own revisit triggers and needs none.
  **Per-polity currencies wait for minting,** and core content has nothing to mint: not built in
  M5 unless metal content arrives (the trade brief, §1.8). Four slices:
  - **AP**: buying from a neighbour by report. Price reports as household beliefs: seen by a
    member who buys at a seller's door or keeps company at another settlement's hearth, passed at
    the hearth at routine news's chance, fading by age. A `fetch` activity with its own walk
    limit and daylight rule takes the purchase choice to the reported offers of known settlements
    within a day's walk; at the door the seller's terms as they stand decide, and a stale report's
    failure is recorded with its reason. Each trade records the buyer's settlement. The inspector
    shows a household's reports; the market panel shows what outsiders bought. A world of one
    settlement lives exactly as before.
    *Step one implemented (2026-10-09, §9):* each household holds price reports by market, good
    and payment good (`[reports]`: believed half as much every 30 days, let go at 120, told at
    0.15 an encounter; design priors). Someone keeping company at another settlement's hearth
    tells what their household and its workshops offer; companions pass on what their household
    holds of other markets, as old as it is. A member who buys at a seller's door sees all its
    offers, and that none is left of what it no longer offers, so that older word of it is not
    believed again. The `fetch` activity (two hours' walk each way at most, reached in daylight)
    takes the purchase choice to the reported offers, each believed by its report's weight, the
    walk there and back counted, one of a household at a time; at the door the seller's terms as
    they stand decide, through the ledger as at home. The trade records the buyer's settlement
    and is tallied in the seller's market; purchases and trips that bought nothing (sold out,
    terms, payment, or no longer needed) are counted per pair of settlements a year, in the
    contact words and in `civ-host neighbours`. Saves schema
    56, content API 56. A world without `fetch`, or of one settlement, lives exactly as before
    (digests). The inspector's reports, the market panel's outside buyers and wire 1.54 are step
    two.
    *Step two implemented (2026-10-09, §9), closing AP:* wire 1.54. The inspector lists what a
    person's household believes other settlements' sellers offer, a line a seller and good ("At
    Elmhollow, Bran's household: a sickle for 2.3 kg of grain or 3.7 kg of provisions, 8.5 sickles
    to be had; told by Ada 12 days ago", or that none is left); a trade in the market panel names
    the buyer's settlement when it came from elsewhere, and each market says what people of other
    settlements bought there last year and this; the settlements list's contact words count
    purchases and trips that bought nothing with why. `civ-host new --neighbour N --known` makes
    a world of several groups, for `web/e2e/reports.spec.ts`.
  - **AQ**: fetching to resell, the replacement anchor and convergence. A household weighs at its
    weekly review the margin of a trip to buy and sell at home, its units bounded by the depth it
    expects at home (08-12 §1.6); repeated fetching may run through a firm. An ask anchors on the
    lesser of own cost and replacement from reports of other markets. The monthly convergence
    record per pair of settlements, the dashboard's price convergence row, caravans as a view of
    trips that share a route and a day, and the demo's twin harness.
    *Step one implemented (2026-10-09, §9):* at its review of what it offers, a household that
    holds reports of other markets anchors each ask on the lesser of its own cost and what it
    believes replacing the good from there would cost: the reported price at its own cost of
    the payment, and the walk there and back (from the distance at its pace off the trails) and
    the trading over a load, each report believed by its weight. Only with the `fetch` activity
    and only from sellers within its walk. The convergence record: on each month's first day,
    per pair of settlements lived in, the median ask of each good offered in both and what it
    fetched in each; through the month, the trips to the other's sellers, their walking hours
    and the units carried home. The twin harness: `Sim::stop_trade_between` leaves a trip to buy
    elsewhere out of every choice (`Stopped`). Saves schema 57. Fetching to resell is step two;
    the convergence row, caravans and the wire step three.
    *Step two implemented (2026-10-09, §9):* fetching to resell. At the same review a household
    that holds reports of other markets plans an errand: of the goods it can offer and is not
    short of itself that its own market wants (buyers found none, or one sold lately), the units
    bounded by the units reported, what it can pay for out of what it can spare and carry, a load,
    and the depth at home (its market's sales over a review and the demand nobody met, less what
    is on offer there, its own included); a unit's cost the reported price at its own cost of the
    payment, with the walk and trading spread over the units and its margin, against what one
    fetches at home. The errand is kept until its next review or the trip; it is a `fetch`
    option after any purchase for its own need, worth what making to sell would be (the walk
    charged once, as on any trip). At the door it buys on the terms that cost it least while a
    unit still pays, up to the units planned, or the trip is counted with why; the goods are
    offered at home at its next review, on its usual terms. Saves schema 58. Running errands
    through a firm is not built.
    *Step three, first part, implemented (2026-10-09, §9):* the dashboard's price convergence row
    (per pair of settlements with 30 purchases between them, each traded good's median monthly gap
    of asks over the last ten years against the band carrying it leaves, or against the year
    before the pair first traded it; red only for a good flowing the wrong way beyond the band),
    and `civ-host twin --load SAVE --years N`, which lives a save as it is and as the twin and
    judges both. Caravans as a view, and the record in the observer, are the second part.
    *Step three, second part, implemented (2026-10-09, §9); slice AQ implemented but for errands
    run through a firm:* wire 1.55. The market panel lists, for each other settlement on record,
    the latest month's asks there and here of the goods offered in both and how far apart they
    stand, and what people of each carried home from the other; and who is on the road to buy
    there today, by settlement, those of one settlement on the same road that day together (a
    caravan, a view of trips and never an actor). The inspector says what errand the person's
    household means to run.
  - **AR**: diffusion through contact. Buildings seen elsewhere (up to 8 a person, finished in
    the past year, each met once) join the yearly taste review, a stranger's building admired for
    its craft and the viewer's own esteem of its owner; awareness by sight (`Seen`, with the
    settlement) from work watched on a trip and goods bought made with a technique; a technique's
    arrival names the settlement its knower came from, and a loss says where it is still known;
    followed links that cross are named in the readouts; style per settlement (founding way, taste,
    new buildings, standing stock: the three clocks); the dashboard's neighbours row.
    *Step one implemented (2026-10-09, §9):* buildings seen elsewhere join the taste review, with
    the patron half of a stranger's building from the esteem its viewers' household holds its
    owner's in; saves schema 59, content API 57 (`style.seen_most`, `style.sight_m`). The map's
    readout and the inspector name the settlement a followed or admired building stands in.
    *Step two implemented (2026-10-09, §9):* awareness by sight (`Seen`, with the settlement)
    from work watched on a visit or at a door within `knowledge.watch_m`, and from a good bought
    there that only one technique makes; a household, spouse or exile coming from another
    settlement brings what it knows, recorded as brought from there, and what only it knew is
    lost where it lived, the loss naming a settlement its kin or friends live in where it is
    still known; saves schema 60, content API 58. *Step three implemented (2026-10-09, §9):* each
    settlement's founding way is kept (saves schema 61) and its style is shown on the three
    clocks (founding way, households' taste, the year's new buildings, its standing buildings,
    and how many new ones follow a building elsewhere; wire 1.56); the building readout names
    where a followed chain first crosses, and the inspector what someone has seen elsewhere; the
    dashboard's neighbours row grades, per pair and direction, what crossed against the contact
    that could carry it. Slice AR is implemented.
  - **AS**: the M5b demo. One save lived from first contact twice, as it is and with the twin
    harness, three times each where runs differ: the traded goods' gaps against their bands with
    realised prices beside them, and the roof-style measure of the diffusion brief §3.4. *Time
    box:* if trade or the roof's adoption has not emerged after a week of tuning, nudge a prior
    (the fetch range, the report half-life, `alpha` within 0.02–0.20) and log a `NUDGE:`; never
    script a trip, a marriage or a copy. *Run (2026-10-10, §9):* two worlds whose founding ways
    stand more than 3° apart, each lived thirty years with trade and as the twin; a life is
    deterministic from its save, so each was lived once. The runs found four faults, fixed first
    (sights kept by the dead, a false red in the neighbours row, households that came to a hearth
    together never building, and the roof check counting households in their first year). In
    both worlds and both lives the smaller settlement took the larger one's way of roofing by the
    brief's definition (Willowholt in years 15 and 18; Alderwick in year 14), with nine tenths of
    Willowholt's new buildings nearer Willowford's way by year 25 (21 for the twin). Trade
    emerged, thin: 20 and 70 purchases between settlements in thirty years, 1 and 79 errand trips;
    no pair reached the row's 30 purchases, and prices did not close to a band, which is reported:
    the asks' gaps lie within what carrying one unit there and back on foot costs. No `NUDGE:`
    (both behaviours emerged). Slice AS is done, and with it M5b.

**M5c: Relations and works.**
- *Contents:*
  - Views of another polity, grievances across the boundary, and claims over wild ground.
  - Agreements from a library of clauses, each ratified as a law in each polity by its own custom;
    tribute as a ratified one-way transfer, short of conquest.
  - Works that households or a polity's law decide: crossings (bridges by system and span, their
    load computed per bridge) and enclosures (ditch, bank, palisade and gates).
- *Demo:* M5's fourth part: a treaty fails ratification, by who came and what they held, with both
  sides' law histories side by side.
- *Design (2026-10-10):* one ADR, [ADR-0020](decisions/0020-relations-between-polities.md)
  (relations between polities: no relation saved, its name a label nothing reads; word that
  crosses only with travellers; views of a polity per person; grievances across the boundary;
  claims over wild ground by law; an agreement as one shared record and a law in each polity,
  ratified by each custom; clauses performed by people with real goods), from
  `docs/briefs/m5-relations.md`; crossings from the settlements brief (§1.6–1.7) amend ADR-0004
  and ADR-0009, and enclosures ADR-0009 and ADR-0010, in their own slices. The relations brief's
  open questions are answered in §9. Five slices:
  - **AT**: knowing another polity. Word of another settlement's gatherings and decisions crosses
    only with people who go there and come back or come to live; per-person views of a polity
    heard of, in three domains (keeps its word, harms us, helps us), fading over five years
    toward no view; `Blamed` may name another polity's party; a *claim a place* policy by which a
    gathering claims a wood or deposit its people know, and outsiders seen using it without leave
    give those who see or hear of it a grievance against them. Relation labels from a pure
    classifier, never read. A relations panel shows each side's label and reasons apart, and
    what each believes of the other. The funnel is measured from here on (issues held, places
    claimed, outsiders' use seen), since neighbours may have nothing to negotiate.
    *Complete (2026-10-10, three steps, §9):* the places each household's people work and the
    outsiders seen there, the issue `outsiders` and the `claim_place` policy, decided by each
    custom (saves 62, content API 59); use of a claimed place without leave as a grievance against
    the outsiders' household, and per-person views of other polities in the three domains, written
    by trespass seen or heard of (saves 63, content API 60); relation labels from a pure
    classifier, each side's apart, in the government panel (wire 1.57). Word of another polity's
    laws moves to AU, where agreements first need it; agreements, and so friendly relations, are
    AU's.
  - **AU**: agreements. *Step one built (2026-10-10, §9):* a claim binds outsiders' choices:
    word of it crosses only with travellers, and a household that heard of another polity's claim
    on a place weighs it at half its worth when choosing where to gather or dig (saves 64, content
    API 61), so leave to use has something to grant. *Step two built (2026-10-10, §9):* the
    agreement itself: the issue *claimed from us*; an elder holding it, who may propose, seeks
    terms with the one of the other settlement they know best who may propose there; the two
    weigh up to eight packages of leave (one side's claims, the other's, or both, for a year or
    five) by their own household's forecast and the support each predicts at home, and agree on
    the one both expect to pass or part with none; each sponsors it at home as a law of the
    appended `agreement` policy kind, decided by its own custom; in force once both have passed it
    and each side has heard from a traveller; failure (no terms, turned down, short of a quorum,
    never put to a gathering, unanswered) and endings (term run out, a law of either side) kept in
    one shared record, and leave in force lifts the discount and the trespass for those who know
    their own law (saves 65, content API 62). Deviations from ADR-0020 §6, recorded in §9: leave
    is the only clause (gifts and transfers need AV's carriers), clauses are a code list rather
    than a content kind, and the meeting takes no time. *Step three built (2026-10-10, §9); slice
    AU complete:* views of the other polity weigh in stances on agreements and are kept with them
    (ADR-0020 §3, saves 66); the *under agreement* label; both law histories side by side in the
    government panel's relations (wire 1.58). Not built: failure recorded on each side once heard
    (a failed agreement's laws lapse at once, a record nothing reads).
  - **AV**: performance. Clauses in force create obligations with a polity as debtor or
    beneficiary on an appended ledger channel; a transfer is paid only from a common store, its
    four accounts kept (paid in, set aside, owed, arrived) and its goods walked by carriers; a miss
    keeps its cause, learned only as a claim, and is answered by existing moves; expiry,
    withdrawal, repeal and failed ratification are separate endings. The tributary label and both
    burden ratios (13-02 §2.1) for each paying village. *Step one built (2026-10-10, §9):* goods
    for leave, and payments carried. A polity whose common store is in force may give, for leave
    to use the places the other claims, a gift once or a transfer every `transfer_days` of the good
    its store holds most, into the other's store when it keeps one (clause kinds `gift` and
    `transfer`, appended); each household weighs its share of what its store gives or gets. A
    payment falls due when the agreement comes into force (a transfer again every
    `transfer_days`); each midnight the paying store sets aside what it holds of it, up to the
    whole, into the payment, a ledger holder of its own on the appended `agreement` channel that
    spoils in the open; its keeper, or else the one who agreed to the terms, may carry it to the
    other's hearth as a duty (the appended `carry` behaviour), where it goes into the other's
    store; one not handed over within `deliver_days` is missed, its cause kept (the store held
    none, nobody to carry it, nobody carried it in time), and what it still holds goes back to
    the paying store (saves 67, content API 63). Deviations from ADR-0020 §7, recorded in §9: a
    payment is its own record, not an ADR-0015 obligation; nothing holds one back by a decision
    yet; the carrier's load is not on their back. *Step two built (2026-10-10, §9); slice AV
    complete:* views written by performance (a payment handed over or missed is, to those of the
    receiving polity who know its own law deciding the agreement, evidence that the payer keeps
    its word or does not, and a gift handed over that it helps; content API 64); the *tributary*
    label for a polity paying a transfer in force, with both burden ratios, T/Y and T/(Y − C),
    or the subsistence shortfall, among its reasons, and the receiver saying what it receives;
    each agreement's payments in the government panel (wire 1.59). Not built, recorded in §9: the
    cause of a miss told by travellers, and moves that answer a miss (remit, suspend, repeal for
    it).
  - **AW**: crossings. Bridge systems as content (a span envelope, members, the techniques each
    needs, work per unit; a timber beam first), a crossing record (reach, spans and supports in
    order, approaches) built in ADR-0009's stages by a household (a log to its fields) or a
    polity's public-work law with labour as obligations; its worth to each household the walking
    its own recorded trips would save and the places reachable only across it; load computed per
    bridge as people step on, decay by exposure, repair never guaranteed, and a failure that kills
    whoever is on it; the deck's cells walkable for routing, a fallen bridge closing every route
    across it. *Steps (2026-10-10):* one, the crossing and its physics (bridge systems as content,
    the crossing record, the deck walked once open, the members checked daily and as people step
    on, rot, failure that drops whoever is on it and closes every route across), crossings placed
    only by tests; two, households build log footbridges where their own walks would gain, and
    keep them or not; three, the polity's public work for a crossing beyond one log (a trestle
    over the trunk river) with labour contributed, and the observer's view. *Step one built
    (2026-10-10, §9):* the content kind `bridge` and its first system, the log footbridge (content
    API 65); the crossing record (saves 68); the walking grid laid with open crossings' decks and
    laid again whenever one opens or gives way, routes and travel fields kept against one routing
    revision (ADR-0004 §7); the logs checked as a beam each midnight and as someone steps on (ADR-0009
    §9), rot daily, and a crossing that gives way drops whoever is on it (a new cause of death, a
    fall) and stops every walk across it. *Step two built (2026-10-10, §9):* each walk counts, for
    the walker's household, the river cells it wades, fading as the places it works do (saves
    69); at its yearly review a household weighs a crossing at each, the walking its wades would
    save over the crossing's life at the quality its people would lay it against the work it
    takes, and begins the best that repays it, of a system someone in it knows; its people work on
    it as a chosen activity, its logs' quality drawn from their skill when its labour is done
    (content API 66: a bridge system's `skill`, the behaviour `bridge`); one begun by a household
    that is no more is taken over. In the measured worlds no household's wades repay a log (§9).
    Not built: giving up a crossing begun, mending one before it fails, and the places a crossing
    would bring within reach. *Step three, the observer's view, built (2026-10-10, §9):* the
    crossings listed with their state, owner, members and condition in words, drawn on the map and
    named by the pointer readout (wire 1.60). *Step three, crossings built together, built
    (2026-10-10, §9):* the policy kind `build_crossing` and the issue `fords` (content API 67): a
    site the village's wades together would repay; its sponsor names one their household wades,
    each household forecasts it at the food its hours could bring (research 11-07 §2.3), and once
    passed the polity begins it, its own, one at a time, each household asked an equal share of
    its work, given by choice, the hours given kept (saves 70). *Crews, built (2026-10-10, §9):* a
    bridge system's `crew` (content API 68; 5 for the log footbridge, research 11-07 §2.3), at
    most that many choosing to work on one crossing a day, counted as they choose (saves 71;
    research 11-11 §1.3). Not built: the trestle over the trunk river and the places reachable
    only across it, giving up a stalled crossing, and any consequence of a share not given.
  - **AX**: enclosures and the M5 demo's fourth part. An enclosure as a line of sectors (a ditch
    as an ADR-0010 earthwork, its spoil a bank inside; a palisade of ADR-0009 parts; gates), raised
    by a polity's law naming the line, how work is raised and whether the watch keeps a gate,
    households outside the line paying without its cover; people cross only at a gate or by
    climbing. The demo: a treaty fails ratification, by who came and what they held, with both
    sides' law histories side by side. Time box: after a week with no failed ratification, nudge a
    prior (a negotiator's caution about thin gatherings), never an event, and log a `NUDGE:`.
    *The demo, lived (2026-10-10, §9):* the M5a and M5b demos lived thirty years again on the new
    walking. No agreement reached a gathering in any lived world: villages counter-claim the
    ground they share within weeks, and nothing in M5 makes contested ground costly, so there is
    nothing to negotiate. Opening that (contested places kept off until leave) was tried and not
    adopted; a household going hungry now weighs no claim. A gathering turning down terms its
    negotiator expected to pass, by what those who came believe, with both law histories side by
    side, is shown in a test world; the nudge offered acts after terms are agreed, so nothing was
    nudged. *Enclosures moved to M6 (2026-10-10, §9):* in M5 nobody takes from another village, so
    a line round one keeps out no taker and every forecast of it is a loss; they come with force.
    Slice AX is done, and with it M5c and M5.

**M6: Towns & their troubles**, split (2026-10-10, §9) into three milestones, each meeting the
usable bar with its own demo. M6's original contents were:
- Operational war; conquest/annexation, vassalage, secession, merger/federation.
- Fortification kit, moved from M5 (2026-10-10, §9): enclosures as lines of sectors (ditch,
  bank, palisade, gates) raised by a polity's law, on the walking grid, and takings between
  villages that give them a purpose before war does.
- **Water, sewage & disease.**
- **Fire.**
- Weather (visuals and sim).
- Fake interiors.
- Incident panels, coverage maps.
- Stone/brick/tile kit.
- Levees and embankments as public-works earthworks, which people can propose after floods.
- Earthworks for farming, moved from M3c: drainage and irrigation ditches, and farm terraces
  (ADR-0012 §7).
- Floods, storms and fire damage feed structural demand; a collapse can prompt building-code
  proposals.
- God tools: fire, plague, flood, drought, storm.

Its demo (a cholera outbreak traced to a well; a great fire leads to a proposed masonry code; a
siege ends in annexation) is shared: M6a's shows the outbreak, M6b's the fire and M6c's the siege.
It *proves* the incident-plus-aggregate service UX (M6a) and war with real consequences (M6c),
and *defers* courts, elections and the full transport system. Weather visuals and fake interiors
are drawn by Unreal, so they go to the Unreal track; the weather's simulation is the kernel's,
and M6a gives rivers a daily flow.

**M6a: Water and sickness.**
- *Contents:*
  - Water as a chain from a source people choose: rivers by the day, a shallow aquifer, springs,
    and wells households dig and keep.
  - Waste and contamination as conserved loads moved by runoff, groundwater, rivers and stored
    water.
  - Diseases as content (cholera and bacillary dysentery first), infections with their own
    clocks, the ill kept abed, tending, deaths by disease.
  - What people see and suspect, and what they do: avoid a source, move away, ask their polity
    to close a source or dig a well.
  - Incidents of every kind in one panel, episodes and typed links (ADR-0015 §8); the Water
    lens; the epidemics row.
  - God tools: plague (a person), drought.
- *Demo:* M6's first part. The observer gives cholera to one person; whether and where it
  spreads is the water's and people's. The outbreak's page lists each source with the cases
  among those who drew from it and among those who did not, what fouled it and since when (the
  kernel's), and who suspects it and on whose word, who stopped drawing from it and any law
  about it (people's). If nobody in the world links the illness to the well, that is reported:
  the trace is then the observer's alone.
- *Design (2026-10-10):* one ADR, [ADR-0021](decisions/0021-water-and-sickness.md) (water and
  sickness: sources people choose, a saved aquifer with derived springs, wells as pits with
  parts, conserved loads with four routes, diseases as content and infections as truth,
  suspicion tallied per person, responses through existing pipelines, introductions the
  observer's), and an amendment to ADR-0015 (§8: incidents of every kind, episodes, typed
  links), from the M6 health and observer briefs (`docs/briefs/m6-health.md`,
  `m6-observer.md`). The briefs' open questions are answered in §9. Four slices:
  - **AY**: rivers by the day, groundwater and wells. Measured first: the 3×1,000 bench again,
    alone on the container. One runoff store per world, fed by the weather's rain and melt and
    drained by a recession, scales each reach's mean discharge to the day's flow (fords stay on
    the mean until M6b); a shallow aquifer, one head per coarse cell, recharged by what the
    reference soil cannot hold, exchanging with its neighbours and the rivers, spun up when a
    world is made or an older save loads; springs derived where heads stand above the ground.
    Sources become places a household chooses among by the walk and the wait, its use flexing
    with the trip's cost within 10–30 L a person a day; draws are logged as uses. A household
    weighs a well once a year as it weighs a log footbridge, digs a pit lined with parts, meets
    water or not, deepens or gives up, and keeps it. The map shows sources, springs, wells and
    their water; the inspector a household's sources; the dashboard a water row.
    *Step one implemented (2026-10-10, §9):* the bench measured alone (1,286.5 s a year, against
    slice AL's 1,179 s); the reference soil's daily surplus and its long-run mean; a shallow
    aquifer of one head per habitat patch (128 m) in three units of ground by habitat (alluvial
    sand, silty slope deposits, weathered rock), each drawing its conductivity and specific yield
    once a world within research 03-02 §2.2's priors, settled to where the mean recharge holds it
    when a world is made or an older save loads; seepage where heads reach the ground, springs
    derived from the heads; one runoff store whose outflow against the mean scales the rivers
    (saves 72, content API 69). *Step two (2026-10-10, §9):* springs are sources: a household's
    people fetch from the nearest spring that flows and has a load left today when it is nearer
    than the river or lake, and a spring gives what flows to it in a day; every draw is logged
    as a use of its source, which no polity may claim; what a household uses a day follows the
    walk to the water it last fetched (saves 73, content API 70). *Step three (2026-10-10,
    §9):* wells, a content kind (the timber-lined well, which founders know); a household weighs
    one beside its home once a year by the walking and lift it would save over its lining's life,
    at the depth it expects from the settlement's wells or its water; its people dig and line it
    a metre at a time; it meets water where the water table stands above its floor, or is dug
    deeper, or given up; its own, its kin and the short of water draw from its column, which
    refills from the ground and is taken from the water table at midnight; its lining rots until
    it is relined or falls in (saves 74, content API 71). *Step four (2026-10-10, §9):* the
    observer's view (wire 1.61): the map draws each well by what has become of it, the springs
    flowing today and the places at the water's edge drawn at today, and its readout names each
    and the rivers' flow today against their mean; the inspector says where a person's household
    went for water today, what each of them uses and what it holds; the dashboard reports a
    water row, grey. Slice AY is complete.
  - **AZ**: sickness. The `disease` content kind, cholera and bacillary dysentery; loads in
    middens, the ground, wells, reaches and stored water, moved by the four routes, each move
    recorded; exposure summed over routes and drawn once a day; episodes with stages, immunity
    and an acquisition record; the ill kept from work; tending as chosen work; deaths as
    `Cause::Disease`, excess over the Siler baseline. The plague tool (a menu of authored
    diseases, a person as its target) and an `Influence` origin on everything it starts.
    Illness incidents and outbreak episodes in the kernel, saved, with `caused_by` and
    `part_of`; the chronicle tells an outbreak when it begins and ends, and every death.
  - **BA**: what people see and do. Sickness seen (abed at home, a death among kin) and told as
    claims; who draws where (one's own draws, households seen at a source, the usual source of
    those one has ties with); suspicion tallied per person and told with its counts; a
    household avoids a source it suspects when it knows another, covers its well, and
    reconsiders where to live after a death or a suspicion. The issues `sickness`,
    `source_suspected` and `water_far`; the policies *close a source* (kept by each person's
    choice) and *dig a well* (built together, as a crossing is), forecast in today's units with
    a death as `w_death` days. The drought tool.
  - **BB**: the Incidents panel and the M6a demo. The Takings panel becomes one Incidents panel
    for every kind (three labelled layers, stamped steps, typed links), with incident and
    episode pages; links from the chronicle; the Water lens's *could reach* and *did come*; the
    dashboard's epidemics row, grey unless an introduction happened. Then the demo, time-boxed,
    with any `NUDGE:` logged.

**M6b: Fire, masonry and floods.**
- *Contents:* fire (ignition from hearths, ovens, crafts and lightning; spread between buildings
  by materials, spacing, wind and dryness; fighting it; what it does to buildings, goods and
  people); the stone, brick and tile kit; floods and storms on what people built, from the
  weather and rivers by the day, with fords that change with the flow; levees and embankments
  as public works; farm ditches and terraces; damage feeding building-code proposals; help at a
  fire as a coverage lens; god tools: fire, flood, storm.
- *Demo:* M6's second part: a great fire leads to a proposed masonry code, its stances' reasons
  on record, and any house built in stone under it keeps the law in its provenance.
- *Design:* when M6a is done, from `docs/briefs/m6-fire.md` and the observer brief, with one
  ADR expected.

**M6c: War and conquest.**
- *Contents:* takings and raids between villages; operational war (who fights and why, a band
  raised, supplied and moved, fighting and its losses); the fortification kit carried from M5;
  sieges; conquest and annexation, vassalage, secession, merger and federation.
- *Demo:* M6's third part: a siege ends in annexation.
- *Design:* when M6b is done, from `docs/briefs/m6-war.md` and the observer brief, with one ADR
  expected.

**M7: Politics deepens.**
- *Contents:*
  - Elections with franchise rules; parties formed from factions; campaigns;
    coalitions.
  - Full corruption and patronage.
  - Lobbying and capture by firms and guilds.
  - **Courts and precedent**, and judicial review as a check.
  - Credit and banking; coin debasement and inflation.
- *Demo:* a republic's assembly captured by a merchant guild passes
  protectionist tariffs; a court strikes down a curfew.
- *Proves:* the deep political layer on top of the M4 primitives.
- *Defers:* planning institutions, transport.

**M8: The growing city (v1).**
- *Contents:*
  - **Planning as politics**: planning institution, plan templates, zoning,
    land value.
  - **Transport & traffic**: road hierarchy, congestion, commuting, boats.
  - Hierarchical pathfinding.
  - Grammar v3: 3–4 storeys, monuments and landmark templates, style drift
    fully live.
  - Crowd LOD at scale.
  - Road cut-and-fill and grading for the road hierarchy (earthworks).
  - Earthquakes act through structural margins; taller buildings and longer
    spans need the matching techniques.
  - Earthquake god tool (damages buildings; terrain unchanged).
  - **Scale gate: 10–50k agents at 60 fps, with Accelerated Max at ≥1 year per
    minute.**
- *Demo:* a 30k-person planned capital next to an organic port town, with
  traffic, in the same world.
- *Proves:* v1 is complete.
- *Defers:* everything in the §1 NOT list.

**Post-v1 (ordered, not sized):**
- **M9, Aggregate LOD & scale:** cohort model, 10–30 settlements, 100k+
  agents; **decide time skips**; climate change god tool.
- **M10, Industrial content:** steam, factories, rail, gas light; capability
  rungs for industrial services; healthcare and education as full services;
  fiat money.
- **M11, Modern content:** electricity and a power grid, motor vehicles,
  transit, telecom, modern architecture primitives, waste.
- **M12, Optional LLM `Deliberator`, multi-viewer, close-up fidelity**
  (MetaHuman-grade notables).

## 8. Risks and open questions

### Technical risks

1. **Runtime terrain in a packaged UE build.**
   - *Risk:* UE Landscape editing APIs are editor-centric, and the alternatives
     are experimental. This could force either an editor-time world-bake step
     (bad UX) or a custom terrain renderer.
   - *Mitigation:* spike S1 opens M2, so it's settled before anything else in
     UE depends on it.
2. **In-process Rust DLL.**
   - *Risk:* a UE crash takes the sim with it; a panic across FFI is undefined
     behavior; the DLL can't be hot-reloaded under Live Coding.
   - *Mitigation:* `catch_unwind` at every exported function; crash snapshots;
     autosave; the headless CLI as the primary sim dev loop; the
     transport-agnostic schema as the escape hatch to go out of process.
3. **Routing and traffic at 50k agents with visible daily life.**
   - *Mitigation:* event-scheduled trips, hierarchical pathfinding with route
     caching per origin zone, time-bucketed congestion, and aggregate flow in
     Accelerated mode.
4. **Crowd rendering of 5–10k visible agents on 12 GB VRAM**, alongside Lumen
   and Nanite.
   - *Mitigation:* vertex-animation instancing at mid distance, impostors far
     away, and a hard visible-agent budget.
5. **Procedural buildings looking mass-produced.** This is the realism
   bottleneck, and it's art, not code.
   - *Mitigation:* budget real time for kit quality, weathering and
     per-instance variation; keep module sets small but excellent.
6. **Divergence between Detailed and Accelerated modes.**
   - *Mitigation:* shared decision functions and the mode-consistency test.
7. **Web panels in UE (CEF):** performance, input focus and packaging quirks.
   - *Mitigation:* prove it in M2, with a fallback to UMG for the highest-traffic
     panels.
8. **Parallel update bugs.** Without determinism, race-driven bugs won't
   reproduce exactly.
   - *Mitigation:* double-buffered state for what agents read, snapshots
     before failures, tracing, and statistical regression tests.

### Scope risks (most likely to balloon)

1. **The technology content multiplier.** Every node implies goods, recipes,
   programs, kit modules and service rungs. This is the biggest threat to "full
   tech progression". *Mitigation:* the "node only with its footprint" rule, and
   v1 stops at pre-industrial.
2. **Composable constitutions and the law pipeline.** The combinatorics are
   large, and debugging them is hard. *Mitigation:* label inference and "why"
   views are built alongside the system, not after it.
3. **Economic stability.** Agent-based economies routinely collapse or blow up.
   *Mitigation:* the sanity thresholds, plus rule 3's time-box and nudges.
4. **Research drift (the TGE failure mode).**
   - *Early warning signs:* milestones ending in reports; ADR counts climbing;
     smoke seeds turning into campaigns.
   - *Mitigation:* rules 2, 3, 4 and 7 in §1.
5. **War.** It touches economy, demography, politics and rendering at once.
   *Mitigation:* operational only, with statistical battles.
6. **UX polish on data-dense panels** takes longer than expected for every
   system. Each milestone's estimate already includes its panels.
7. **Collapse tuning.** Too frequent and it's irritating; too rare and nobody
   notices. *Mitigation:* a band for structural failures per 1,000 buildings
   per year on the sanity dashboard (§4.7), tuned by eye. Failures should be
