  - Route searches and travel fields keep Tobler's speed of each step in a per-thread table for the grid; the ground under a grid never changes, so a step is worked out once.
  - A search's scores sit side by side in one array.
  - The routing view is also kept a byte per cell, so a trail factor is one look-up.
  - Finding deposits along a walk first rules out bodies beyond the walk's box.
  - A deal's own costs are worked out only once an offer is worth pricing.
  - The route cache keeps two generations rather than emptying itself when full.

  **Measured over thirty spring days:** 1,000 people went from 1,674 to 1,354 ms a day, 2,000 from 5,256 to 4,278. **A whole year** of 1,000 people founded at once lived in 485 s at Max (1.33 s a day): 0.58 s in the first month, 1.5 s in spring, 2.2–2.6 s in the harvest weeks and 0.8–1.0 s in winter. The goal was two minutes, so it is **not met**. **Why:** on the slowest days route searches are 86 % of the time (callgrind). There are few of them (250–800 misses a day, half of them the way back of a cached route, with no route unreachable), but each grows costly once trails are worn: the search's lower bound assumes a trail's speed everywhere while most ground is walked off-trail at 0.6 of it, so a search expands 12,000–16,000 cells. A tighter bound (landmarks, or a bound by region) would cut that several-fold, but it can change which of two equally fast routes is walked, so it is not exact. It waits until a demo needs towns of thousands (M5, M6), with a one-time change of digests checked for equal route times. **Decisions** cost the rest of the time, spread thin: the deal search 12 %, patches 4 %, jobs 3 %, and `own_costs` 56,000 times a day at 2,000 people. Time grows faster than the population: 2,000 people cost 3.2 times 1,000. M4's own demos found villages of tens to hundreds, which this does not limit.
- **Ties, as built (2026-10-07, M4a slice Y's first step, ADR-0014 §1-2):** each person keeps up to 48 directed ties (content API 30, `[ties]`). A tie holds familiarity, warmth, good and bad evidence in four domains (provision, craft, word, counsel; counts that fade toward a prior of 1, research 04-06 §5.2), fear (0 until M4b), a balance of help in hours, and the act that last moved it, when and how many times running. Every quantity fades at its own half-life, worked out when the tie is next touched. Ties are written only at the acts the engine records:
  - a gift in answer to an ask: the asker writes provision and help received, in hours of the receivers' own work; the giver's elder writes the asker familiarity and help given;
  - wages paid: the worker writes word for the owner's elder, who writes craft for the worker;
  - rent: the tenant who threshed writes word for the holder's elder, and back;
  - a trade: buyer and seller's elder each write a little word;
  - learning beside someone: craft, by the hour;
  - company at the hearth: familiarity and warmth, by the hour.

  The household's elder stands for it: its eldest of an age to keep a household. At the hearth, each session touches three of those present from other households, a block of sessions three for each. 15 % of that company comes from people not yet known, the rest from those known, the better known the likelier. A first version weighted every face present, known ones up to 5 times, and at 1,000 people strangers filled every draw: 227,070 ties were let go in 30 days. With the share it was 16,888 (04-04 §1.5: people keep up the ties that matter). With no room, the least salient tie (familiarity plus warmth plus a tenth of its evidence) without more than 4 hours of help owed either way is let go. Ties to the dead and gone are let go on the first of each month. Nothing reads a tie yet, so **every other section of a save is byte for byte as before** (30 days of the 1,000-person save, `bench_village --digest`; only the content's fingerprint and the new section differ). Saves schema 28 (the `ties` section; an older save starts with none). **Measured:** a band of 40 after a year holds 32 ties a person, 36 of 42 people holding some, none let go; the reasons are the hearth 1,067 and trades 94. 1,000 people after 30 days hold 46.8 each, nearly all full, and let go about one tie in two a person a day. 30 days at 1,000 people take 1,385 ms a day, against 1,354 without ties.
- **Standing and notables, as built (2026-10-08, M4a slice Y's second and third steps, ADR-0014 §3-5):** on the first of each month, after ties to the gone are let go, each settlement's standing is summed from its adults' ties (the people profile's `[standing]`, content API 30). **Standing:** each adult's esteem by domain is the sum of the net good evidence the settlement's other adults hold for them. **Influence:** how many adults count them among the eight people they regard most, regard being esteem in every domain with warmth to tell apart those esteemed alike. ADR-0014 §3 had "among the few they esteem most in some domain"; one ranking over all domains with warmth was simpler and keeps company from counting as esteem. **Notables:** the most influential, 1 % of adults and at least three, ties broken by esteem and then id. A notable keeps the place while ranked within 1.5 times their number. The table is saved beside the ties (schema 28, an optional field; a save without it works it out afresh). Nothing reads the notable flag; deliberation, and the gate that checks the tier against every adult deliberating (ADR-0014 §4), come with slice Z. **The first choice that reads ties:** a household short of food asks, among those who could give it as much, the nearest once a walk is weighed against how well its members regard the one who stands for each household. They would walk `ask_known_min`, 30 minutes (a tuning value), further to ask someone they regard fully than a stranger. **The observer (wire 1.26):** the inspector lists someone's twelve strongest ties with the kernel's reasons ("kept them company at the hearth 35 times, last in April of year 1") and their standing; `GetStanding` and a Standing panel show each settlement's notables and those most counted, with the ties kept and let go across the world. **Measured:**
  - **Two bands of 40, three years at Max:** 31–34 ties a person, none let go. The reasons were hearth 877 and 1,042, trades 180 and 198, wages and work seen 7 and 26 each, rent and land lent 13 and 0, and gifts 3 and 0. Every adult was esteemed by someone, and the most influential was counted by 15 of 25 adults and 19 of 26.
  - **Ten-year smoke:** passed; 9 of 10 bands kept 43–63 people, and ria coast 5 died out in year 4, as before.
  - **Gate B:** passed with the same figures as before slice Y. Its fixture years see no shortage, so nobody asks for food. It does not yet compare ties.
  - **Tests:** the end-to-end suite passes (15 tests, the demos skipped), with a new standing test.
- **The polity and its first law, as built (2026-10-08, M4a slice Z's first step, ADR-0013 §1-5):** each settlement has a polity of its own id, founded at the first midnight under the custom every world shares: its adults decide by acclamation at the hearth, more for than against, a quarter of them needed, a tie failing (the people profile's `[polity]`, content API 31). The body is saved with the polity as values. **Issues:** food short, present when the settlement's food ran short in the last year or a household's food will not last until its next harvest. ADR-0013 §5 names "a food shortfall against the outlook"; a settlement-wide shortage alone never arose in five-year runs of three seeds. **Deliberation:** once a week, when nothing is before the gathering and an issue is present, the notables and the elders of the households whose food will not last each weigh proposing each level of each template that answers the issue (the new `policy` kind; the core pack's common store at a twentieth, a tenth or a fifth), against proposing nothing. A move is worth the person's forecast for their household, plus half the regard-weighted forecast for those who regard them, times the share of those they know who would back it, less a cost; a softmax picks. **The forecast:** the change in the expected log of a household's year of food above subsistence (half a year's food), over an ordinary year and a lean one at half the harvest. A lean year is believed one in four before any is seen; lean years seen are those the settlement's food ran short in. It pays the levy on its harvest and draws relief up to a year's need from a store holding the levy on an average harvest. A household near the edge gains and one with plenty loses, so a store splits a village by its fortunes (research 09-17 §1.2). **The gathering:** the next evening, for two hours from an hour before sunset. Attending is a new activity (`behavior = "attend"`, adults only), worth a point plus half of what the household and regard for the sponsor have at stake, beside the company. Every adult hears of it: awareness by word is left for M4c. At the next midnight each member who came takes a stance from their household's forecast and their regard for the sponsor, against a margin; the body decides; if it passed, those who came know the law. **Word of a law:** households tell their members at midnight, and companions at the hearth tell each other. **The levy:** at threshing, a thresher who knows the law pays its share of the grain their household keeps into the store, as their choice. They cannot when paying would leave the household below subsistence. Otherwise the chance of paying is logistic in the custom's pull (1.5 points), their stance (±1) and regard for the sponsor, less what paying costs their household; there is no sanction until M4b. **Relief:** the store, kept unroofed at the hearth, is one more place a household short of food may ask, by a law one of its members knows: at most five days of food an ask. The polity is a ledger holder (channels `levy` and `relief`), and every good stays accounted for. **History:** every law keeps its sponsor, issue and level, the stances of those present with what moved them, the decision and the counts, who knows it and since when, and what was paid, could not be, kept back or not known of, and given. Nothing is pruned (saves 29). The chronicle says each proposal and decision in words ("The gathering at Stonewick turned down a common store, taking a fifth of each harvest: 1 for, 18 against; 28 of 28 adults came."). **Measured:**
  - **Three seeds, four years:**
    - Seed 1: a twentieth passed in year 3, 20 for and 3 against, with 23 of 24 adults present.
    - Seed 2: a twentieth passed in year 1, 14–0.
    - Seed 3: a fifth was turned down 1–18, and a week later another sponsor's twentieth passed 27–0.
    - Afterwards: 300–1,100 kg levied a year, with 5–43 % of what was owed kept back or unpayable (the most in seed 2, whose poorer households could not pay). No relief was asked for, since no household ran short.
  - **Ten-year smoke:** all 10 worlds passed, and with them the new checks: one polity a settlement, and every law decided by its body's rule.
    - Every village ended with a common store at a twentieth, after 1–15 proposals (the smoke records how many, not at which levels). The levels were the same everywhere, but the timing, the number of failed proposals and the payments differed.
    - Each store took 1.8–6.8 t over the years, and 0.4–2.5 t was kept back or unpayable.
    - Four stores gave 143–286 kg in relief. The rest gave none, because nobody there ran short.
    - The unroofed stores lost a third to two-fifths of what came in to spoiling (32–40 %), and held 1.1–3.8 t at the end.
    - Ria coast 5, which died out in year 4 before this step, ended with 15 people, after its store gave 143 kg. The other nine kept 44–59 people.
    - **An observation, no nudge made:** every village converging on the smallest levy is a finding to watch, not a target. With one template, and a forecast that prices risk only through the lean-year belief, the twentieth is the level most households can back. Offices (Z4) and leaving as a choice (Z3) change what a store is for.
  - **Gate B:** passed (20 runs, 1.1 minutes, every run passing its checks). The largest differences were walking −4.9 % in the valley and work +6.3 % on the coast. The coast's Detailed spread of work grew to ±0.27 h a person-day from the ±0.15 its tolerances were calibrated at (its "calibrated" column reads 15.7 % for work against a 10 % tolerance); the cause was not traced. Gate B does not yet compare the polity.
- **The government panel, as built (2026-10-08, M4a slice Z's second step, ADR-0013 §7):** `GetGovernment` answers with each settlement's polity (wire 1.27): its custom in the kernel's words ("The adults who come to the hearth decide by acclamation: more for than against carries it, and a tie fails; a quarter of them must come."), its adults, what its store holds, the gathering called if any, and every law proposed there, newest first. Each law comes with its whole history: what it is, its sponsor and the issue it answered, the day of its gathering, the decision in words ("agreed: 14 for, 1 against; 25 of 25 adults came, 7 needed"), every stance of those who came with the points that moved it and why in words ("for: it makes little difference to their household, but they think well of the sponsor"; the sponsor's reads "they proposed it"), how many living people know it, and what its levy and the store's relief came to. The observer fetches it once a simulated day and opens a law to its history on a click. **Measured:** the end-to-end suite passes (16 tests, the demos skipped), with a new government test on river valley seed 2 lived 300 days. In its runs a fifth was turned down 1–21 and then a twentieth agreed 14–1, all 25 adults coming. In the one run whose stances were read in full (11 for, 1 against), most of those for it said their household gained little or lost, and backed it for their regard for the sponsor. **Found on the way:** a world's tie-breaks come from its identity, drawn afresh at each `civ-host new`, so two worlds of one seed differ in their details (the counts above varied between runs). This is by design (ADR-0011 §5's tie-break streams), not new; tests that need one world fix its identity.
- **Leaving weighed, as built (2026-10-08, M4a slice Z's third step):** before, a household out of food (less than a day's), worn down (its members had drawn on average `leave_at_depletion` of their reserve) and with no crop of its own ripening within 30 days or reaped and waiting, left with `leave_per_day`'s chance a day. Those conditions stand; the chance is now `leave_per_day` times the logistic of points for going less points for staying (the people profile's `[household]`, content API 32). **Going:** `leave_w_gap` (6) for the whole of the wait to its next harvest that staying would not cover. What covers it is its own food, its share of what the settlement's other households could spare (split among those out of food), and the common store's food if one of its members knows a common-store law in force, in days of its need against the days to its next harvest. **Staying:** `leave_w_stake` (3) for a whole year's food its fields should bring, which leaving gives up, and `leave_stay` (2) before either. With these tuning values a household with nothing to wait on leaves at 0.98 of the old rate, one that others or the store could carry to its harvest at 0.12 of it, and one with nothing in hand but a whole year's harvest coming at 0.73. The chance never exceeds the old rate. **Measured:** the ten-year smoke (on the build with the next step) passed all 10 worlds. Ria coast 5 was down to 7 people in year 3 and ended with 8 (15 in slice Z's first step, dying out in year 4 before it; worlds of one seed differ by run). Lived alone for four years, its third year's harvest was 0.67 of an average year's after 0.82 the year before; 22 of 42 people left in that year, though the store answered 167 asks with 312 kg. The store held 167–323 kg against what 42 people eat, and the levy could not be paid 264 and 393 times in years 2 and 3, so there was little to wait on: leaving ran near the old rate, as weighed. Whether a richer store keeps such a village is untested; the levy levels people chose are small (below).
- **The fifty-year dashboard on the build with leaving weighed (2026-10-08, before the next step, beside other work):** worlds took 790–1,974 s. Population green (all five keep ten or more; 67, 87, 91, 76 and 99 people at year 50; the most growth 2.5 times the founders), food prices green (grain asked 3–8 log points more before the harvest than after; stocks rose after 159 of 160 harvests), the Gini of goods amber (0.21–0.35), firm sizes green. **Structural failures red:** 9 failures of lived-in buildings in 4,470 building-years, 2.01 per 1,000 against at most 2, every one a hut roof whose rafters broke in a storm. Across the four recorded runs the counts were 11, 5, 7 and 9 (1.76 per 1,000 pooled), so the threshold sits inside the row's spread by chance; nothing was tuned. **Deaths:** 6 of the 9 killed someone (7 people), where both of slice W's runs killed nobody in 12. A time-boxed probe found no change: the collapse code is the same; at midnight, when buildings are checked, 99.9 % of people stood within reach of one of their household's buildings in both builds (3 seeds, 3 years each); and in slice W's saved run 3 of its 5 failures were huts their households had moved out of (20–57 m from home), while the 2 at home killed nobody, about even odds with four asleep at 0.15 each. The deaths are the designed risk for those under a roof that falls (ADR-0009 §5), not a regression.
- **Keeping the store, as built (2026-10-08, M4a slice Z's fourth step, ADR-0013 §2 in a first form):** a common store in force that holds food and has nobody keeping it is a new issue, `store_unkept`. It makes proposing a keeper a move under a new policy kind, `keep_store` (`core:policy/keep_store`, content API 33; `levy_shares` and `relief_days` now belong to `common_store` alone). **Nominating:** a deliberator's move names the adult of a sheltered household they regard most, themselves counted at full regard. Each household weighs its share of a year's spoilage saved under a roof (the store's food that would keep a year roofed but not in the open), as the change in the log of its year of food above subsistence; stances weigh regard for the one named. **The office:** the law names its holder. While they live in the settlement and their household is under a roof, the store spoils as a roofed store does and a household short of food asks for relief at the keeper's home. The goods stay the polity's on the ledger, apart from the keeper's household (ADR-0013 §4). **Succession:** when the holder dies or leaves, the law lapses (a new status, saved), the chronicle says so (in words like "Ada no longer keeps the common store at Stonewick: they died."), the store is unkept again, and a new proposal answers the issue. Nobody is heir to it (research 09-03 §3.1: early farming leadership can be task-specific; §5.1: an office has a holder and a vacancy state). **A deviation from ADR-0013 §2:** the ADR's Office primitive (a title, seats, eligibility, a selection procedure, removal, powers, pay and tenure as conditions) is not built. The first office is a law naming its holder, so it reuses the law pipeline's proposal, gathering, knowledge and history whole. Selection is the sponsor's nomination confirmed by the gathering, tenure lasts while the holder is resident, and there is no removal, pay or power over who gets relief. The general primitive waits until a second office, or M4b's sanctions, need seats, removal or pay. Saves keep the holder on the law (schema 30), and a later office section can be derived from such laws on load. **The observer (wire 1.28):** a law carries its holder, and a polity its offices in the kernel's words ("Storekeeper: Ada, since 3 May of year 2"; a lapsed one says how its holder went). The Government panel lists them, and the chronicle and panel name a keeper law "Ada as keeper of the common store". **Measured:** the ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds. Every village named a keeper after its store, the store's food spoiled 11–18 % of what came in (32–40 % when nobody kept any, slice Z's first step), and stores ended holding 0.8–10.5 t. Eight villages kept a twentieth; two kept a tenth. Four stores gave 77–357 kg in relief. In ria coast 5 lived alone, Rilla was named keeper 25–0 in year 2. In year 3 that law lapsed, the store lay unkept, and Brisa proposed themselves and was named 13–0. Gate B passed (20 runs, 1.1 minutes, every run passing its checks); the largest differences were walking −4.9 % in the valley and work +6.5 % on the coast, whose Detailed spread of work stays at ±0.28 h a person-day, as after the first step. The end-to-end suite passes (16 tests, the demos skipped) with the panel's offices list.
- **Labels, as built (2026-10-08, M4a slice Z's fifth step, ADR-0013 §6):** `civ_sim::labels` works out what each polity would be called from its saved history alone, and nothing is saved. **The evidence** covers the last 24 months, or since the founding if the polity is younger (research 09-15 §4B: 24 months; test 12–36). De jure: the share of the governed adults its body admits. De facto: the matters it decided and how many failed for want of a quorum; the mean share of the body that came; the laws passed and who put them forward; of those who backed the leading sponsor's laws, how many would have gained too little for their households to back them, so that regard carried them; laws passed before the window that still bind; the levies paid and kept back under the levies in force (those unable, or who did not know, did not refuse: 09-01 §6.4); and the offices its laws created, with how many have held each. **The classifier** is a short decision tree over that evidence, with 09-15 §2's vocabulary. A body admitting at least 90 % of the adults is a council community (binding collective decisions, leadership answering to an assembly; 09-15 §4B's broad-franchise display threshold); one admitting half to 90 % is a council community qualified by its share; one admitting less than half is an oligarchy. One sponsor of at least two thirds of at least three laws passed sets the agenda (09-15 §4B's dominant-controller tag). If regard carried at least half of that sponsor's backers, the name adds big-man leadership (09-15 §2, after Sahlins); otherwise it adds "led by its proposer". **Modifiers:** its custom not yet exercised; few come (a mean turnout below half); each office, and "that outlived its holder" once a second holder was named (ADR-0013 §6's "whether authority outlived its holder"); and its levy mostly paid (80 % or more), often kept back (half or more) or mostly kept back, judged only from ten cases up (09-15 §6.5: a small denominator is unobserved, not compliance). **Confidence** is n/(n+2) for n decisions with a quorum in the window plus laws still binding from before, at least 0.2. Every threshold is a display convention or tuning value, uncalibrated. **Nothing reads it:** the people live in `civ-agents`, which cannot see `civ-sim`. A test lives a village five days twice from one save, working out every label and the government panel daily on one copy, and every save section ends byte for byte the same. **Not built:** 09-15 §4B's six-month label persistence, which would need saved state, and comparisons with published datasets, which do not apply to a hearth gathering. Membership other than all adults does not exist yet, so the oligarchy branch is reached only in tests. **The observer (wire 1.29):** a polity carries its label, modifiers, reasons and confidence. The Government panel shows "Would be called: Council community: a storekeeper's office; its levy mostly paid (confidence 0.67)", opening to the reasons in words. The run report adds a line per settlement, the smoke note the name and confidence. The dashboard's Regimes row lists each world's labels and stays ungraded: every world keeps its founding custom, which none can yet amend or seize (M4c), so labels that differ would show offices and evidence, not who rules. **Measured:** the end-to-end suite passes (16 tests, the demos skipped), its government test now also opening the label to its reasons. In its world (river valley seed 2, 300 days) the label read "Council community", with a storekeeper's office and a levy owed 9 times, too few to judge. **The ten-year smoke** passed all 10 worlds, and every village ended labelled a council community at confidence 0.50. None had decided anything in its last two years; its store and its keeper's law still bound. Each proposed 2–16 laws and passed 2–3. In three villages a keeper was gone and a successor named, which the classifier reads as an office that outlived its holder. A first run of this smoke, before laws still in force counted as evidence, labelled every village at 0.20 and would have called a custom exercised years before "not yet exercised"; that was fixed. **An observation, no nudge made:** with one founding custom and two policy templates, every village gets the same name. Labels that differ wait for histories that change who decides (M4c).
- **The notables' gate, as built (2026-10-08, M4a slice Z's sixth step, ADR-0014 §4):** the notables' tier is a run setting (`Sim::set_notable_tier`, never saved; a loaded world has it on). With the tier, the weekly review lets these weigh institutional moves: the notables, and those something reached since the last review (the elders of households whose food will not last, and, new in this step, whoever came to a gathering or learnt a law). Without it, every adult does. **The gate** (`civ-host notables`) founds Gate B's two fixture worlds with a fixed identity, lives their first month by the minute and saves them. From each save, five runs with the tier and five without live at Max to the end of year 3, each with its own tie-break stream and each checked at every year's end as every long run is. Four polity aggregates are compared: laws proposed, laws passed, offices filled, and the share of the food reaped that the polity levied or gave in relief. A difference of means is taken as a share of the larger of the mean with the tier and a floor (one law; one percent), since counts this small need one. **The first calibration and a failure:** with the tier as slice Z's first step built it (notables and households running short only), eight runs with the tier proposed nothing in river valley 1. Ria coast 2 proposed 3.6 ± 0.7 laws, passed exactly 2 and filled exactly 1 office in every run, its polity moving 0.4 % of the food reaped. Tolerances were fixed from that before any run without the tier: proposals 40 % and moved 65 % (three standard errors, rounded up); laws passed and offices filled, which did not vary, 40 % (two runs in five differing by one, a tuning value). The gate then FAILED: without the tier the coast filled 1.6 offices against 1.0, and passed 2.6 laws against 2.0. The runs' notes showed why. In three of five runs without the tier, Ulmer named themselves keeper on day 971, died, and Oren was named on day 1027. With the tier, others were named (Wren, Calla, Pell, Lomas) and still held the office. **The fix followed the ADR, not the tolerances:** ADR-0014 §4 lets everyone else deliberate "when something reaches them: a gathering they attend, a law they learn, or their household running short", and the first step had built only the last. With all three, a new calibration (eight runs with the tier) gave the coast 4.0 ± 1.1 proposals, 2.25 ± 0.46 laws passed, 1.25 ± 0.46 offices and 0.3 % moved; in two of the eight, Ulmer's path appeared with the tier too. The same rule (three standard errors, rounded up to the next 5 %) set the tolerances at 55 %, 40 %, 75 % and 25 %. **The gate passed** (20 runs in 1.6 minutes, every run passing its checks). On the coast: proposals 3.8 against 4.0 without the tier (+5 %), laws passed 2.4 against 2.6 (+8 %), offices 1.4 against 1.6 (+14 %), moved 0.3 % against 0.4 % (+8 %). River valley 1 proposed nothing either way, so it shows only that the tier invents nothing there. **What it does not show:** five runs of one coast world, whose runs share most of their history, are a thin sample; the gate guards against a tier that suppresses what every adult would do, not against every difference. At a village's size the tier saves nothing measurable: runs took 18.4 s (valley) and 18.8 s (coast) on average either way.
- **A sponsor remembers the vote (2026-10-08, M4a slice Z's sixth step, content API 34):** the first run of the M4a demo voted 27 times on a fifth or a tenth, each turned down (1–20 to 1–24 for a fifth), before a twentieth passed in its second year. Mira proposed 14 of them and Vane 7, and nothing a sponsor saw at a gathering entered what they expected the next. Now one who came to a gathering that decided the same proposal (the same policy at the same level, or naming the same holder) within `vote_memory_days` (a year, a tuning value) expects no more support for it than it got there (research 09-05 §1.2: a sponsor weighs expected success). A sponsor whose household stands to gain enough may still try again, since a proposal is worth its forecast times the support expected. It changed nothing in the notables' gate's fixtures (the same calibration, run for run).
- **The M4a demo (2026-10-08, plan §7):** river valley seed 2, the earlier demos' seed, which draws a dry year 4 (§9, the M3c demo), at 768 cells with a band of 40. It was lived five years from the command line twice (`civ-host run --seed 2 --size 768 --years 5`) and to 1 November of year 5 in the observer (`web/e2e/m4a-demo.spec.ts` with `TCE_DEMO=1`), whose screenshots show the Government panel with the label and a law opened to its history, the Standing panel, the chronicle and the village. Runs differ (each world draws its own identity). **Run 1:** in the lean weeks before the first harvest, on 30 August of year 1, Arrel proposed a common store at a twentieth "because food would not last until the harvest". The gathering agreed 9–1, 23 of 25 adults coming. The store lay unkept until year 2's harvest, when Fara proposed Mira as keeper (16–0, 17 of 23 adults). Over five years the levy was paid 1,715 times (2.4 t), kept back 180 times and could not be paid 331 times. In the dry year 4 the village reaped 9.2 t against 18.5 t the year before; nobody went short, the store gave no relief, and it held 1.4 t, then 2.2 t at the end. **Run 2:** Vane proposed a twentieth on 23 August of year 1 (21–1) and themselves as keeper three weeks later (24–0); year 4 brought 8.8 t against 18.4 t, again with no relief and 2.3 t in store at the end. **The observer's run** (the final build): a twentieth passed at its first gathering, 10–1 with 24 of 25 adults coming, and Arrel proposed Jask as keeper (18–0). Opened, the law shows its history: the sponsor; nine who backed it, eight of them saying their household stood to lose but they thought well of the sponsor; Rilla against, their household standing to lose; and thirteen who abstained. The village's notables were Imma, Hild and Loren, each counted by 15–16 adults among those they esteem most, mostly for their word. At 1 November of year 5 it would be called a council community, with a storekeeper's office and its levy mostly paid (91 % of those able and knowing of it paid), at confidence 0.50: it had decided nothing in its last two years, and its two laws still bound. An earlier observer run, before a sponsor remembered the vote, voted 27 times on a fifth or a tenth before a twentieth passed (the entry above). **What it shows:** institutions held by people, from a custom every world shares, whose history can be read. Here the store was kept and paid into but gave nothing in the dry year, because households' own stores carried them. That is what the village did, and it is what is shown.
- **The dashboard on M4a's last build (2026-10-08, `db4b45b`):** passed in 47 minutes on four cores, a test run beside it for part of the time. Four rows green and the Gini of goods amber (0.25–0.39). **Population:** all five worlds keep their bands, ending with 77 to 94 people, the most growth 2.4 times the founders (world 3, year 44). **Food prices:** grain asked 2 to 9 log points more before the harvest than after, and stocks rose after all 157 harvests. **Firm sizes:** 128 to 226 workshops a world. **Structural failures:** 2 in 4,630 building-years (0.43 per 1,000), both hut roofs in storms, neither killing anyone; no empty building gave way. Over the five recorded runs: 11, 5, 7, 9 and 2 failures. **Regimes (ungraded):** every world ended a council community whose storekeeper's office had outlived a holder, its levy mostly paid.
- **Opinion, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AG step one, ADR-0016 §4):** no new ADR. **A position per question:** a policy template may ask a question (`question`, content API 41: "whether to keep a common store"; four of the core templates do), and every adult holds a position on it, 0 against to 1 for, with a salience (1 while a law of it is in force or before the gathering, 0.3 otherwise). **The anchor** is what the household's own lot makes of the policy: on the first of each month the sponsor's forecast (the same one a sponsor weighs, refactored out of the polity review so both read it) is worked out for the law in force, or else the template's middle level, and passed through a logistic of one point; the position is then pulled toward it with a two-year half-life (research 06-04 §1.4, §3.2: Friedkin–Johnsen anchoring; without anchors opinions collapse to consensus, §2). **Talk at the hearth:** companions say where they stand, each question by a keyed chance at its salience, and a listener closes a share of the gap, η 0.03 times salience times their regard for the teller times a smooth disagreement gate (ε 0.25), doubled under 30 (06-04 §3.2's priors); no negative influence in v0. **At a gathering** the stance adds two points per unit that talk has moved someone from their household's lot, so a member's own gain still decides most stances, and the law's record keeps that part and says "talk at the hearth had drawn them toward it" (or turned them against it) when it mattered. **Measured, and changed:** the first build told 14 to 36 positions a person a week, against 06-04 §3.2's 0.5 (0.05–5); the chance a session was cut to 0.0015, and the smoke measured 0.46 to 1.85 told (0.33 to 1.62 taken in) a person a week, with talk moving positions 0.003 to 0.007 from their anchors on average: talk shades opinion, it does not make it. **A latent flaw of slice AF surfaced:** with stances a little more spread, overruled members began to amend the custom, and two worlds cycled, 51 amendments (50 passed) and 8 (8 passed) in ten years. An amendment's forecast had re-decided every decision a household saw, amendments included and those made under earlier customs, so each change gave the other side cause to change it back. It now counts only the laws and cases decided under the present custom, never an amendment. **The ten-year smoke then passed all 10 worlds:** one village amended its custom once (coast 2, to its households' elders, a quarter of whom must come; labelled an oligarchy, 0.75) and none cycled; about 50 people hold 104 to 136 positions. The smoke's own check of each law's body now reads the custom in force on the day it was decided, and counts a superseded amendment as having passed. **Boundary:** saves schema 37 (36 still loads: positions are taken on the next first of the month), wire 1.36 (each person's positions in the inspector, the talk part of a recorded stance), content API 41. **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** values, ideologies and norms (the rest of slice AG); confidence and redundancy in taking talk in (06-04 §1.4's b·z term is the monthly pull); positions of children; and positions that weigh in anything but a gathering's stance.
- **Norms, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AG step two, ADR-0016 §4):** no new ADR. **A norm is content** (`kind = "norm"`, content API 42): what it says, what the kernel does with it (`does`; only `abide_by_laws` is built) and the priors its states are drawn from; the core pack's `core:norm/gathering_binds` says that what the gathering decides binds everyone. **Each person's state** (research 06-05 §1.1, §5.1–§5.2): an endorsement, drawn by a key on the norm's content id and pulled toward the mean of their parents' as the objection to taking is (log-odds mean 0.4, spread 1, heritability 0.5); a belief of the share of households that abide, which founders start at 0.85 (the custom they brought) and someone new takes from their household; and a threshold drawn evenly from 0.2 to 0.9 (06-05 §2.2), with a smooth step a tenth wide. Each takes theirs on the first of the month, in id order so parents draw first, or when first asked to pay a levy. **What it does:** paying a levy or keeping a curfew the gathering passed weighs one point for a full endorsement and one for a belief well above one's threshold (06-05 §5.2: μV + γA), and the people profile's flat `comply_base` falls from 1.5 to 0: the founders' village starts near the old weight (about 0.58 + 0.93), and from then each person's own state carries it. In a test village, a store's levy was paid 0.91 of the times it was decided where everyone held the norm firmly and believed every household paid, and 0.59 where nobody did. **What moves belief:** only what companions at the hearth say of their own household's last levy (the share of the season's threshings it paid; a household that could not pay is not counted, as the custom does not ask what a household has not got), each by a keyed chance of 0.001 a session, closing a tenth of the gap (06-05 §2.2: 0.02–0.30); never the settlement's true rate. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds:** endorsement 0.52 to 0.60 on average; people believed 0.90 to 0.95 of households abide where 0.86 to 0.95 had paid at their last levy; 0.82 to 0.96 were held to it by others' doing it; 0.08 to 0.29 accounts a person a week (06-05 §2.2's learning window is 8 to 32 informative encounters). The norm is stable here because levies are mostly paid: it amplifies a lean year's keeping back only weakly (with these priors a change in belief moves paying about a fifth as much, worked out from the logistic and the spread of thresholds rather than measured, so no spiral), recorded and not tuned. Three villages amended their custom once each, none cycled. **Also in this build:** grievances and hearing records of the dead and departed are now let go each midnight (slice AE kept them: a coast world of 10 people reported grievances held by 28; it now reports 6 held by 2). **Boundary:** saves schema 38 (37 still loads: everyone takes a state at the next first of the month), wire 1.37 (each person's norms in the inspector), content API 42. **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** the normative expectation (what others think one ought to do; nothing yet says what anyone approves of), sanctions for breaking a norm, people hiding what their household kept back, and gossip of other households' acts; norms of other kinds, among them sharing in need, which waits for asks a household can refuse at will; and norms that supply a grievance's expectation (ADR-0016 §2: grievances still arise only against a law's terms).
- **Values, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AG step three, ADR-0016 §4):** no new ADR. **A value is content** (`kind = "value"`, content API 43); the core pack names three after research 06-04 §1.1's list (security, autonomy, reciprocity): safety from want and harm, a household's say over what is its own, and giving back what was given or taken. **Each person's** is drawn between -1 (less than most) and 1 (more than most) as `tanh(mean + sd z)`, by a key on the value's content id, a child taking half its parents' mean (06-04 §1.7: transmission with variation), and kept for life (§3.2's twenty-year drift toward experience is not built). The core priors are a mean of 0 (the core pack tilts nobody one way), a spread of 0.8 and a weight of one point: design priors. **A policy template says how it bears** (`[bears]`, -1 to 1, an authoring judgement): a common store for safety (1) and giving back (0.5) and against a household's say (-0.5); a law against taking for giving back (1) and safety (0.5); a watch for safety (1) and against a household's say (-0.5); a curfew for safety (0.5) and against a household's say (-1). **What a law does to what someone holds dear** (their values times its bearing, at each value's weight) adds to their anchor on its question beside their household's lot, to their stance at its gathering (the record keeps that part and says "what they hold dear drew them toward it" or "turned them against it"), and to how a sponsor weighs proposing it. Nobody sees another's values: a sponsor counts on others by their households' lots alone. **Taking states moved to midnight:** everyone takes their values and norms the first midnight they are here (the founders on the first night, a child the night after their birth), not on the first of the month: the curfew test lives only three days, and found that until 1 April nobody held the norm, so a curfew weighed nothing. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds:** values held -0.19 to +0.29 on average and 19 to 68 % strongly (|v| ≥ 0.5) in villages of 10 to 50; what people hold dear weighed (beyond the stance margin) in 24 to 54 % of the stances taken; villages proposed 2 to 6 laws in ten years, as before (2 to 13 under norms), and three amended their custom once each. In the government end-to-end world, a village amended its custom to two-thirds, 23 to 0. **Boundary:** saves schema 39 (38 still loads: everyone draws their values the next midnight), wire 1.38 (each person's values in the inspector, the values part of a recorded stance), content API 43. **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** values that move with experience, status and obligations beyond kin (06-04 §1.1's other values), values seen or guessed by others, and values weighing in anything but laws.
- **Ideologies, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AG step four, ADR-0016 §4):** no new ADR. **An ideology is content** (`kind = "ideology"`, content API 44), after research 06-04 §6.1's record: the problem it explains (an issue the polity already names), its commitments (how it tilts each value its holders hold, -1 to 1), its program (the policy templates it proposes), its legitimacy story in words, and how it travels. The core pack names three: common provision (explains food short; safety 0.5, giving back 0.3; a common store and a storekeeper), order kept by all (explains takings; safety 0.4, giving back 0.4; a law against taking and a watch) and each household its own (explains being overruled; a household's say 0.6; no program). **Holding one:** the first midnight someone is here, one with no parents recorded (a founder, a newcomer) brings each ideology by a keyed chance of 0.1, and a child takes up what a parent holds by a chance of 0.5 (06-04 §1.7); the record keeps when and from whom. At the hearth a holder speaks of one by a keyed chance of 0.0005 a session, and a listener who does not hold it takes it up by 0.3 × their trust in the teller × how well its commitments fit what they hold dear (the sum of value × commitment, clipped to 0 to 1), so one that runs against what they hold is never taken up (06-04 §1.4: exposure is not acceptance). Nobody gives one up yet. **What it does:** a holder weighs every law with the ideology's commitments as if they were values they hold, in their anchor, stance and weighing of proposing it; and when the problem it explains is before the village (06-04 §1.2: an explanation shapes the answer to a problem that is felt, not the problem itself) the templates its program names join their moves with a point (`w_program`) for proposing them. A law proposed by one who holds an ideology naming it keeps that creed (saved apart from news claims, not "spread as a claim" as ADR-0016 sketched: a creed is a holding, not an account of an event), and the Government panel says it was proposed "as one who holds to" it. **Two findings on the way.** First, programs weighed whether or not their problem was present: holders proposed stores in villages never short of food, and eleven tests broke; programs now wait on the problem. Second, with adoption by trust alone, every ideology reached 31 to 52 of about 50 people in ten years, everyone holding everything; the fit gate stopped that, and at an adoption chance of 0.1 only 3 to 12 held each, so the chance was set at 0.3 (a design prior, chosen by this calibration). **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds:** each ideology was held by 0 to 35 of 17 to 56 people (most often a sixth to a half; each household its own died out in one valley); 0.019 to 0.071 spoken of and up to 0.002 taken up a person a week; 15 of the 29 laws proposed were proposed by someone holding an ideology that names them (14 passed: common stores under common provision, one law against taking under order kept by all), and villages proposed 2 to 6 laws in ten years, as before. That a holder's creed marks a law does not show the creed caused it: most were stores proposed when food was short, which the household's own lot already favoured. **Boundary:** saves schema 40 (39 still loads: everyone takes their start the next midnight), section `creeds` (eight-byte tags), wire 1.39 (each person's ideologies in the inspector, a law's creed in its issue), content API 44. **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** giving one up or turning against it, ideologies made or recombined by people (06-04 §1.7), organizations that teach one (ADR-0016 §4), the god tool that introduces one (slice AJ), and programs that name amendments of the custom.
- **Factions, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AH step one, ADR-0017 §2):** no new ADR (ADR-0017 decided the organization, its treasury on the ledger and memberships on persons). **A faction** organizes those who hold grievances against one party, the gathering or an office, and records its founder, organizer, store and history. **Founding** (04-10 §1.1, §1.5; never because enough people are angry, 04-06 §1.8): on their review day, an adult who belongs to none, knows of no faction against that party and feels their keenest grievance against the gathering or an office at `found_floor` 0.3 or more weighs founding one at `w_grievance` 1 × how keenly they feel it + `w_shared` 1 × how far it is shared (the others they regard at `trust_floor` 0.25 or more whom they have heard hold one against the same party, each counted once however often told, of `shared_full` 3) − `found_cost` 0.5, against a threshold of their own drawn evenly from 0.5 to 2 by a key (04-10 §1.3); with nobody to share it, never. **Joining and staying:** each adult reviews where they belong on a day of their own each month (`review_days` 30; 09-12 §2.2). Belonging is worth `w_grievance` × their grievance against its party + `w_organizer` 0.5 × their regard for its organizer + `w_belong` 1 × the share of those they know who belong (the count beside it, 04-10 §5.2) − `w_dues` 5 × `dues_share` 0.05; one who belongs nowhere joins the faction they know of (whose organizer or a member they know) worth most, if it beats their threshold, and a member leaves only when it falls `leave_margin` 0.5 below it (04-10 §5.4). Membership is kept apart from belief: a member's grievance may fade while they stay, and someone who holds one may never join (04-10 §5.1). Every number is a design prior; no report gives a founding size or a joining rate. **The store** is a holder on the ledger, kept at its organizer's home: a member's household gives `dues_share` of each threshing (channel `dues`, appended as code 17) while the store holds less than `reserve_days` 20 of its members' households' food, unless it cannot spare it (the levy's test), and a member's household short of food may ask it, as it asks the common store, for up to `aid_days` 5 of its food (04-10 §1.2: mutual assistance; channel `gift`). Its longest-standing member takes up its organizing when the organizer dies, leaves or quits it; one with no members ends, and what its store holds goes to its last organizer's household. **Three findings on the way.** First, dues without a bound: stores reached 2,026 and 3,766 kg in ten years and gave nothing, because members' households never fell below five days of food after they joined (an instrumented coast village found no member's household asking for food in eighteen months); dues now stop at the reserve. Second, a founder counted full regard for themselves as organizer, which kept founders nobody joined in their own factions (a village with factions of 13, 1, 1 and 1); without it, a founder nobody joined quit and founded again the next month (117 foundings in one village), so one whose faction ended does not found another within `retry_days` 365 (04-10 §3: a failed attempt lowers what one expects of the next). Third, a smoke run left two coast villages at 16 and 14 people where earlier runs had 41 to 50; the same two seeds with fixed identities, factions on and off, ended at 44 against 42 and 42 against 42, so it was the draw of the worlds' lives (a smoke world's identity is new each run), not factions. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds:** factions formed in the three hungry coast villages and nowhere else (the valleys hold no grievances): one of 26 members in a village of 48; two with members at the end (12 and 1) of 8 founded in another, a lone founder trying again each year; one of 4 in a village down to 6, with 29 joinings and 25 leavings as people died and left. Dues came to 251 to 742 kg a world and the stores gave out 0 to 108 kg. **Boundary:** saves schema 41 (section `factions`; 40 still loads, with none), wire 1.40 (a person's faction and why in the inspector, kernel words; each polity's factions in the Government panel), content API 45 (`[faction]`). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** a faction's program and demands, petitions, assemblies, refusals of a levy, damage and violence (steps two and three), splits, factions over an ideology alone, belonging to two, and spending the store on anything but aid.
- **Petitions, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AH step two, ADR-0017 §3):** no new ADR (ADR-0017 §3 decided episodes: coming is a scored choice and the answer is the gathering's). **Calling** (research 09-04 §5.5: petitioning is one response beside complying, evading and leaving; 09-05 §1.2: petitioning is a right apart from proposing): at their monthly review a faction's organizer weighs calling a petition at the hearth once it has `petition_members` 3 members, none of its petitions waits and its last was called `petition_days` 180 or more days ago, at `w_grievance` 1 × its members' mean grievance against its party + the share of those the organizer knows who belong − `petition_cost` 0.5, against their own threshold. **Its demand answers the party blamed** (04-10 §1.1): against the gathering, the common store at another of its template's shares, or at none, in place of the one in force, whichever its members' households forecast most for, and only if they forecast it to bring them something; against an office, another holder in place of the one in it, whom the organizer would name as any proposer names a keeper (themselves if their roof would do, or whoever under one they regard most; for the watch, any adult), never the one blamed. A law may now name the law it replaces (`ends`), and passing it supersedes that law, so no new policy kind was needed; a store at no share levies nothing and still gives what it holds, and since a store in force keeps ordinary proposers from proposing another, only a petition changes it. **Word** goes from the organizer to its members the day it is called, then by word of mouth as a gathering's call does (a third claim kind, `petition`, let go once it has sat); it sits the next evening (`notice_days`). **Coming** is a scored activity (`core:activity/petition`, behaviour `petition`) for an adult who heard of it, on the evening it sits, with 04-10 §5.3's terms from their own records: `w_grievance` × their grievance against its party, `w_member` 1 if they belong or else `w_organizer` 0.5 × their regard for its organizer (identification and organization), and `w_expect` 1 × f(S), S the share of those they know, by how well, who belong; f(S) = S, or 4S(1 − S) for the keyed `free_ride_share` 0.2 of people for whom more others coming adds less once half do (04-10 §1.4: strategic substitutability; the shape is a design prior). It competes with everything else they might do that evening, and a curfew in force weighs against it (only the watch and those at a gathering are exempt). **The answer:** at the first midnight after it sat with no gathering waiting, the organizer proposes the demand as called (issue `petition`), those who came learn of the proposal, and the gathering decides it as any law. For a new officeholder, each who came weighs their regard for the one named less their regard for the one in office, which a grievance against the office lowers (the holder defends their place at full regard). A petition turned down, split or too thinly attended to decide is a wrong (`refused`, felt as `refused_days` 5 of a household's food) that each who came holds against the gathering (04-10 §3: failed petitioning is a precursor); one nobody came to ends there, and one whose law in force changed or whose organizer is gone is never put. A petition records who came (unique participants, 04-10 §4.2); it sits one evening, so participant-days equal them and the peak and duration are not kept. **Two findings on the way.** First, the first build let only factions against the gathering petition, and its ten-year smoke called none: a probe of the three coast villages with factions (11 to 23 members) found every faction against the storekeeper (an empty store is held against its keeper), so the demand now follows the party blamed. Second, those who came then abstained at the gathering (one coast village called 15 petitions in ten years, 13 of them split, one person for and one against), because nothing in their stance held their grievance; a grievance against an office now lowers regard for its holder in that decision. **Also fixed:** a keeper whose own household found the store empty held it against their own keeping; they now hold it against the gathering. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds.** Factions formed only in the three hungry coast villages, as before. In two of them organizers called petitions, all for another keeper: in one, 22 came and the gathering granted it; in the other, 16 and 17 came to two, one granted and one without a quorum, so 16 held its refusal against the gathering. The third (a faction of 12) called none. A run of the same build before the smoke report counted replacements had petitions in all three (one each with 24 and 23 who came, granted; two with 14 and 27, one granted, one without a quorum). No petition was for a store's share, and none was turned down by a vote. One coast village ended at 26 people where four earlier runs of its seed today ended at 46 to 53: not probed; the world's identity is drawn anew each run and step one's fixed-identity comparison found such swings were the draw. **Boundary:** saves schema 42 (laws' `ends`; petitions in `factions`; 41 still loads, with none), wire 1.41 (each polity's petitions in the Government panel, in kernel words), content API 46 (the `petition` activity and seven `[faction]` keys). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** assemblies with other demands (another template, an amendment of the custom), what petitioners expect it to achieve and the sanction they expect (04-10 §5.3's E and pD), a grievance weighing at the gathering on a store's share (households weigh only what they forecast), refusals of a levy, damage and violence (step three).
- **Refusals of a levy, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AH step three, ADR-0017 §3):** no new ADR. ADR-0017 §3 names three kinds of episode (petition, assembly, refusal of a levy); this is the second built, after the petition. **Calling** (research 09-04 §5.5: refusing beside petitioning and leaving; 04-10 §1.9: a withdrawal works through what it withholds): at their monthly review, when no petition is called (one waits, the last was within `petition_days`, nothing answers, or it is not worth it), an organizer whose members blame the gathering or the store's keeper weighs calling on them to keep back the levy of the store in force together, openly, for `refusal_days` 365. It needs a store that levies something, `petition_members` members, and none of the faction's petitions waiting nor refusals standing. Its worth is what a petition's would be (`w_grievance` × the members' mean grievance + the share of those the organizer knows who belong), less `refusal_cost` 0.5 (as calling a petition costs) and less what the norms the organizer holds weigh for abiding by what the gathering decided. Where a petition can be called it is always worth more, and in a village that holds the gathering binds (about 1.4 points on average) a refusal is rare. **Word** of it goes from the organizer to its members, then by word of mouth (a fourth claim kind, `refusal`, let go when it ends). **Joining** is each thresher's choice at their own threshing, for one who heard of it: what joining is worth to them, with the same terms as coming to a petition (grievance against its party, belonging or regard for the organizer, the share of those they know who belong, free-riding for some), is taken from the points for paying, so it weighs toward keeping back. A levy kept back by one who heard of a refusal is counted on the law as kept back in a refusal (`refused`, `refused_kg`), apart from one kept back unannounced (`evaded`; `withheld_kg` counts both); the refusal records who kept back and how much. Their household tells companions it did not pay, which moves others' belief of how many abide, as any keeping back does (M4c slice AG). **Answer:** none beyond what the gathering already does. Nobody collects a levy (households pay at their own threshing), so a refusal meets no one; the store has less to give, and members may petition again when they can. A test village with no one holding the norm called a refusal of a fifth's levy; at the harvest 29 kept back 355 kg under it while 6 levies were paid. **The first prior was wrong:** with `refusal_cost` 1 a refusal was worth less than its organizer's threshold even with no norm held (0.65 against 0.78 in the test), so it could hardly ever be called. Since what sets refusing apart from petitioning is the norm it breaks, the cost is now a petition's. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds** on a second run. The first run failed one coast world's 30-day check: a household was without water at a day's end, the rare gap recorded twice before (1 in about 35 runs). Nothing this step changed runs before a village has a store and a faction. No refusal was called in either run: every organizer held the norm or could petition. Petitions were called in three coast villages, five in all, every one to replace a keeper and every one granted (9 to 28 came); one village replaced its keeper three times in ten years as the store ran empty again. **A probe:** one coast village ended at 49, 26, 32 and 16 people in the four runs since keeper petitions began, against 46 to 53 before, so the same seed was lived ten years from four fixed identities with petitions on and off. On and off ended at 42 and 39, 45 and 45, 33 and 30, and 17 and 17 people: petitions did not cost the village people, and the spread is the identity's draw. **Boundary:** saves schema 43 (laws' `refused`, `refused_kg`; refusals in `factions`; 42 still loads), wire 1.42 (a law's `refused`; each polity's refusals in the Government panel, in kernel words), content API 47 (`refusal_cost`, `refusal_days`). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** an answer to a refusal (a sanction proposed, the store's keeper collecting), refusing other obligations, and damage and violence, moved to slice AI (below).
- **Damage and violence wait for an office of force (2026-10-08, plan decision):** ADR-0017 §3 has damage to property and violence against people as separate choices within an episode, and the plan put them in AH's step three. They move to slice AI, which brings the first office of force. Violence is an interaction process that answers encounters with those who disperse, collect or repress (04-10 §1.8), and none of these villages has anyone who does: a levy is paid at each household's own threshing, the watch walks rounds against takings, and nobody commands force. Built now, a per-person choice to damage or harm within a petition or a refusal would either never fire or fire without cause, which would be plot. 06-10 §4 also asks that harm the engine can produce be answerable (who did what to whom, under which decision, and what followed); that wants the incident records of an office's orders, which AI brings. Not an ADR: ADR-0017 is unchanged, only the slice that builds it.
- **Founding, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AI step two, ADR-0017 §5):** no new ADR (ADR-0017 §5 decided founding as bargaining from what was inherited). **The window:** for `founding_days` 90 after the custom is taken from the gathering (a design prior: research 09-11 §1.6's founding processes run 3 to 36 months and none is forced to finish), while some law the old custom made is still unweighed, the issue `founding` is before the village. **Representation** (09-11 §1.6: through institutions that exist): every member of the body that took the deciding deliberates at each weekly review in the window, not only its notables. **Proposals** (09-11 §1.6: packages built from the inherited arrangements): a new policy kind `repeal` (content `core:policy/repeal`, answering `founding`) opens to each of them an end to each law the old custom made: a store's levy at none, as a petition would end it (the store gives what it holds), or any other law repealed. **Weighing:** each household weighs an end as what the law, kept, would bring it, turned about, and what the law does to what its people hold dear (values, and a creed that proposes it), turned about too; the new body decides it as any law (its own procedure, the ratification). A repeal that passes is *carried* (a new law status), the law it ends superseded; an office ended leaves a vacancy the ordinary review may fill. **Each law is put once:** what the body leaves, or turns down, stands (provisional authority, unless actors dismantle it). **A fix in passing:** a petition to end a store's levy had weighed what people hold dear as if it were a store; any end to a law now weighs it turned about. **Test:** after the revolt of step one held, the old custom's heavy levy and a curfew were both put within two weeks by members of the new body, and both passed (22 for and 1 against; 24 for and none against): the levy at none, the curfew repealed and carried; it saves and loads exactly. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds** on the first run; no revolt was called, so no founding (three petitions in two villages, all for another keeper, all granted). **Boundary:** saves schema 45 (laws' status `carried` and issue `founding`; 44 still loads), wire 1.44 (status 5, carried; the web says "superseded by a later law" for any law a later one replaced, not only an amendment), content API 49 (`repeal`, `founding`, `[polity] founding_days`, `core:policy/repeal`). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** bargaining among factions over packages of provisions (09-11 §1.6's U_f), the old gathering as a second representative body, reserved privileges for the losers, appointments as a founding move of their own (an office ended is filled by the ordinary review), and a founding after a coup (step three).
- **A watch of several and coups, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AI step three, ADR-0017 §4):** no new ADR (ADR-0017 §4 decided that a coup is open only to those holding an office that commands force, the watch when it is several, with no roll). **A watch of several:** while takings continue (food taken from a household since the newest watcher was named) the watch template stays open with a watch in force, so a further watcher may be proposed, each named by a law of their own, and only by one it would serve (as an amendment); what one more adds is what the others leave unguarded, `g(1 − g)^n` of the guard `g` one watcher gives beside `n` others (independent rounds; a forecast, not a measured rate), so each further watcher is worth less. Every watcher walks rounds and sees; a further one's rounds start past those before. **A coup** (research 09-11 §1.4: willingness and coordination apart): at their monthly review, a watcher of several who holds a grievance against the gathering weighs calling on the others to take the deciding for the watch, worth their grievance and how well the other watchers regard them (officers act on what they believe the others will do), less `coup_cost` 0.75 and the norms they hold. Not where the watch decides already, nor while a coup or a revolt stands there. **Sides:** each midnight the watchers, and only they, stand with it, with the gathering or with neither, as anyone stands with a revolt (their grievance, their regard for the one who called it, and the share of the other watchers with it the day before), less the norms they hold. **Holding:** once more watchers stand with it than with the gathering for `hold_days` 7 (09-11 §2.2's coding), the body becomes those who keep the watch (a new membership, `watch`), by the custom's own quorum and rule; the custom is taken, its version naming the one who called it, and the founding of step two follows. It comes to nothing when its caller no longer keeps the watch, the watch is no longer several, or after `revolt_days`. The rest of the village takes no side: without force (step four) nothing they do bears on it. **Test:** three watchers who all held a grievance and regarded one another well: one called a coup at their review, all three stood with it, and it held a week later, the watch deciding thereafter; it saves and loads exactly. A watch of two each walked their own rounds. **The first build was wrong:** with the template simply left open, the first ten-year smoke had one coast village name 18 watchers (17 at once, 80,094 rounds in ten years) and another 28: a move worth almost nothing is still proposed now and then (the choice among moves is soft, against a proposal's cost), and it passes on regard for the one it names. Before this step a law in force closed its template, which hid that. Requiring that takings continued since the newest watcher was named, and that the sponsor gains by it, brought the rerun to one watch in one village and three named over ten years in another. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds** on both runs; no coup was called (no village had a watch of several when a watcher reviewed with a grievance), and three petitions were, all granted. **Two tests changed:** a crime test that assumed one watcher now accepts a further watcher the village named while takings went on (it did, in that test); and the ideology test that asked companions to take up an ideology within a month failed about 1 run in 20 because, for some worlds' identities, it fitted nobody who heard it (0 of 337 tellings taken up), so the test now has everyone hold dear what the most-held ideology commits them to. **Boundary:** saves schema 46 (coups, kept as revolt records with no faction; 45 still loads), wire 1.45 (each polity's coups in the Government panel, in kernel words), content API 50 (`coup_cost`; the membership `watch`). A decision's tally now names the body's members ("3 of 4 watchers came"). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** leaving the watch in a coup (09-11 §1.4's fragmenting), which waits for step four, where staying has a cost; resistance by anyone but the watchers; a coup by the storekeeper; and counter-coups beyond what any later coup or revolt can do.
- **Force, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AI step four, ADR-0017 §3–§4):** no new ADR (ADR-0017 §3 has violence as a person's choice within an episode and §4 the office of force; the plan decision above moved damage and violence to this slice). **Going to collect** (research 09-06 §1.3: carrying out an order is a stage of its own): at their monthly review, one who keeps the watch weighs going to take what a gathering's finding owed and the household refused (an obligation a case imposed, still owed, on a household of their settlement other than their own, and not gone to within the faction review's span). It is worth `w_collect` 0.5, the norms they hold (what the gathering decides binds), and their regard for the household owed (none for a fine, which goes to the common store) less their regard for the household owing, against their own threshold. **How they are met** (04-10 §1.8: violence answers encounters, and is each person's choice): each adult of the household lets it be taken, worth the norms they hold and `w_regard` × their regard for the watcher, or resists, worth how strongly their household refused it and their grievances against the watch and the gathering. One whose will to resist passes their own reluctance to strike, keyed per person in `strike_threshold` [1, 4] (a design prior), strikes; the others who resist stand in the way. **Force:** where anyone resisted, the watcher takes it by force if it is still worth it less `w_harm` 1 for each who did, or turns back. What is taken is the household's food up to what is still owed, whatever it keeps back, and goes where the finding said, on the obligation's channel (an execution takes what is held, 09-06 §5.4). **Blows** (06-10 §4.C: every injury and death resolves to a record and a cause): only where it was taken by force, each who struck strikes the watcher and the watcher strikes back. A blow keeps the one struck from work for 3 to 21 days (`hurt_days`, drawn; they still sleep, eat, fetch water, keep company, rest, ask, and come to gatherings and petitions, with the reason "a blow keeps them from work"), and kills with `kill_share` 0.02, a design prior (no report gives a rate for a blow among villagers): a death by violence, "died of a blow". **What it leaves** (04-10 §1.7): each adult of the household holds a grievance against the watch for what was taken by force and for each blow the watcher struck; whoever is struck thinks the worse of the one who struck them (a tie act, `struck`, a bad act in provision at weight 3). The chronicle tells the encounter in plain verbs, nothing justified (06-10 §4.C–D: who did what to whom, under which decision, and what followed): "Dara, who keeps the watch, came to the household of Brisa to take what a finding of the gathering owed; Brisa and Vane met Dara with blows; Dara took 4 days' food for the household of Tam by force; Brisa struck Dara, who was kept from work 15 days; …". The Order panel lists encounters newest first. **Tests:** a watcher went to a household that had refused a finding, was met with blows, took what was owed by force, and every blow was recorded with its days, the household's grievances against the watch and the tie written, saving and loading exactly; with `kill_share` at 1 a blow killed, a death by violence with its record. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds**; no village named a watch or refused a finding this run, so no watcher went to collect and force is shown by the tests alone. **Boundary:** saves schema 47 (encounters on the order; 46 still loads), wire 1.46 (the Order panel's encounters, in kernel words), content API 51 (`w_collect`, `w_harm`, `strike_threshold`, `hurt_days`, `kill_share`; the tie act `struck`). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** keeping a seized store (a coup or a revolt changes the body by its holders' choices, and the store's keeper only by law); damage to property; fear or deterrence from a blow seen or heard of (ties' `fear` is still not written); the inspector showing how long a hurt person is kept from work; and whether those of the household are at home when the watcher comes (every adult of it meets them).
- **A whisper and an ideology heard of, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AJ step one, ADR-0016 §5):** no new ADR (ADR-0016 §5 decided the god tools: each submits only what a person could perceive, one record per recipient and proposition, every use logged). **The record:** each use of a god tool is an intervention record (`civ_agents::influence`): its number, when first and last used, how often, its kind, whom it reached and what it submitted; a repeat to the same person with the same thing refreshes that record and never makes another (research 15-05 §4.5: a hundred identical whispers equal one). The chronicle logs each first use as one recorded influence ("One recorded influence: the observer whispered to Mara that Bo holds a grievance against the gathering over what was taken from their household."), and nothing in the world reads the records. **The whisper:** a true claim the adult's settlement's word holds and they have not heard (a gathering or a petition called and still to sit, a refusal or a revolt declared and still standing, or another's grievance someone has told), placed in their hearing from no one. What they do with it is what anyone who heard it would do: come to a gathering or a petition, join a refusal, take a side in a revolt, or, for a grievance against a party they also blame, count one more who shares it when they weigh founding a faction (04-10 §1.1). Its salience is the claim's own: a call lapses once its day is past and a grievance after `news_days` 60 untold, two of the monthly reviews that read it (15-05 §5's one to three decision cycles). It is not passed on as news unless it is a call; a grievance is told only by those who hold it. **An ideology heard of** (as `IntroduceTechnique { aware_only }` makes a technique known): the adult weighs it as one heard at the hearth is, by how well its commitments fit what they hold dear (06-04 §1.4: exposure is not acceptance), with no teller to doubt, so the chance is the content's `adopt` × the fit, none unless it fits at all (a design prior: a stranger's word would carry nothing, a trusted teller's the most). The draw is keyed to the person and the ideology alone, so telling them again draws nothing new; they weigh it again at each monthly review while it is fresh (within `news_days` of the last telling), by the same draw, so only a change in what they hold dear can change the answer (15-05 §4.5: new context can matter). One who takes it up holds it as anyone does and may speak of it at the hearth. **What came of it** (15-05 §6): the inspector's new section, the observer's hand, lists each influence on a person with what followed, read from the records people keep and drawing nothing: for a whisper, when it was heard, how many others they told, and whether they came, joined, kept back their levy or stood with a revolt, or founded or joined a faction against the party whose grievance they heard; for an ideology, how often they weighed it and when they took it up, or how well it fits them now and the chance that carries, and how many took it up from them. The news a person has not heard, and the ideologies, are offered there to whisper and to tell; a child cannot be reached, and a claim they heard already, or an ideology they hold, is refused. **Tests:** a whisper of another's grievance is heard from no one, logged once, and a hundred repeats leave one record of 101 uses and one chronicle entry; one who has heard it, and a child, are refused; it wrote no grievance, faction, law or holding; an ideology told of to one it does not fit is not taken up however often told (one record, weighed once), one it fits takes it up from no one, and the first takes it up at a review a month after what they hold dear changed; all of it saves and loads exactly; a schema-47 save loads with no records; the end-to-end test tells an adult of common provision from the inspector and a repeat adds nothing. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds** (it uses no god tool, and one the observer never reached weighs nothing more at their review). **Boundary:** saves schema 48 (the `influenc` section; 47 still loads), wire 1.47 (the commands `Whisper` and `TellOfIdeology`; the welcome's ideologies; a person's news and influences; a heard claim's and a holding's influence), no content API change. **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** the whisper of a deliberator's move (the M4 observer brief's other reading of 15-05; ADR-0016 §5 chose a true claim), the truth of a taking whispered as a belief (ADR-0015's beliefs keep their own sources), an ideology seen by the witnesses of a public sign (15-05 §5's 25–100), and "considered" for a whisper, which would need decisions to record what they read.
- **The agitator, blessings and curses, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AJ step two, ADR-0016 §5):** no new ADR. **The agitator** (ADR-0016 §5: a newcomer holding an ideology, sent by the path that sends a family, an ordinary person bound by every law, starting with a stranger's ties; settled in the M4a design that an agitator is not a person rewritten): the map tool sends one adult, 20 to 40 years old, with a household of their own and a newcomer's provisions, where a family would go (joining a settlement within reach, else making camp). They hold the ideology chosen from the day they come, as one who brought it (and, like any newcomer, whatever else the content's founders' shares give them that midnight), know nobody, and are moved by no special disposition: whether anyone takes it up from them is the hearth's ordinary telling and trust, which a stranger has none of. The chronicle tells it as one recorded influence ("…the observer sent Sela, who holds to order kept by all, to Hazelford.") in place of a family's arrival, and the inspector says how many took it up from them and how many they know there now. A family's making is unchanged; a household of one has no couple, partner or union. **A blessing or a curse** (the brief's proposal, research 15-05 being silent on luck: for a stated period, the target's own keyed draws for named material purposes): for 30, 90, 365 or 1,825 days, the person's own chance of illness or accident (the baseline hazard; hunger's part is the body's, never luck's) is scaled by `1 − share` for a blessing and by `1 / (1 − share)` for a curse, and their chance of finding a technique out the other way about, at most 1; the share is a little (0.1), a quarter or a half (0.05 to 0.5 accepted, so a chance is at most halved or doubled). The draw itself is unchanged and nothing else is drawn, so what it turned is exact: the same draw against the chance without it. Each death it spared or brought, and each find it brought or cost, is counted on its record and told in the chronicle ("One recorded influence: Arrel was spared a death by illness or accident: the observer's blessing turned the draw.").
