It never touches a decision's draw, a vote, a tie, detection or punishment, nor food, harvests or buildings: those draws are not the person's own (a building's quality is drawn for the building, and spoilage and making are not drawn at all). A blessing of one already blessed refreshes it (its period at least the new one from today) and never stacks; a curse ends a blessing that day, and the other way about. A child may be blessed; the whisper and the ideology reach adults only. **Tests:** an agitator sent to the hearth joins the village alone, an adult with no partner or union, holding the ideology from no one and knowing nobody, logged once and not as a family; sent into the water or with no such ideology, refused; it saves and loads exactly. With the constant adult hazard raised by hand (80 a year), half a village's adults blessed and half cursed at half the way for 30 days: within 10 days blessings had spared deaths and curses brought them, every turned draw had its chronicle entry, a share out of range or a period of none was refused, a second blessing refreshed the first, and a blessing given to one cursed ended the curse that day; a schema-48 save loads with no one blessed; the end-to-end test blesses an adult from the inspector and sends an agitator with the map tool. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds** (it uses no god tool). **Boundary:** saves schema 49 (an influence's period, share and what it turned; 48 still loads), wire 1.48 (the commands `SendAgitator` and `Bless`), no content API change. **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** a blessing of a household's or a firm's fortunes (harvests, spoilage, a building's quality: draws that are not a person's own, or not drawn), and an agitator with an organizer's disposition (the M4 factions brief's proposal; ADR-0016 §5 makes them ordinary).
- **The regimes row and the M4 demo, as built (2026-10-09, M4c slice AJ step three):** no new ADR. **The regimes row** (plan §4.7: "two seeds differ"; the M4 observer brief: "differ" only where the label's reasons name a structural difference, the classifier frozen before the first run, no reference rate of divergence): each world's polity is graded by its label's principal name without the person it names ("Council community", "Oligarchy — big-man leadership"), which says who may decide and who leads, with the first of its reasons; green when two worlds end under different names, amber when they do not ("reported and not forced"), grey with fewer than two worlds labelled. The row also shows each tenth year's names where they changed. The classifier (ADR-0013 §6, M4a slice Z) was not touched for it, and it does not read the property regime, which the worlds take in turn. **The M4 demo** (`web/e2e/m4-demo.spec.ts`, run with `TCE_DEMO=1`): the dashboard keeps each world's save at every tenth year's end (`--keep-saves`), each world in a folder of its own, so the folder it kept them in is a saves folder the observer opens; the demo loads each world's save of year 40, shows the Government panel (the custom, what it would be called and why, its laws), opens the oldest decided law of the world whose gathering decided the most to its whole history, and logs them side by side with the number of regimes. A world whose regime changed after year 40, by the dashboard's report, is shown again at year 50 with the newest law its body decided opened: a coda past the demo's forty years, labelled so. Without kept saves, the demo runs the dashboard for forty years first. **A display fault, found by the demo and fixed:** the Government panel told every decision's tally with the members of the body now ("34 of 40 elders came" for an amendment the adults decided before it made the elders the body); a decision is now told under the version of the custom in force before it (`Polity::body_at`), so that one reads "34 of 40 adults came". **Tests:** the row is amber for one regime, green for two with the decade it changed, grey for one world; a decision is told under the body that decided it, the amendment that changed the body under the one before. **The run and what it showed:** the next entry.
- **The M4 demo and the dashboard after M4c (2026-10-09):** `civ-host dashboard --keep-saves`, the five river-valley worlds (seeds 1 to 5, 1024 cells, household fields and village fields in turn) lived fifty years on four cores in 2,237 s, every world's yearly checks passing; 77, 82, 79, 50 and 78 people at the end. **At forty years** (the demo, from the kept saves): all five were council communities under the founding custom, each with a common store at a twentieth and a keeper (the office passing from holder to holder by new laws in three), the levy 86–91 % paid, no relief ever given, no faction, petition, refusal, revolt or coup, and nothing decided in the last two years: one regime, which is what the demo reports. Thorndale (world 1) had decided the most; its oldest decision, opened: Davor proposed a common store at a fifth on 31 July of year 3 "because food would not last until the harvest", and it was turned down two days later, 1 for and 13 against, 19 of 20 adults coming, most against because their household stood to lose though they thought well of the sponsor, talk at the hearth having drawn many of them toward it. **By fifty years one world parted:** Hazelstead (world 4) ran short of food in its fiftieth year; its store gave 12 t of relief in 8,018 asks and fell to 557 kg; 11 died and households left (82 people to 50). In its last two years its gathering decided ten matters, all in the fiftieth: a law against taking, two laws naming keepers of the watch, a curfew; Talia's faction (39 members, against Ferrin as keeper of the store) petitioned for Marek in Ferrin's place, which the gathering granted (35 came); and Marek proposed that the households' elders decide, "because the gathering had decided against them", carried 33 to 0 with 34 of 40 adults coming. Under elders, 19 of its 41 adults may decide: an oligarchy by the classifier's 50 % line, with big-man leadership, Delia having put forward 4 of the 6 laws that passed and all 130 who backed them doing so more for their regard of Delia than for their households. So the regimes row is green, two regimes; the other four stayed council communities. Divergence came late, in one world of five and from a famine, not by year 40; that is what happened. **Rows:** population green (5 of 5 keep 10; the most growth 2.1 times the founders), firm sizes green, structural failures green (1.12 per 1,000 building-years), the Gini of goods amber (0.26–0.41), crime grey (21 attempts, 30 needed), settlement sizes and epidemics grey, regimes green, and **food prices red**: in Hazelstead grain was asked only 1 log point more before the harvest than after, over 40 harvests, against the row's 2 (the other worlds 5 to 8; earlier dashboards 2 to 9; the research's markets 17 to 61). **The red row, probed (time-boxed, not rerun: research 16-03 §4.7):** the gap is a mean over all 40 harvests, and to year 40 Hazelstead's institutions matched the others' (the same custom, store, share and keeper, no relief given), so nothing found ties it to M4c; the row's threshold is a tuning value and every world's gap sits far below the research's range, as it has since slice L. Not tuned toward a pass. **Not built:** a regimes row that weighs the restricted council (50–90 % admitted) apart from the broad one, which the frozen classifier calls one name with a modifier.
- **M5 design (2026-10-09, plan §7):** from four briefs (`docs/briefs/m5-settlements.md`, `m5-diffusion.md`, `m5-trade.md`, `m5-relations.md`), their key citations checked against the reports: 10-01 §5.5 (entity update order must not decide who gets the best land) and its founding buffer (the time to dependable production plus 1–3 months); 05-06 §1.1 (an off-map reservoir must be explicit) and its table of priors (a review every 6 months, 12 destinations, about 2 completed moves per 100 residents a year as an output benchmark, not a quota); 10-02's 0.05–0.20 reconsiderations per household-year ("reconsideration is not migration"); 01-03's one authoritative model; 09-16 §1.2 (a message "is stationary until departure"); 13-01 §1.4 (a council's approval and a ruler's oath "need not be interchangeable"); 08-05 §1.7 (the no-arbitrage band and its 100/130/25 example); 11-02 §2.3 (39 % of the stock after fifty years); 07-02 §1.2 (buying a steel tool does not teach steelmaking). **M5 is split in three** (§7) because its contents are three kinds of work, each with a demo of its own: M5a, several settlements (identities, residence, movement and scale); M5b, trade and diffusion (the demo's prices and roof); M5c, relations and works (treaties, tribute, bridges and enclosures; the demo's failed ratification). **One ADR now,** [ADR-0018](decisions/0018-settlements-residence-movement.md); M5b and M5c are designed when reached, from the same briefs, with one ADR each expected. **The open questions, answered:**
  - **Every daughter founds its own polity** (one to one, ADR-0013 §1). A polity over several settlements waits for M6's conquest, federation and secession.
  - **Leaving the map** keeps its M4a meaning and prior; the off-map account keeps the books, and nobody returns from it.
  - **Reviews:** yearly on a keyed day and at events, and daily when out of food. 05-06's six months and 10-02's 0.05–0.20 a household-year disagree; once a year lies between them (a design prior).
  - **Roads:** M5 builds only crossings. A worn trail already gives full walking speed, so a kept way changes nothing people notice until carts (M8).
  - **World size:** the new-world dialog offers no worlds of thousands until the scale budget is met; benchmarks build them.
  - **Setup groups:** each a band of its own, with its own way of building (so a roof style has somewhere to cross from) and kin only within it. Whether they know one another's sites is a setup choice.
  - **Budget:** a year of 3,000 people in three settlements in at most ten minutes at Max (a design prior); 8,000 measured, not targeted.
  - **Settlement sizes** are reported, not graded, until M9 (10-01 §4 and 10-02 §5 warn against fitting distributions to a few towns).
  - **Climate** stays one series a world: neighbours a few kilometres apart share their weather and lean years, which is also why trade between them may buffer little (08-12 §4).
  - **An exile** goes where they choose, as any leaver does; nothing keeps them out, since no polity yet decides who settles.
  - **The wave:** 5–50 households of one band, walking in from the nearest edge and knowing the settlements near where they are sent.
  - **Marriage between settlements is in** (the diffusion brief expects it to be the commonest carrier at village sizes), the couple placed by land and room, never by sex. **Visits are a behaviour of their own** (company at a known settlement's hearth), besides trade's trips.
  - **Cultures stay derived;** no conformity term is added; esteem replaces goods in admiration only for buildings elsewhere (M5b), so M3b's behaviour at home is unchanged.
  - **Metal:** M5 adds none, so per-polity currencies are not built in M5 (§7).
  - The trade brief's remaining questions (the procurement anchor, scarcity in grain asks, goods in transit, the food-prices row, the demo's twin) and the relations brief's eleven wait for M5b's and M5c's designs.

  **The hearth was everyone's (fixed before the design, `98c9b52`):** `hearth_company` gathered everyone whose target was "the hearth", whichever settlement's hearth they sat at, so with two settlements ties, word, opinions, norms and ideologies passed between people kilometres apart. The diffusion brief found it; a test showed it (a camp of ten families 1,500 m from the village, lived twenty days: hearth ties across the two before the fix, none after); company is now kept with one's own settlement. Worlds of one settlement are unchanged. **Scale, measured (2026-10-09, `bench_village`, seed 2 on 768 cells, on the development container):** 1,000 people lived their first thirty days at 691 ms a day (252 s a year at that rate; slice X's whole year, with its harvest weeks, was 485 s); 3,000 in one village lived their first ten days at 4,105 ms a day, 5.2 times the 1,000's first ten days (785 ms) for three times the people. Three settlements of 1,000 should cost about three times one if searches stay within each (ADR-0018 §7): about 24 minutes a year at slice X's rate, so slice AL needs a further factor of about 2.4. **What would be plot, and is not built:** no settlement size, rate of moves or founding is a target; no timer splits a village or sends a wave; nobody's destination is chosen for them.
- **Several settlements at setup, as built (2026-10-09, M5a slice AK, ADR-0018 §1–§3, §6):** **Founding groups:** `NewWorld.neighbours` (wire 1.49) gives up to two further groups, each a band of its own keyed by its settlement, so each brings its own way of building. One pool of `camp_candidates` × groups sites is drawn and each site scored for each group's size; one draw over the summed scores of the assignments whose hearths lie at least two field reaches apart (about 3 km, so no field counts for two groups) chooses them all, groups taken largest first, so the order they are listed in cannot choose their land (tested by listing them both ways). A group that finds no room is left out, the last listed first, and the new world says so. One group draws exactly as before: a save made by the previous build, lived twenty days by both, differs only in the sections whose layout changed (settlements, history, receipts), its time use and ties the same. **Records:** a settlement keeps its parent, how it was founded (`civ_land::Founding`: setup, sent, wave, coalition) and when its last resident died or left, noted at the day's end; older saves read the first settlement as the setup band's and any later one as sent. The hearth target names its settlement, and company at a hearth is whoever is there; a hearth saved without one is the person's own. Everyone's residence history (`PersonRecord.residence`: settlement or off the map, since, and why) is written at founding, birth, arrival, leaving, exile, marriage into a household elsewhere and kin taking in children; older saves infer it from where people live, the chronicle and how they came. **Accounts** are derived from those histories (`Population::accounts`), never kept beside them: `problems()` checks that every living person's last stay is their household's settlement and that everyone who left went off the map, and the smoke and the dashboard check each year that every settlement's births − deaths + arrivals − departures is its change in residents. **Isolation:** two settlements founded together and lived two years with no contact share no tie, marriage, heard claim, creed, technique learnt, taking, faction or admired building, while within each people come to know one another; a two-settlement world saved at a midnight or cut at odd minutes ends byte-identical (Gate A). **The dashboard** founds two groups a world (`--groups`, 1 for the dashboard of M3c to M4), grades a population-accounting row and reports settlement sizes without grading them (10-01 §4; 10-02 §5). **The observer:** the new-world dialog's "Neighbouring groups", each settlement's way of founding and its past year's accounts, an abandoned settlement marked, and the inspector's "Where they live". Saves schema 50, wire 1.49. **Not built here:** whether the groups know one another's sites at the start, which needs known places (slice AM).
- **Scale for several settlements, as built (2026-10-09, M5a slice AL, ADR-0018 §7):** **Measured first** (`bench_village`, seed 2 on 1,024 cells, at Max, on the development container): from the same saved day early in the year, three settlements of 1,000 cost 3.25 times one; over a whole year (after the first step below) 4.95 times, 1,550 s against 313 s, the ratio climbing from 3.7 to 6.8 as the world's fields piled up: work that grew with the whole world, not with each settlement. **What grew with the world is now kept to each household's own ground, exactly** (a save lived on by the build before and the build after gives the same digests, at days 10, 30 and 245): finding ground for a field checked every field, plot, earthwork and home in the world, and its daily cache was keyed on all of them, so any settlement's new field sent every household looking again (12.8 times one settlement's work); it now checks only what lies near its candidates, its cache is keyed on the household's own ground, and the cache is stamped with counts that say whether anything it read could have changed (fields, plots and earthworks are only ever added; moves of homes are counted), so a hit costs nothing. Company at the hearth is looked up in each settlement's list of those who set to it, not by scanning everyone. A household's faded places are let go once a day, not on every trip a neighbour makes. Ten places that found a household's fields by scanning every field in the world read an index kept by household, brought up to date at midnight and emptied whenever a field changes hands. Each keeps a check in debug builds, on in every test run, that it agrees with the scan it replaced; it found one gap, a test that edits a household by hand, which no count sees, so editing people or land by hand now forgets these caches. **Route searches** after each monthly survey were then 46 % of a day's work. **Landmark bounds** (research 01-08 §2A, "ALT") now bound them: eight landmarks round the ground people walked at the survey (the wear tiles' box and half a tile beyond), the fastest times from and to each, built on four threads at the first search after the survey (about 1 s on a 1,024-cell map with three settlements) and kept until the next; each landmark's search stops once every walkable cell of that ground has its time, or after three crossings' walking time, a cell not reached keeping a lower bound. On 1,500–3,000 pairs near homes the bounded searches expand about 1,900 cells against 12,000–16,600, and give the same fastest time for every pair (and in a test with hills, lakes, an island and bounds cut short); which of two equally fast routes is taken can change, a one-time change of digests, as slice X's was. The A* search is compiled for its score table and trail, so their look-ups are inlined. **Result:** a year of 3,000 people in three settlements takes 1,179 s (19.6 minutes; days 1–60 ran at 3,976 ms a day before the slice and 2,396 after) and one settlement of 1,000 takes 273 s, so three cost 4.3 times one, still more as the year's fields pile up (3.9 times in the first month, 5.7 in the ninth). **The budget, ten minutes (a design prior), is not met**, and is recorded as slice X's was: what is left is spread over every person's decisions (choosing among activities, deals, own costs, route searches after each survey) and grows with population more than with the number of settlements. **8,000 people** (three groups of 2,667 on 2,048 cells, the default map) lived their first 30 days at 14.6 s a day, about 89 minutes a year at that rate: measured, not targeted. The ten smoke worlds pass. **Not built:** a route cache sized by population (emptied at each monthly survey, it never filled its 50,000 entries at 3,000 people); reverse travel fields (no destination drew enough repeated searches to pay for one); parallel preparation (01-03 §4.4): the world keeps one order of events, and running settlements side by side needs a design of its own, which slice AM's visits between settlements would also have to fit.
- **Known places, as built (2026-10-09, M5a slice AM, step one, ADR-0018 §4, §6):** each household keeps the other settlements it knows (`civ_agents::places`): the settlement, how it first came to be known (founded alongside, seen on a walk, kin there, visited, told), who told of it, the first and last day it was known, and what a member last saw of its food (empty until visits, step two). A household's own settlement is never among them, and a place known again is only known as of the later day. **Seen:** a walk passing within `sight_m` (500 m, a design prior; no research gives a distance at which a village is seen) of a settlement's hearth makes it known to the walker's household; the check runs once per walk, and only where there are several settlements. **Told:** at the hearth someone from another settlement always says where they are from, and each companion tells the other of a place their household knows and the other's does not by `share_told` (0.15, inside 09-16 §2.2's 0.05–0.25 for routine news), keyed by teller, listener, place and minute so it touches no other draw (13-01 §1.1: awareness comes by contact). **Founded alongside:** the new-world choice ADR-0018 §6 names (`NewWorld.neighbours_known`, the dialog's "The groups know where the others camped"). Households that merge or take people in bring what those people knew, except where they now live; a household that ends forgets. Saves schema 51 (`places`; an older save knows no place), content API 52 (`[places]`), wire 1.50 (`NewWorld.neighbours_known`, `PersonInfo.places` in the kernel's words). **Nothing acts on what a household knows yet**, so no choice has changed: a saved world of three settlements of 1,000, lived five days by the build before and the build after, gives the same digests in every section but the content (its new values) and the new places section, which stays empty there (no walk in those days passed within sight of another settlement, the hearths lying 3 km and more apart). Not built here: visits (step two) and marriage between settlements (step three).
- **Visits, as built (2026-10-09, M5a slice AM, step two, ADR-0018 §2, §4):** **The behaviour** `visit` (`core:activity/visit`): an adult of 14 or more walks to the hearth of a settlement their household knows and that is lived in, keeps company there for a session (90 minutes) and walks home. It is offered only within `max_walk_minutes` (120, a design prior: a day's range there and back at 09-16 §2.1's foot speeds) of their own hearth by the settlement's travel field, so it costs no new route search, and only while daylight lasts the walk there. **Its worth:** the company any hearth gives (the loneliness term), plus `w_kin` (6) for each parent, child, brother or sister living there, `w_ties` (4) × s/(1+s) for those they know there, and `w_seek` (6) for an unpartnered adult who looked for a partner at home within `seek_days` (180) and found nobody; all of it grows back over `revisit_days` (30) after a member of the household was there; against the walk there and back at `decision.w_walk_hour`. All are design priors; no study gives a rate of visiting between neighbouring villages. **Why a failed search is a reason:** without it nobody would go where they know nobody, and settlements founded apart would stay sealed until trade or moves; research names partners met beyond one's settlement as the bridge (04-08 §1.1: the candidate pool comes from actual exposure, neighbouring settlements among it; 04-04 §3.1). A failed search is recorded when the daily partner search finds nobody at home (`Population::unmatched`, saved) and dropped at partnering. **First values failed:** at 3 points each (set for a 45-minute walk) nobody went, since hearths lie at least about 3 km apart and the walk there and back cost about 4; they were raised to 6, with the damping after a visit added so that kin elsewhere are not visited daily. **What visitors do:** they are company like anyone at that hearth, and ties, claims, grievances heard, opinions, ideologies and known places pass by the existing paths; the laws in force and what a household paid of its last levy are told only between people of one settlement, since both are about one's own polity (talk of a neighbour's laws waits for M5c). A visit never changes residence, household or membership. The visitor's household notes the day and what they saw of the place's food (the share of its people there not drawing on their body's reserve), shown in the inspector's places. **Counts:** visits and the minutes spent, by year and by from- and to-settlement (`Contacts`, with marriages for step three), saved in the places section, shown in the settlements list ("in year 2: 3 visits from Brook, 4 hours at the hearth here") and reported on the dashboard's new grey row, Contacts. **Measured:** two bands of 30 that know each other, a year at Max: 17, 0 and 16 visits on seeds 1, 2 and 3. Three settlements of 1,000 that know each other: 3 visits in 60 days (no kin across, and partners at home in villages that size). Computing visit options took 29 ms a day there (20,000 calls a day, about 1.3 % of the day's work); the whole run was 2,555 ms a day against 2,374 ms for the same worlds not knowing each other, the rest of the difference being their lives diverging. Tested: a woman and her son in different settlements visit, each visit counted, nobody's residence changes and nobody knows a law of another polity; when every unpartnered adult of a band had found nobody at home, some went to the other settlement within 30 days. **A world of one settlement lives as before:** a saved village of 1,000 lived five days by the builds of steps one and two gives the same time use and ties, and the same digests in every section that records what was done (land, fields, buildings, wear, market, wealth, knowledge, polity, word, opinion and the rest); the people and workshop sections differ only in the activity numbers they store, which the new activity shifted, and the receipts and places sections in the visit's reason for being left out and the failed searches now kept. Saves schema 52 (a schema-51 save has no visits or searches on record), wire 1.51 (`SettlementBrief.contacts`), content API 53. **Not built here:** marriage between settlements (step three); staying overnight, which needs hosting.
- **Marriage between settlements, as built (2026-10-09, M5a slice AM, step three, ADR-0018 §2, §5):** **Who may be found:** the daily partner search (each seeker with the content's monthly chance) takes in, besides the unpartnered adults of the seeker's own settlement, those of another settlement the seeker holds a tie with (research 04-08 §1.1: the pool is those one has met; visits and the hearth are how they meet), under the same rules of age and kin. **Where the couple lives:** partners of one settlement settle as before (a world of one settlement lives exactly as the step-two build did: a saved village of 1,000 lived 30 days by both gives the same 48 digests). Partners of two settlements settle with or beside the household with more land worked per member, then the better housed (its best dwelling's stage, roofed ones first), then by a keyed lot, never by who is the woman or the man (ADR-0018 §5; the content's residence rule says whether they join a household or set up their own, not where). One who keeps a household alone still keeps it, as before. **Records:** each marriage across is counted per year from the mover's settlement to the couple's (`Contacts`), the mover's residence history gains the stay, both households come to know the other place as where kin live (so kin visits follow), and the chronicle names where they settled ("… set up a household of their own in Ashford"). A couple's new household takes its way of building halfway between the woman's and the man's households, as before, whichever side it settles beside. **Measured:** over five years, two bands of 30 that knew each other made 78, 0 and 370 visits and 1, 0 and 0 marriages across (seeds 1, 2 and 3; why nobody in seed 2 went was not looked into). Contact is possible, not assured; nothing nudges it. Tested: when the unpartnered of one band are brothers and sisters and hold ties with those of the other, they marry across, each marriage counted, each mover living where the couple settled by marriage, the couple's household knowing the mover's old settlement as where kin live, the accounts balanced and the chronicle naming the place; the land-and-room rule is tested on its own. **Not built here:** marriage payments and families' say (04-08 §1.2–1.3), and moving between settlements other than by marriage (slice AN).
- **Moving between settlements, as built (2026-10-09, M5a slice AN, step one, ADR-0018 §3, §5):** **The review:** each household of a world of several settlements reviews where it lives once a year on a day of its own (keyed by household) and at midnight after it forms; the other events the plan names (an inheritance, land short at the yearly allocation, a refused petition) are not built. It weighs each settlement it knows and that is lived in against staying, in points as its other choices are (`[moving]`, design priors; research 05-06 §5.1 gives the terms and no values): `w_kin` (2) for each close kin of a member living there less each at home outside the household; `w_ties` (2) × s/(1+s) for those its members know there less the same at home; `w_fed` (4) × the share of the place's people a member last saw not going hungry less the share at home now (no impression, no difference); `w_grievance` (3) × the most keenly felt grievance a member holds; against `w_stake` (3) × the harvest its fields here should bring over a year's need, `cost` (2) for a new home and new ground, and the walk once at `decision.w_walk_hour` (straight between hearths at the pace people walk off trails). A place must win `reviews` (2) reviews running before the household moves (10-01 §2.3: 1–3; a year apart here), and a leaning is saved. **In an emergency:** a household out of food that gives up (`departures`, unchanged) goes to the known place whose kin, ties and fed look draw it most, if any does, and beyond the map otherwise; with nothing known for a place, leaving the map keeps its meaning (ADR-0018 §3). **The move:** its people form a household anew beside the other settlement's hearth, carrying all it holds, and build as a sent family does; the household they leave is no more, so its ground falls vacant under the regime's rules, its home stands empty, its workshop closes, and an office its members held lapses by the rule that an office lapses when its holder no longer lives in the settlement (not separately tested). What they knew of other places goes with them, and where they lived becomes a place they know ("lived there"). Each move is a stay in each person's residence history (so the accounts count it as departures and arrivals), a count of people moved per pair of settlements a year (`Contact.moved`, shown in the settlements list), and a chronicle entry ("The household of … (5 people) moved to Ashford."). **Measured:** over five years, two bands of 30 that knew each other moved 2, 0 and 0 people between them (seeds 1, 2, 3; about 0.6 per 100 residents a year in seed 1, inside 05-06 §5.4's 0.5–10), and nobody left the map. Tested: a household whose member's five adult children live in the other settlement leans there at one review and moves at the second, its people, residence histories, places, contacts and chronicle as above, the accounts balanced; when a whole settlement runs out of food at once, the household with kin elsewhere goes there and the rest of its band, kin of those who just went, follow one after another (chain migration, 05-06 §1.2), none leaving the map. A world of one settlement lives exactly as before (a saved village of 1,000 lived 30 days by the builds before and after gives the same digests but for the content and the places section, which now also holds leanings). Saves schema 53, content API 54. **Not built here:** the review at events other than a household forming; carrying limits (they take all they hold, as households leaving the map do); returning; exile's destination and the migration wave (step two); the dashboard's moves row and Gate B (step three).
- **The migration wave, exile's destination and the moves row, as built (2026-10-09, M5a slice AN, step two, ADR-0016 §5, ADR-0018 §1, §3, §4):** **The wave:** the observer picks a point, 5–50 households, 1–7 days and 0–12 months of food (the plan's ranges, design priors; 05-06 §5.4 tests 6–12 months' provisions for an isolated crop-dependent founding). Its households come to the point from the map's edge nearest it, those due on the day it is sent at once and the rest at the midnights of the days after, spread evenly over its days. Their walk from the edge is not lived: they appear where sent, as sent families do (a walk of hours from the edge would need routes beyond what trips plan), and what they passed is what they know. Each household is made as a sent family is (a couple and their children, seed and the tools their work needs) and carries the months of food chosen; all build in the way of the first household's band; and in each run of three households one of each couple is a child of the same parents, who stayed behind and have no record (the inspector lists the brothers and sisters, not the parents), so the households are brothers' and sisters' and their kin count for visits, moving and refuge as any kin do; the households' draws are keyed by the wave's number, so no two families sent to one point at one minute come alike. They join the settlement within 600 m of the point or found one (founded by a wave, ADR-0018 §1), as a sent family does, and later households join the first's settlement while anyone lives there; a household that finds no room on the spiral of homes around the point goes on and never comes. Each household knows the settlements lived in that its straight way in from the edge passed within sight of (`places.sight_m`) and has been told of those within 5 km of the point (`WAVE_KNOWN_M`, a design prior: beyond the 3 km setup keeps groups apart). They hold no ties to one another when they come; they come to know one another as anyone does. Once there they are ordinary people, and nothing chooses where they go next. **Records:** one intervention record (influence kind 5) whose target is the first to come, saved with the wave itself (where it was sent, its edge, its days and food, how many households have come and found room, everyone it brought); the chronicle tells it once ("One recorded influence: the observer sent Ada's household, the first of a wave of 20 households from the west, to Ashford.") and each household's coming as a sent family's; everyone it brought arrives from off the map in the residence histories and the accounts; for each person it brought, the inspector's "observer's hand" says how many of its households came, the food they carried and where its people live now. **Measured (time-boxed, not tuned):** a wave of 20 households (85–102 people) with six months' food, sent to dry ground near a corner of a 768-cell river valley ten days after two bands of 30 settled, in seeds 1, 2 and 3: each founded a settlement of its own, lived on its food until it ran out (its households began leaving 319, 174 and 220 days after it came, in seeds 1, 2 and 3) and was abandoned within the year, nearly everyone leaving the map; in seed 1, 12 of its people went to live in another settlement, 6 of them alive there a year on. With no food the same wave in seed 3 began leaving on its 18th day. The setup settlements were known to them only by word, and word alone draws nobody in an emergency (step one's rule), so they left the map rather than go there. This is the founding of a hundred at once failing its first year, as §9 already records of large founding bands; a wave gets no better ground or food than the observer gives it. **Exile's destination:** an exile goes where a household in an emergency would (step one): to the known place whose kin, ties and fed look draw them most, else beyond the map as before; there they live alone in a household of their own beside its hearth, with nothing but what their household knew of other places, the move counted (`Contact.moved`), in their residence history (exiled) and in the chronicle. Tested: an exile with kin in the other settlement goes there; one drawn nowhere leaves the map. **The moves row:** the dashboard grades moves between settlements per 100 residents a year (residents' years counted at each year's end): those who came to live in one settlement from another with their household, on marrying or taken in by kin; exiles are left out as forced displacement (05-06 §5.4 excludes it), while moves in an emergency cannot yet be told from chosen ones and are counted. Amber outside 0.5–10, never red (05-06 §5.4 offers the range as an output benchmark, not a quota); grey for a world of one settlement; turnover (everyone who came or went, off the map too) is shown beside the net change, since a steady size can hide much of it (05-06 §4). **Fixed with it:** since slice AK the dashboard has founded two groups a world, but its first-month check still expected one band of 40, so every world failed before living a day (found by running the dashboard for this row; the dashboard runs nightly, not on pull requests). It now expects a band for each founding group that settled. **Measured:** a five-year look at the dashboard (`--years 5`, 403 s) passes, 5 rows green and 2 amber: the moves row is amber at 0.0 in all five worlds, with turnover 0.0 and net growth of 3.0–5.0 per 100 a year, because their two groups, founded at least 3 km apart and not knowing where the others camped, never came to know one another in five years (no visit, no marriage, nobody seen on a walk), so nobody had anywhere known to go; the regimes row is amber as before. Whether contact and moves come within the dashboard's fifty years is left to its next full run; nothing nudges them. **Evidence:** a world with no wave lives exactly as before (a saved village of 1,000 lived 30 days and three settlements of 1,000 lived 10 days by the builds before and after give the same digests in every section but the influences, which now also hold waves, and the same time use and ties). Tested: a wave of ten households over three days, refused outside its ranges, brings four households at once and three at each of the next two midnights, founds a settlement of its own far from both camps, carries six months' food, comes as brothers and sisters in runs of three, knows the settlements near where it was sent, saves and loads with households still to come and goes on alike, and is counted as arrivals from off the map in balanced accounts; the host answers the command and the observer sends a wave from the map (end to end). Saves schema 54, wire 1.52. **Not built here:** moves in Gate B (its fixtures are worlds of one settlement; a statistical test of moves needs fixtures of several settlements and their calibration, which the M5a demo's worlds can give); the wave's walk drawn on the map; telling emergency moves from chosen ones in the moves row.
- **Fix: two settlements of one world could share a name (2026-10-09):** a new settlement's name was drawn without looking at the others, so the five-year look at the dashboard after slice AN had two settlements called Willowfield in world 5. A name is now drawn again while it names a settlement on record, abandoned ones too (up to 16 draws, then told apart by a number, "Ashford 2"). A draw that names no other place is the one a world always drew, so only worlds with a clash change (world 5 now has Willowfield and Sedgemere). Tested on its own; the kernel suite and a one-year look at the dashboard pass.
- **Splinter founding, as built (2026-10-09, M5a slice AO, step one, ADR-0018 §1, §5; research 05-06 §1.3, 10-01 §1.5, §2.3, §5.2):** **The plan:** at the yearly review (and after a household forms), in worlds of one settlement too, a household weighs founding a settlement with others beside staying and joining a settlement it knows. Founding's worth is in moving's points (`[founding]`, design priors): `w_stake` × the share of the coalition's yearly need the site's cropland within a field walk would bring at `yield_share` (0.7) of the believed yield (10-01 §2.3: a 10th–30th percentile forecast) less the share its own fields and home's vacant cropland (cropland within a field walk of the hearth less every field there) would bring; `w_grievance` × its most keenly felt grievance; `w_kin` for each close kin of a member among the households going; against moving's `cost` and `cost` here (1) besides, and the walk once. **Where:** up to `candidates` (16) of the patches its people have walked (keyed by household and day), on dry, gentle ground people can walk, two field walks from every settlement lived in (so no field counts for two; the rule that keeps setup groups apart) and within `walk_hours` (2) of home; of those, the one a founding band would score best (food, cropland, water, slope, floods), measured cell by cell. **Who goes:** the household whose plan beats its best other plan organizes a coalition: in up to three passes, each household of its settlement tied to those already going (close kin of theirs, or someone of theirs a member regards) goes if founding with them is worth more to it than its own best plan (10-01 §1.5: coalitions from actual relationships, never a random half). **When:** when the plan has won `moving.reviews` (2) reviews running; it goes only if its households hold food for everyone to their first harvest and `buffer_months` (2) beyond (10-01 §2.3: 1–3), after seed for the ground it needs; the first harvest is this year's if its adults, at `work_h_per_day` (6) each, can break that ground before sowing ends. Otherwise it waits for the next review; a plan that stops winning, or whose organizing household is no more, is given up. **The founding:** the settlement is written (founded by a coalition, its parent the settlement left), named as any new one, and founds its polity at once under a copy of the body its founders lived under (ADR-0018 §1), with no law, store or office (offices are laws here, so none goes with them); each household moves there as a moving household does, its people's residence histories saying "went with others to found it"; households left at home with kin among them know where they went. **Records:** every coalition is saved (organizer, the person it is named for, site, members, reviews, what it lacked, its fate); the chronicle tells its beginning ("The household of Ada began gathering households to found a settlement 3.1 km north of Ashford."), a lack when its time came, its giving up and its founding ("The household of Ada and 2 more households (14 people in all) left Ashford and founded Birchford."); the settlements list shows each coalition gathering or ended in the past year; the dashboard's moves row counts those who went founding. Saves schema 55, wire 1.53, content API 55. **Measured (time-boxed, not tuned):** in a world of one settlement or three, a saved village of 1,000 lived 30 days (and three of 1,000 lived 10) by the builds before and after gives the same digests but for the content and the places section, within 2 % of the speed; reviews draw nothing and change nothing unless a plan wins. Lived on for 800 days, that village of 1,000 gathered 58 coalitions, almost all young households without fields, whose village's cropland was taken, gathering with their parents' households (lineal fission, 10-01 §1.5); none went: each, when its time came, lacked food or seed for its first harvest and two months beyond, and the village then starved and emptied in its third year exactly as it does without this step (the build before lived the same people, day by day). That a village founded as a thousand at once dies in a bad year is §9's old finding; that no coalition could afford to go is the viability rule working. Tested: a household whose member has adult children in other households, with a walked site two field walks out (moving's and founding's costs set to nothing for the test), gathers a coalition, waits when it lacks seed or food at its second review, and founds when it holds enough; the settlement's parent, kind, polity body and empty laws, the residence histories, the chronicle, the settlements list, the accounts and a save and load with a settlement founded are checked. **Not built here:** a faction's "leave together" and the M5a demo (step two); scouting walks, goods carried in stages and a camp before the move (they move at once, as a moving household does, 10-01 §5.2's stages but one); a household's return after a failed first harvest (the yearly review may take it home like any move); compensation or bargaining within a coalition (10-01 §5.3); abandoned sites' cleared fields lowering the cost; exclusivity between coalitions (a household counted in two goes with the first to found).
- **A faction's "leave together", coalitions that can go, and the M5a demo, as built (2026-10-09, M5a slice AO, step two, ADR-0018 §1, §5; research 10-01 §1.5, §2.3, §5.5, 05-06 §5.4):** no new ADR (ADR-0018 §5 already makes founding a coalition's project). **Leaving together:** when the gathering turns a faction's petition down, or decides it too thinly heard (any outcome but passed), its organizer's household reviews where it belongs that same midnight (petitions are answered before the reviews are made), beside its yearly review: negotiating has failed, so leaving is weighed (10-01 §1.5: households compare remaining, negotiating, joining another settlement and founding a daughter one, and "institutional adaptation and fission should be competing possibilities"). A household planning to found counts as tied to it, beside close kin and those a member regards, every household with a member in a faction that one of its own people organizes (10-01 §1.5: coalitions "from actual relationships", here a grievance organized together). Each such household still goes only if founding with them is worth more to it than its own best plan, so nobody is carried along; nothing else of the faction changes (it is not dissolved, and members left behind keep it). **Coalitions that can go:** a household is taken on only if the coalition with it would still hold food and seed enough to go (10-01 §2.3's rule, applied as the coalition forms and not only when its time comes, the households' stores pooled as before): one that would leave it short is not asked, and one whose surplus carries the rest may join an organizer short alone. The tied are asked closest first (close kin of those going, then fellow members of a faction one of them organizes, then those regarded), so where not all can go, who goes is not decided by the order households were made in (10-01 §5.5). A household counted among those going with a coalition another household organizes goes with that one: it weighs no plan of its own to found and is not asked to another. **Why, found by the demo:** its first run, built before the last two rules, gathered 45 coalitions in thirty years, 22 of them in one settlement in its last year, each counting mostly the same households; three founded settlements (of 4, 26 and 30 people, two abandoned within ten years). A second run, with only the one-coalition rule, gathered one coalition of 15 households (56 people) that won fifteen reviews running, lacked seed every time and gave up in year 30, holding every one of those households from any other plan for fifteen years. Both came from asking everyone tied whatever they held. (Runs of one seed differ, each world drawing its own identity, so the three runs are three histories, not one history under three rules.) **The M5a demo:** `civ-host neighbours` makes a world of three founding groups of the content's default size (40), knowing where the others camped (the new-world choice), on the dashboard's 1024-cell river valley under the content's default regime, and lives it thirty years after its first month, a day at a time at Max as the dashboard's worlds do. At the end of year 10 the observer sends a wave of 10 households over 3 days with 12 months' food to the dry, gentle ground nearest the middle of the settlements that is 1,500 m from every hearth (beyond the 600 m within which a wave joins a settlement); these are the observer's choices, stated in the report. Every year's end makes the long run's checks (each settlement's accounts balance, the books, residence histories agree with the world) and reports each settlement's accounts, the visits, marriages and moves between each pair, every move with its reason, the coalitions begun and ended and where the wave's people live; the end lists every coalition from its first review to its fate and says so if none founded a settlement. `--keep-saves DIR` keeps the saves of years 10, 20 and 30, and `web/e2e/m5a-demo.spec.ts` (`TCE_DEMO=1`, `TCE_DEMO_SAVES=DIR`) loads years 10 and 30 in the observer: the settlements list (how each was founded, its year's accounts, contact and coalitions), someone who moved between settlements with their residence history, and someone the wave brought with the observer's hand. **The run (seed 1, 37 minutes, partly beside other work on the machine):** every year's checks passed. Ashford, Oakholt and Sedgebrook settled 3.2–5.4 km apart; 120 founders and the wave's 49 became 231 people. **The wave** came to a point 1.7–3.1 km from the three, founded Alderholt, and its households chose where to go: 26 of its people moved to Sedgebrook in its first year and 20 more in its second, when Alderholt was abandoned 13 months after it was founded (6 died there); nobody left the map. At year 30, 19 of the 49 lived (11 in Oakholt, 8 in Sedgebrook) and 30 had died. **Splinter founding:** Ulmer's household of Oakholt began gathering in year 12 and, its plan having won two reviews, went the next year with 13 households (51 people), founding Fernfield; Oakholt fell from 74 to 24. In year 14 a famine in Sedgebrook, swollen to 79 by the wave, starved 20, and 33 of its people moved to Oakholt, where the founders had left room; Fernfield grew to 74 by year 25, then in year 26 its own famine starved 23 and 24 left, most back to Oakholt; 18 lived there at year 30. The coalition is inspectable from its first review (the chronicle, the settlements list in its year, the residence histories: Kellan "went with others to found it" in year 13 and moved back to Oakholt in year 26) to its fate. **Moves:** between settlements, 179 people moved with their household, 51 went to found one, 21 married into another and 1 was taken in by kin, none exiled: 252 in 5,886 resident-years, 4.3 per 100 a year (3.4 without the founding), within 05-06 §5.4's 0.5–10 benchmark, an output and not a target. Ashford, farthest from the others, exchanged 1,293 visits with them in thirty years and no marriage or move; Oakholt and Sedgebrook exchanged 16,375 visits, 11 marriages and 67 people moving. No NUDGE: a splinter founding came on its own, so the plan's time box did not apply. **Fixed with it:** the settlements list showed an abandoned settlement as short of food (its shortage was last judged while people lived there); it no longer does. **Tests:** a refused petition has its organizer's household gather a coalition that midnight that takes in a household tied to it only by the faction and not one tied by nothing; members of a gathering coalition start none of their own; a household gathers alone while its kin would leave it short, waits when its time comes, and takes them on and founds once they hold enough (step one's test, rebuilt for the rule); the wave's point lies 1,500 m from every hearth and its households found their own settlement there. Each rule's test fails without the rule. **Evidence:** a saved village of 1,000 lived 30 days and three of 1,000 lived 10 days by the builds before and after give identical digests in every section, at the same speed (no founding plan won a review in those spans); the kernel suite, clippy, the smoke, the web suites and the demo's observer spec pass. **Not built here:** scouting walks, goods carried in stages and a camp before the move (10-01 §5.2: they move at once); bargaining or compensation within a coalition (10-01 §5.3), whose go-check pools what its households hold; abandoned sites' cleared fields lowering the cost; Gate B's moves, which the demo's worlds could now calibrate. Step one's measurement of a village of 1,000 (58 coalitions, none went) predates the rule that sizes coalitions to what they hold.
- **M5b design (2026-10-09, plan §7):** from the trade and diffusion briefs (`docs/briefs/m5-trade.md`, `m5-diffusion.md`), their key citations checked against the reports: 08-05 §1.7 (the shipment condition, every cost in one unit, and its no-arbitrage band with the 100/130/25 illustration), §1.5 (a reference price "incorporating procurement, carrying costs and expected scarcity"; production cost not a floor); 08-12 §1.6 ("a merchant should know dated observations, not the current price everywhere"; estimate price and depth, or every merchant answers the same shortage and gluts it); 11-02 §2.2 (α 0.02–0.20 per meaningful encounter, and walking past the same building "must not count as an unlimited series" of encounters); 07-02 §1.2 ("buying a steel tool should not automatically teach steelmaking"). **One ADR,** [ADR-0019](decisions/0019-exchange-between-settlements.md): reach by price reports held per household, goods changing hands at the seller's door, trades tallied where they settle with the buyer's settlement on record, the replacement anchor from reports of other markets only, the monthly convergence record and the demo's twin as a harness. **Four slices** (§7): AP (buying from a neighbour by report), AQ (fetching to resell, the replacement anchor and convergence), AR (diffusion through contact) and AS (the demo). Much of the diffusion brief's groundwork came with M5a: the hearth keyed by settlement, visits, marriage and moves between settlements, residence apart from presence and the contact counts. **The trade brief's open questions, answered:** (1) *distance:* the demo's settlements are a few kilometres apart, as setup places them (3.2–5.4 km in the M5a demo's seed 1), within a day's round trip; nobody stays away overnight. (2) *Metal:* none in M5; per-polity currency waits for minting. (3) *The procurement anchor:* yes, the lesser of own cost and replacement from reports of other markets only, so a world of one settlement is unchanged. (4) *Scarcity in grain asks* (funded attempts to buy food recorded as unmet demand): not in M5b; it changes every world and the food-prices row, and is a model question of its own, recorded here. Grain is a weak test good for convergence anyway (one cost anchor world-wide, one weather series), so the demo rests on goods whose costs differ by place. (5) *Goods in transit:* no holder in M5b; a purchase reaches the buyer's household at the seller's door, as within a settlement, while trips are under a day and nothing on the way can take a load (ADR-0019 §3). (6) *The food-prices row:* unchanged; trade may narrow seasonal gaps for good reasons (08-05 §5 B), which the row would then show, and nothing is tuned to pass it. (7) *Polity moves* (a store buying from a neighbour, a ban on grain leaving): deferred; each is a new law move, and M5c's relations or M7's trade policy are where they belong. (8) *Reports per household*, like known places. (9) *The twin:* a harness switch in memory, never content or a save (ADR-0019 §8). **The diffusion brief's open questions, answered:** (1) *adopting* a continuous trait is the brief's §3.4 definition (new buildings' mean pitch nearer the other's founding way, a followed link that crosses, the contact on record). (2) *Esteem or goods:* esteem only for buildings elsewhere, where a stranger's goods are unknown; home buildings keep M3b's rule, which every world is tuned to. (3) *Marriage:* built in M5a. (4) *Conformity:* none; home exemplars already outnumber foreign ones. (5) *A first visit* meets only buildings finished in the past year, as at home. (6) *Visits:* built in M5a as a behaviour of their own. (7) *Cultures* stay derived. (8) *Collapse news* does not move a settlement's caution (ADR-0009 §6). (9) *A neighbours row:* yes, on the dashboard: nothing crosses without contact, and with contact at least one crossing within fifty years, graded by direction and never by rate.
- **Buying from a neighbour by report, as built (2026-10-09, M5b slice AP, step one, ADR-0019 §1–§4):** **Price reports** (`civ_agents::reports`) are held per household by market, good and payment good, with the price, the seller and its ask in hours, the units, the day and how (seen by a member, or told by whom); of two as new, one seen beats one told. Keyed by payment as well as good, refined in ADR-0019 §1 before the code landed: a seller takes several goods for one, and the first probe's buyer, holding only "sickles for hoes" of a seller who took grain too, never went. `[reports]`: `half_life_days` 30, `max_age_days` 120, `share_told` 0.15 (design priors; 09-16 §2.2 gives routine news 0.05–0.25 an opportunity and no source gives a half-life for a price heard of). **Where they come from:** someone keeping company at another settlement's hearth tells what their household and its open workshops offer; companions pass on what their household holds of markets other than the listener's own, by a keyed draw at `share_told` (a good's terms together), if the listener holds none or an older one, keeping the day it was seen; a buyer at a seller's door notes every offer it sees, and that none is left of what the seller no longer offers, dated (without that, a neighbour at the hearth told the buyer the older report again the same evening, which the stale-trip test found). Reports end with their household, come with people who move or marry into another (except of the market now theirs), and are let go past `max_age_days` or when their market becomes home. **The fetch** (`core:activity/fetch`, behaviour `fetch`, content API 56): the purchase choice over reported offers of sellers still living in the reported settlement, within `max_walk_minutes` (120) each way by the household's walking field (whose reach the visit and the hunt already set), each offer's saving believed by its report's weight (0.5 to the power of its age over the half-life), the walk there and back certain; reached in daylight; `NoReport` when none is worth it. One of a household goes at a time (`AnotherGoes`), as the demo world's first run had two members walk to one seller the same day, the second finding it sold out. At the door the seller's terms as they stand decide, through the ledger as at home (ADR-0006 §3). A trip that buys nothing is counted with why, judged by the goods the household holds reports of and still wants: sold out (none offered, or less than a whole tool), payment (offered only for what it cannot spare), terms (offered on terms that no longer serve it), or no longer needed. The first run counted 21 of 23 misses as "terms" because the seller still offered other goods; judged by what was wanted they were sold out. **Tallies:** the trade records the buyer's settlement (`Trade.from`) and is remembered in the seller's market; `Contact` counts purchases and misses per pair of settlements a year, in the settlements' contact words and `civ-host neighbours` (which also reports the reports held). Saves schema 56 (the reports and their goods dictionary, written only when any are held, so a world without them saves its places as before; purchases and misses; the trade's settlement). **Exact without it:** with the core content less `fetch.toml`, the new kernel lives a village of 1,000 for 30 days and the M5a demo's year-10 world (three settlements that visit) for 90 days with every section's digest as the build before (`40dceb5`), but the content's own and, in the demo world, the places section, which now holds what people heard. With the full content the demo world's time use and ties over those 90 days also match (nobody fetched yet); the sections that differ are those that hold activity indices (`fetch` sorts before `fetch_water`) and the reports. **What happens:** the demo world lived two more years from year 10: 18 purchases between settlements (the 12 the markets still remember were all sickles), 12 trips that bought nothing (10 sold out, 1 payment, 1 no longer needed), and 5,795 reports held by 51 households, about 114 each (some 5 KB a household; ADR-0019 §1's 4 MB at 8,000 people assumed one report a market and good, and is about twice that by this measure). At the content's two points an hour of walking, a tool a household lacks (four points) is worth an hour's walk each way and no more, so the tests weigh the walk at one point an hour between hearths 103 minutes apart, and nothing in the content is tuned. **Tests:** `civ-sim/tests/fetch.rs`: a purchase by report tallied where it settles, word passed on as old as it is, pruning, one of a household at a time, and save and load going on alike; a stale report's trips missed for payment and for sold out, the buyer's beliefs corrected; a visitor hearing offers; a world of one settlement holding none. Each fails when its rule is removed (checked by mutation). Also `reports.rs`'s unit tests and the roundtrip of a schema-55 save. Cost: about 2% a day on the village of 1,000 (855 against 838 ms), within the run-to-run spread. The inspector's reports, the market panel's outside buyers and wire 1.54 are step two.
- **Wire 1.54: price reports and outside buyers in the observer (2026-10-09, M5b slice AP, step two):** `PersonInfo.reports` (the household's reports in the kernel's words, a line a seller and good, its terms in every payment it was reported to take, the units to be had or that none is left, and how and how long ago the newest was seen or told), `TradeInfo.from` (the buyer's settlement when not the market's own; the trade's words name it: "… to Ada's household of Oakholt for …") and `MarketInfo.outsiders` (purchases by people of other settlements last year and this year so far, from the contact counts). The observer shows the reports under "Prices their household has heard of elsewhere" and the outsiders under the market's summary; the contact words (step one) need no wire. `civ-host new` gains `--neighbour N` (repeatable) and `--known`. Tested: the kernel's words in `civ-sim/tests/fetch.rs` (the inspector's line for the buyer's report, the market's outsiders and the trade's settlement, a report of none left), the web decoder, and `web/e2e/reports.spec.ts`, which makes river valley seed 1 with two groups of 30 that know each other, lived 100 days (the first visit comes in the third month in every world identity tried, after which 12 households hold 48 reports), and finds someone whose household holds reports in the inspector. Old observers refuse wire 1.54 hosts as with every minor bump.
- **The replacement anchor, the convergence record and the twin, as built (2026-10-09, M5b slice AQ, step one, ADR-0019 §5, §7, §8; research 08-05 §1.5, 08-12 §4):** **The anchor:** at its weekly review of what it offers, a household that holds reports of other markets prices each good it offers from the lesser of its own cost and its believed replacement cost (the household's own cost when it cannot make the good): for each report of the good with units to be had, the reported price at the household's own cost of the payment good, plus the walk there and back and the fetch activity's trading minutes over a load (`carry_kg`, or the units reported of a tool), the walk estimated from the straight distance at the content's walking pace off the trails (a belief, and the same after a save and load, where the walking fields are rebuilt); each report believed by its weight, what is not believed falling back on own cost; sellers beyond the fetch walk do not count; payments are still valued at own cost; workshops' asks are unchanged. Without the `fetch` activity there is no replacement. **The record** (`civ_agents::convergence`): on each month's first day, per pair of settlements lived in, each good offered in both with the median of its sellers' asks in each (hours a unit) and what it fetched there that month (from the markets' history; -1 if none sold); through the month, per direction, the trips to the other's sellers (those that bought nothing too), their walking hours (twice the walk there, from when the buyer set off) and the units bought by good. Saved (schema 57, in the places section with the reports' goods dictionary; written only when there is any). **The twin:** `Sim::stop_trade_between` (in memory; a host command or a test sets it) leaves a trip to buy elsewhere out of every choice (`Stopped`), and a buyer already on the way buys nothing (the trip is still recorded). **Measured:** with the content less `fetch.toml`, a village of 1,000 for 30 days and the M5a demo's year-10 world for 90 days live every section as the build before (`40dceb5`) but the content's and, in the demo world, the places section (the reports and the record). The demo world lived two more years with trade and as the twin, from the same save: the first months' records match exactly. With trade, 8 purchases (all sickles among those the markets remember) and 9 trips to sellers sold out. The mean of |log(ask there / ask here)| over goods offered in both stayed about 0.1–0.3 in each pair, in both runs: trade narrowed some pairs late (0.18 against 0.25 for the oldest pair in two of the last three months) and widened another (0.31 against 0.22), which is within what the twin moves by itself. At this volume the anchor does not close gaps; fetching to resell (step two) is the mechanism ADR-0019 expects to. Tested: an ask falls toward the replacement cost of a fresh cheap report (two worlds alike but for the report), a month of trade is on record (trips, walking hours, units carried, and on the next month's first day the gaps) and saved, and the twin stops every trip; each fails when its rule is removed (checked by mutation; recording the stopped trip before the harness returns at the door is what makes the test see a harness the choice ignores).
- **Fetching to resell, as built (2026-10-09, M5b slice AQ, step two, ADR-0019 §6; research 08-12 §1.6, 08-05 §1.7):** **The errand** (`civ_agents::reports::Errand`, one a household, saved in schema 58 with the price reports' goods dictionary): planned at the household's weekly review of what it offers and replaced at the next. Of each report of another market with units to be had, of a good it can offer and is not short of itself: what one would fetch at home is what buyers who found none would have given, else what one fetched in the latest month it sold (none, no errand); the units are the least of those reported, those it can pay for out of what it can spare (and carry, for a payment that is not a tool), a load (`carry_kg`, for goods other than tools) and the depth at home: its market's sales over a review (`1 − 0.5^(review_days/memory_days)` of the tally) and the demand nobody met, less what is on offer there, its own included. A unit costs the reported price at its own cost of the payment, valued by how much it wants that good (as a buyer pays), plus the walk there and back (distance at its pace off the trails, as for the anchor) and the trading over the units, with its margin; the errand is the one that keeps the largest share of the home worth with all that paid. **The trip** is a `fetch` option after any purchase for the household's own need, while the errand is fresh and its seller still sells there: worth `w_tools` times the share kept without the walk, times how much of a unit it is (up to one), as making to sell is (reason "others want it"); the walk is charged once, by the scorer. A first version charged the walk in the share and again in the scorer: every errand trip in the demo world then scored below zero and none entered the draw (the two years matched step one exactly); charging it once, as for a purchase for one's own need, is the rule ADR-0019 §2 already applies. **At the door** it buys the good on the terms that cost it least, up to the units planned and what it can pay and carry, while a unit still costs it less with its margin than it expects at home; otherwise the trip is counted as sold out, terms or payment. Either way the errand is done. The goods are offered at its door at its next review, its ask anchored on replacement (step one). **A door trade that fails** (the seller holds less than its terms say, or the buyer less to pay with) is now counted between settlements as sold out (or payment), and the buyer's household holds that none is to be had there; before, such a trip counted nothing and the household went back for the same offer. **Measured:** with the content less `fetch.toml`, a village of 1,000 for 30 days and the M5a demo's year-10 world for 10 days live every section as the build before trade (`40dceb5`) but the content's and, in the demo world, the places section (the price reports heard at hearths, inert without `fetch`); the demo world's every section, places included, is identical to step one's build without `fetch`. The demo world lived two years from its tenth with trade and as the twin: households planned errands (53 plans seen at a day's end by 16 households, mostly querns; 203 by 34 under the twin, where none can run), but only two errand trips were made, one to a seller sold out and one by a household with too little left to pay with, and nothing was bought to resell. With trade, 13 purchases between settlements (11.5 sickles), and 16 trips that bought nothing (15 sold out, 1 payment), 29 trips in all, 64 hours walked. The mean |log(ask there / ask here)| over goods offered in both stayed about 0.1–0.4 per pair with trade and 0.1–0.3 as the twin, its moves within what the twin moves by itself: at this volume trade does not close the gaps. **Why errands seldom run:** errand trips were weighed 11,347 times in the two years, at walks of 31–105 minutes by the walking fields and shares kept of 0.22–0.83. At the content's weights an hour of walking costs 2 points, so the walk there and back costs 2.1–7 points against at most `w_tools` (4) times the share: most errands scored below zero, the best about +0.5, short of what people chose instead. The plan counts the walk in the household's own hours and finds the trip worth it; the scorer weighs an hour's walk at half a tool's worth. Whether goods trips between settlements should weigh the walk as the planning does is a question for slice AS's time-boxed demo, to be logged as a `NUDGE:` if a weight is changed. Running errands through a firm is not built. **Found while measuring:** workshops whose offer is their whole stock post units rounded up past what they hold (offers are kept as `f32`), so the transfer refuses and the offer never shrinks; 1,973 trades failed that way in the two years, all at workshops, all but 11 within a settlement. Fixed as a step of its own, since it changes every world with workshops (next entry). Tested: an errand planned, saved and loaded alike, run (more than one sickle bought at the door, the errand done, the purchase tallied) and the sickles offered at home at the next review; an errand to a seller sold out counted as such, its report then saying none, and no errand there again; none when the home worth does not pay for the sickles, the trading or the walk, or when a neighbour at home already offers as many as are wanted; planned and never run under the twin; a trip to a seller holding less than its terms say counted once and not made again; and the roundtrip of a schema-57 save. The rules of depth, of paying for the walk and of the sold-out report were each checked by mutation.
- **Workshops could not sell their whole stock, fixed (2026-10-09, found while measuring M5b slice AQ):** an offer keeps its units as `f32`, so a workshop offering all it held posted, about half the time, a hair more than it held (about 2e-8 of a tool), and the ledger refused any leg whose giver was short by more than 1e-9. The buyer walked to the door, nothing moved and nothing was counted, and as the offer never shrank, buyers came again: in two years of the M5a demo's year-10 world, 1,973 trades failed so, all at workshops, 11 of them by people of other settlements (every one of which since step two is counted as a trip that bought nothing). The ledger now also lets a giver be short by a millionth of the leg (rounding, never a shortfall) and still moves no more than it holds, so goods stay conserved (a unit test gives a whole stock as its `f32` posting reads it, and refuses a real shortfall). Every world with a workshop that sells its whole stock changes from that sale on. **Measured:** in the same two years, no trade failed at the door, and time spent trading fell from 0.005 to 0.001 hours a person-day (the trips to doors where nothing moved). The ten-year smoke (`smoke --years 10`, ten worlds) passed every world: villages of 19 to 55 people at the end; food stocks rising after the harvests green in 8 worlds and amber in 2; grain asked more before the harvest than after green in all 10; the Gini of goods amber in all 10; workshop sizes green in 7, too few to judge in 3.
- **The price convergence row and the twin command, as built (2026-10-09, M5b slice AQ, step three, first part, ADR-0019 §7, §8; the M5 trade brief's row; research 08-05 §1.7, 08-12 §4, 16-01 §4.1, §4.5):** **The measure** (`civ_host::trade`) reads the convergence record and the purchases between settlements. Per pair, each good offered in both gets: its median monthly gap over the last ten years on record (100 × |ln(ask ÷ ask)|); the same over the twelve months before the pair first traded it; a band of 100 × ln(1 + carrying hours a unit ÷ its median ask) + 5, the carrying hours being the hours walked between the pair over the units carried, all goods together (the record does not split the walk by good); the units carried; and the years it flowed on net from the dearer settlement to the cheaper while the gap exceeded the band. **The grade:** grey below 30 purchases between the pair; amber with no traded good of 24 months' asks on both sides; red for any traded good flowing the wrong way so; green when every traded good is within its band or narrower than before trade; amber otherwise. These are the brief's proposals, tuning values. **The dashboard's row** "Price convergence" takes the worst of the pairs it applies to, grey for a world of one settlement or with no pair traded 30 times, and shows each pair with its trades, trips and walking hours. **`civ-host twin --load SAVE --years N`** (default 2) loads a save twice, sets the twin harness on one (ADR-0019 §8), lives both a day at a time at Max in parallel, and reports each year's purchases, trips, goods carried and each pair's mean gap of asks for both, then judges every pair with the twin's gap beside each good. The long run's checks fail it, as does a red pair. **Measured:** the M5a demo's year-10 save lived two years (about 4 minutes): with trade, 12 purchases between settlements (8, then 4), all sickles (9.7 carried), in 29 trips walking 63 hours; as the twin, none. Every pair stayed grey: the most traded pair (Oakholt–Sedgebrook) had 10 purchases in the two years, against the row's 30. Their sickle gap was 9 points with trade and 12 as the twin, inside a band of 104 (16 the year before they first traded); that is reported, not graded. Every check passed in both lives. Tested: the grading on made-up records (grey for too few trades; green within the band; amber outside it and no closer than before, green when narrowing after trade; red for the wrong way) and the dashboard row taking the worst pair.
- **Trade between settlements in the observer, and caravans, as built (2026-10-09, M5b slice AQ, step three, second part, wire 1.55; ADR-0019 §6, §7):** **The market panel** gains `MarketInfo.between` (id 11): for each other settlement with any month on record with this one, a line from the latest such month, built by the kernel from the convergence record. The line gives each good offered in both, asked here and there in hours (a kg for goods by weight), with how many points apart they stand, and what people of each carried home from the other's market in how many trips: "With Birchhollow last month: a hoe 2.3 hours here and 2.8 there (19 points apart), provisions 0.77 hours a kg here and 0.77 there (0 points apart)". `MarketInfo.on_the_way` (id 12) is who is on the road to buy here today, by the settlement they live in; several of one settlement on that road the same day are "together", the caravan as a view of trips sharing a route and a day (the M5 trade brief: no road danger or pack animal yet gives a convoy any economic reason, so it is never an actor). **The inspector** gains `PersonInfo.errand` (id 54), the household's errand in words ("Their household means to fetch 3.6 sickles from Bran's household at Elmhollow to sell at home; planned today"). The markets revision now also changes with the convergence record and with trips to buy, so the panel follows them. Tested: the kernel's words in the fetch tests (the buyer on the road home after buying, an errand planned, the month's line with its asks and what was carried) and the decoder in the web unit tests; `web/e2e/reports.spec.ts` now also checks that each market of a two-settlement world 100 days old lists the other settlement's asks against its own.
- **Buildings seen elsewhere, as built (2026-10-09, M5b slice AR, step one; the M5 diffusion brief §1.3; research 11-02 §1.1, §2.2, §5.5):** **Seeing.** Someone who keeps company at another settlement's hearth, or stands at a seller's door there on a trip to buy, sees its buildings that are finished, standing and were finished in the year before, within `style.sight_m` (200 m, a design prior) of the hearth or the door, and keeps up to `style.seen_most` of them in mind (8, inside 11-02 §5.5's 5–20 salient exemplars a person, which the report calls a storage choice rather than an empirical figure), newest first, a building seen again kept once. Nobody notes a building of their own settlement. **The review.** At the yearly taste review each household meets, beside its own settlement's new buildings, those its people saw elsewhere that are still among the year's new buildings standing whole, each once however many of its people saw it, all in one order by building. A building at home is admired as in M3b (its owner's standing in goods and its craft rank among its settlement's year). For a building elsewhere the craft half is the same, ranked among its own settlement's year, but the patron half is the esteem the household holds its owner's household in: the highest that any member holds for any member of the owner's, summed over the domains (ADR-0014 §3, a tie's evidence above the prior, so a stranger's is 0), taken as e/(e+1), as the M5b design answered the brief's open question 2 (esteem only for buildings elsewhere, where a stranger's goods are unknown). What was seen is let go at the review. **Readouts.** A building's style words and a household's taste words name the settlement where the followed or admired building stands when it is not their own ("after Bo's hut at Westford"). No wire change. **Boundary:** saves schema 59 (`PlacesSave.seen`, by person; 58 still loads with nothing seen); content API 57; `seen_most = 0` turns it off.
