In a world of one settlement nobody notes anything, and the review meets what it met before in the same order: thirty days of the village of 1,000 match the build before in every saved section but the content. **Tested** (`civ-sim/tests/diffusion.rs`): a stranger's building moves taste exactly by α × prestige(rank 0.5) ÷ 3; a building seen by everyone of a household is met once; esteem for the owner moves taste further, exactly by its e/(e+1); a building finished over a year before the review is not met, and what was seen is let go; a visitor to kin elsewhere notes only finished buildings of the other settlement within sight of its hearth or a door, and a save keeps them; a world of one settlement notes none; the schema-58 roundtrip. **Measured** in the M5a demo's world from its tenth year, two years at Max (`bench_village --digest`, with and without `--no-sights`, which sets `seen_most` to 0): with sights off the run matches the AQ build in every section but the content; with them, 68 people noted 29 buildings of other settlements (511 sightings held at a day's end), and at the end 16 households admire a building elsewhere most. Settlements' mean tastes (pitch in hundredths of a degree, eaves and overhang in cm, as a distance): the two founding settlements in most contact (ids 1 and 51) went from 89.6 apart to 12.4 (89.8 without sights); 51 and the third founding settlement (100) ended 172.5 apart against 265.5 without; 1 and 100 ended 184.8 apart against 175.7. These are small differences, under 2° of pitch, in a world whose groups were founded with near ways of building; the third settlement drifted from both in either run, as its own new buildings moved it (M3b's rule). Whether a roof's way crosses where the founding ways differ, and only with contact, is what step three's neighbours row and the demo (AS) will test.
- **Techniques seen elsewhere, arrivals and losses between settlements, as built (2026-10-09, M5b slice AR, step two; the M5 diffusion brief §1.2; research 07-02 §1.2, §5.4; ADR-0008 §2, §5):** **Awareness by sight.** A person source `Seen(settlement)` (code 5, its id saved where a person's is) joins founder, upbringing, taught, found and observer. Someone of another settlement at a hearth they visit, or at a seller's door on a trip to buy, comes to know of each technique the work they see that settlement's people doing needs, within `knowledge.watch_m` (100 m, a design prior: close enough to watch hands at work) of where they stand: a person at the work step (not walking) of an activity whose technique (its own or its recipe's) or, for building work, whose building's program needs one. A good bought at another settlement's door shows a technique only when every recipe that makes it needs that same technique (dried meat shows drying, a rotary quern its craft; flour, ground at either quern, shows nothing). Seeing gives awareness only, never knowing ("buying a steel tool should not automatically teach steelmaking", 07-02 §1.2); awareness counts toward trying three times over (ADR-0008 §3's reconstruction route), and that is all it does. What someone knew of already is unchanged. **Arrivals and losses.** M5a moved households, spouses and exiles between settlements without the knowledge record following them: nothing was lost where they left or noted where they came. Now a household that moves (or goes to found a settlement), people who join a household of another settlement on marrying, and an exile taken in elsewhere carry it: each technique only they knew where they lived is lost there (ADR-0008 §5, as on a death; an exile's was already checked as they were sent away), and each they know that was not known where they come is noted there as brought by its eldest knower among them, with the settlement they came from (`KnowledgeEvent.elsewhere`; the knowledge panel: "Year 12: Ada brought it from Westford."). A loss also names, when there is one, another settlement where the technique is still known in which someone left behind has close kin or a tie (07-02 §5.4: an accessible practitioner, not only a local one), the one with the most such links and then the most knowers (the panel: "lost with Bo; still known at Westford, where people here have kin or friends."; the chronicle adds "It is still known where some here have kin or friends."). Nothing reads it in M5. **Boundary:** saves schema 60 (`Knowledge.elsewhere`, a list beside the entries, written only when one has another settlement; 59 still loads), content API 58 (`knowledge.watch_m`, 0 to 1,000 m); no wire change, as the words are the kernel's. A world of one settlement has nobody seeing elsewhere and nobody crossing. **Tested** (`civ-sim/tests/diffusion.rs`): a visitor within sight of drying at work knows of it, seen there, and does not know it; one farther off, one of that settlement and one watching someone walking learn nothing; dried meat and a rotary quern bought there show their crafts, flour neither quern's; what was known of already is unchanged; the inspector's words and a save keep it; a household that moves loses what only it knew where it lived, still known where one left behind has a friend, and brings it to where it went, named as from where it lived, in the record, the chronicle and a save; the schema-59 roundtrip. Four mutations of the rules (the distance, the single maker, the settlement brought from, the settlement still known in) each fail a test. **Measured:** thirty days of the village of 1,000 match step one in every section but the content. Two years of the M5a demo's world from its tenth year match step one in every section but the content, the knowledge record and the chronicle: nobody there knows drying or the rotary quern, so there was no work or good to see them by, and every settlement already knew what every mover brought. The difference is the wave's settlement, emptied in year 12 as its last household moved to a neighbour: its eleven techniques are now recorded as lost there, with nobody left behind to have kin elsewhere (before, its record went on saying they were known).
- **Style per settlement and the neighbours row, as built (2026-10-09, M5b slice AR, step three; the M5 diffusion brief §1.7, §3.1, §3.4; research 11-02 §4):** **Founding ways.** Each settlement keeps the way of building it was founded with: a founding band's drawn way (the draw households' tastes are taken around, now split out as `style::band_way`, the draws unchanged), and for a settlement founded by a sent family, a wave or a coalition, the mean taste of the households that founded it (saves schema 61, `PlacesSave.ways`; an older save draws a band's way again from its key, and takes any other settlement's as its households' taste on loading, an approximation). **The three clocks** (11-02 §4: exposure, implementation and the built stock change at different speeds), derived when asked, never stored: the founding way; the households' taste now (mean, and the pitch's spread); the year's new buildings that stand whole; the standing finished buildings; and how many of the year's new buildings follow, through their chain of followed buildings, a building in another settlement. A building's settlement is its household's, or once that household is gone, the one whose hearth is nearest. **The observer** (wire 1.56): `SettlementBrief.style`, the clocks in words in the settlements panel ("Building: founded to build 48° roofs, 1.9 m to the eaves, 0.5 m out; its 12 households would build 47.6° (±1.1°) roofs, …; the year's 3 new buildings: …, 1 after a building elsewhere; its 31 standing buildings: …"); `PersonInfo.seen_away`, the buildings of other settlements someone has seen since their household's review, by settlement; and a building's style words name the first building elsewhere its chain goes back to when the building it followed stands at home ("after Bo's hut, which goes back to Cal's hut at Westford"). **The neighbours row** (`civ-host dashboard`; the M5b design's answer to the brief's open question 9): per pair of settlements and per direction, what crossed (standing buildings whose followed chain reaches the other, households admiring a building there most, techniques brought from there, people knowing of a technique seen there) against the contact that could carry it (visits and trips to buy by the receiving side's people, moves and marriages from the giving side). Red when anything crossed a way no contact could carry it (the hearth leak the brief warns of); grey with no contact either way; amber with contact and nothing crossed; else green. Graded by direction, never by rate. **Tested:** the founding bands' ways are their draws and a save keeps them; the clocks count every household and finished building, and two homes whose chain reaches a home of the other settlement are counted as following one elsewhere; the settlement's words, the building's chain words and the inspector's words; a schema-60 save draws the bands' ways again as they were; the row's grades in a unit test; the settlements end-to-end test checks the panel's words; founding tastes draw exactly as before the band's way was split out (a unit test against the old formula). **Measured:** thirty days of the village of 1,000 and two years of the M5a demo's world match step two in every section but the places, which now hold the founding ways. At the end of the demo's two years the clocks read, for instance: Oakholt founded to build 46° roofs, its households now at 47.7° (±0.5°), its 17 standing buildings at 46.6°; Sedgebrook founded at 48°, its households at 49.5°, the year's 11 new buildings at 49.7°, all 11 after a building elsewhere (the wave's households that moved there from Alderholt brought what they admired). `civ-host twin` now reports the neighbours row for both lives, and red in either fails it: over the same two years nothing crossed without contact in either; Oakholt–Sedgebrook (some 3,000 contacts each way) crossed both ways (buildings, and 15 households of Oakholt admiring one of Sedgebrook most), Sedgebrook–Alderholt toward Sedgebrook, and the pairs with few contacts were amber.
- **Homes for households that come to a hearth together, fixed (2026-10-10, found by M5b slice AS's demo):** thirty years of the demo's worlds failed the long run's roof check in their trade lives (58 of 73 households under a roof in seed 1's year 26, 38 of 56 in seed 9's year 20). **Two causes, one a fault.** (1) A settlement founded or filled within the year is still building: seed 9's Rushford, founded in year 20, had none of its 18 households roofed at that year's end and all 19 a year later. The check now counts only households that began before the last twelve months did (a move, a founding or a new couple draws a new household, so its id says when); one stuck longer still fails it. (2) **The fault:** a household that comes to a hearth (a coalition founding, a move, an exile) was set 20 m from it at an angle of one degree per household id, and a coalition or a wave draws its ids in a row, so Oakfield's 26 founding households were set within 26° of each other, a few metres apart; each looked for clear ground only within 30 m of where it was set, and four of them never built in nine years (24–26 plots within 36 m of each). The same happened to 13 households that moved back to Rushmere when Oakfield emptied in year 26. Now each is set the golden angle (π(3 − √5), about 137.5°) on from the last, which spreads any number evenly about the hearth, and a household with no home that finds no clear ground within 30 m looks out to 120 m (`FIRST_HOME_SHIFT_M`, a tuning value: a short walk, within its fields' reach) and builds at the settlement's edge. From seed 1's year-20 save, Oakfield went from 22 of 26 households roofed to 26 of 26 within the year. A world of one settlement is unchanged (nobody comes to a hearth there, and nobody failed the 30 m search): thirty days of the village of 1,000 match the build before in every section. Tested (`tests/settlements.rs`): five households moved together are set more than 8 m apart, and with every clear spot within 50 m of the hearth taken, each begins a home more than 30 m from where it was set; each half of the fix, undone, fails the test.
- **The M5b demo, as run (2026-10-10, M5b slice AS; the M5 diffusion brief §3.3–3.4; ADR-0019 §7–8):** **The worlds.** Ten river-valley worlds were made with a band of 100 and a neighbour of 40; the brief has the demo pick its worlds by the founding draw, visible in year 0, and six had founding ways more than 3° apart. Two were lived: seed 1 (Rushmere 100 at 47.7°, Alderwick 40 at 51.8°, hearths 4.5 km apart) and seed 9 (Willowford 100 at 52.0°, Willowholt 40 at 46.8°, 3.1 km), each thirty years at Max with trade and as the twin (`civ-host twin --years 30`). A life is deterministic from its save, so each was lived once. **Found by the runs and fixed first** (entries above and below): someone who died between seeing a building elsewhere and the review left their sights behind, which failed the year-end save's load; the neighbours row graded red a way of building carried into a splinter by households that came from a settlement in contact with the giver (now counted as a route through a third settlement); households that came to a hearth together never built (the previous entry); the roof check counted households in their first year; and the twin named the twin life's settlements from the other life. After the fixes every check passed in both lives of both worlds. **The measure** (the brief's §3.3–3.4, in `civ-host twin`): only between settlements founded at least 3° apart (`MIN_APART_DEG`; below that a change hides in households' own spread, the brief's arithmetic), each year's share of a settlement's new buildings nearer the other's founding way than its own, the year the brief's definition first held (the mean pitch of its new buildings nearer the other's way, a followed link crossing into the other, and contact on record), and T10→90 read off the record in years of at least three new buildings (`MIN_NEW`, a tuning value), not fitted: the few new buildings a year make a logistic fit meaningless. **Roofs.** Seed 9: Willowholt took Willowford's way in year 15 with trade and 18 as the twin; none of its new buildings stood nearer Willowford's way in years 1–13, then nearly all from year 15 (3 of 4 in year 21, 3 of 3 in year 25): T10→90 from year 21 to 25 with trade, 21 to 21 as the twin. Willowford met the definition for Willowholt's way in year 4 in both lives on a single building (1 of 1 that year; 0 of 20 in year 1, 1 of 6 in year 6, none after): the definition's first clause reads a year's mean, so one building can satisfy it, and the shares say it was no shift. Seed 1: Alderwick took Rushmere's way in year 14 in both lives; thereafter a third to all of its one to three new buildings a year stood nearer Rushmere's way (as the twin a tenth from year 19, nine tenths not reached); Rushmere never took Alderwick's. In both worlds the way passed from the larger settlement to the smaller, the asymmetry the brief expected (§1.3), and in both lives: the contact that carried it was visits, marriages and moves (thousands of contacts a pair against a few dozen trips to buy), so stopping trade changed little. Splinters founded from the larger settlement share its way, so the smaller one's buildings also stand nearer theirs; only a followed link that crosses names whose way was taken. **Trade.** It emerged, thin. Seed 1: 20 purchases between settlements in thirty years, all between Rushmere and Wrenmere (a splinter founded in year 13), none between the founding settlements; one errand trip, which bought. Seed 9: 70 purchases (at most 10 in a year); 79 errand trips, 24 of which bought. No pair reached the row's 30 purchases, so the price convergence row stayed grey in both. Of seed 9's founding pair (17 purchases, 77 trips, 152 hours walked), the sickle, the good most carried (14.7), stood 21 points apart against 23 for the twin and 42 in the year before the pair first traded it, within its band of 122; realised prices stood closer than asks (sickles 17 points apart, hoes 14). Seed 1's founding settlements carried nothing; their asks stood 4–53 points apart (sickles 53, hoes 36) as the twin's did. **Why prices did not close.** A trip is chosen against everything else a person could do: a tool bought for one's own need is worth at most a tool's worth (`w_tools`, 4 points), an errand that worth times the share of its home price it keeps, while the walk costs 2 points an hour (slice AQ, step two). Between settlements 3–5 km apart, 40–100 minutes' walk each way, only a tool much needed, or an errand that keeps most of its home price, beats the walk; one person carries one load, and villages of 40–100 people buy a few tools a year. The gaps between the founding settlements' asks (20–50 points) lie inside what carrying one unit there and back would cost (a 5-hour tool and a 3-hour round trip: 100 ln(1 + 3/5) ≈ 47 points), so the prices staying apart is what 08-05 §1.7's shipment condition predicts for single units carried on foot. The record cannot measure that band while nothing is carried; the twin now shows it as "-" then. **No `NUDGE:`.** The slice's time box calls for one if trade or the roof's adoption has not emerged; both did. What did not happen, prices closing to a band, is reported, as the demo's own words ask. The question slice AQ left, whether goods trips should weigh the walk as the errand's planning does, is answered no: the scorer weighs every trip's walk alike, and an errand as making to sell is; raising one to thicken trade would tune toward the row. What would carry more belongs to later work: a periodic market day at a hearth (ADR-0019's alternatives), carrying for others, errands through a firm, and pack animals or carts (M8). **Found, not investigated:** seed 9's founding pair has one year in which axes went from the dearer settlement to the cheaper beyond the band, on less than one axe carried; the row would grade that red once the pair reached 30 purchases. The rule weighs a year's net flow against mean asks without asking whether the cheaper market had any on offer; to be looked at when a pair first reaches 30 purchases. **Readouts added:** the twin compares a pair with the twin's only where both lives hold the same two places, prints realised prices beside the asks (the dashboard's row too) and reads roofs by the measure above.
- **M5c design (2026-10-10, plan §7):** from the M5 relations brief (`docs/briefs/m5-relations.md`) and the settlements brief §1.6–1.7, their key citations checked against the reports: 13-01 §1.2 (an envoy "may be authorized to discuss everything but conclude nothing"), §1.4 ("a council's approval, a ruler's oath, constituent communities' assent, and a legislature's consent need not be interchangeable"), §1.5 (update trust "only after a relevant opportunity"; "ten peaceful years ... are not ten successful alliance tests"), §3.3 (8–32 packages per bargaining decision, an engineering choice) and §4 (for early farming villages, seasonal access, compensation, kin-mediated negotiation and joint ceremonies are hypotheses only); 13-02 §2.1 (burden as T/Y and T/(Y − subsistence): 10 paid from 100 with 80 needed is 10 % of output but 50 % of surplus) and §2.3 (a ceremonial relation has "no default GDP percentage"); 11-11 §1.1 (Amnya's banks, ditches and palisades among hunter-gatherers about 6000 BCE), §2.2 (3–4 people per continuously kept post) and §2.3 (about 500 posts for 100 m of close-set palisade). **One ADR,** [ADR-0020](decisions/0020-relations-between-polities.md); crossings amend ADR-0004 and ADR-0009 and enclosures ADR-0009 and ADR-0010 in their slices. **Five slices** (§7): AT (knowing another polity), AU (agreements), AV (performance), AW (crossings), AX (enclosures and the demo). **Checked in the code first:** takings stay within the taker's settlement (`population/crime.rs`), and nothing bars outsiders from a market, so the brief's *takings heard* and *leave to trade* clauses would grant nothing: the clause kind starts with a gift, leave to use a claimed place and a recurring transfer, and the others are appended when there is something for them to grant. Small rivers are waded slowly and big ones cannot be crossed on foot (`civ-world` nav), so a bridge saves wading time or opens a route. **The relations brief's open questions, answered:** (1) *relation states* are labels from a pure classifier that nothing reads, as regime labels are. (2) *Who negotiates:* anyone holding an issue a clause answers who knows someone there; nobody can bind, so the gathering's ratification is the authorization (13-01 §1.2), and an amendment may give an office the power to make agreements. (3) *Transfers* are paid only from a common store; a polity without one cannot perform one, and its negotiator's forecast sees that. (4) *An offer reaches the other gathering* only through a sponsor among its members: the counterpart negotiator sponsors it, so it can die without a vote (09-05 §1.2). (5) *Claims over wild ground* are in scope, as a *claim a place* policy, or access clauses grant nothing and disputes over resources cannot arise. (6) *Takings between villages* are not in M5c: walking to another village's stores to take is a raid, which waits for force between polities (M6); the harms that cross are use of a claimed place without leave and breaches of terms. (7) *Enclosures* are the polity's works, raised by law; a household's yard fence waits until something gives a household a reason (outsiders' takings), and a gate is kept by the watch, not a new office. (8) *Alarm* at a neighbour's works is wholly M6's. (9) *Emblems* wait for M6's war bands; each village's way of building (slice AR) already marks it, and the observer shows it. (10) *Splinter grievances* carry over unchanged: a grievance whose blamed party is the body its holder left now blames a foreign body, which is what it is. (11) *Agreements after a revolt* bind the polity, so a new body or custom inherits them and may repeal them like any law. **Risk named by the brief, to be measured from slice AT:** neighbours with plentiful land may never have an issue a clause answers. The funnel (issues held, places claimed, outsiders' use seen, *seeking terms* available and chosen, meetings, packages agreed, laws decided) is counted before anything is tuned, and a move that is never available is a bug, not a preference (02-04 §2).
- **Claims on wild ground, as built (2026-10-10, M5c slice AT, step one, ADR-0020 §5):** no new ADR. **The places people work** (`civ_agents::uses`): a gathering trip's block (by its centre patch) and a deposit dug are logged as worked, with the food got there; at midnight the day's work is folded in per household: a day counts once however many of its people went, the food is summed, and for each other settlement whose people worked the same place that day the household counts a day of outsiders seen. Everything fades with `use_half_life_days` (180, a design prior), and a place is let go once faded below a twentieth of a day unless outsiders were seen there within the year. Only what a household's own people saw is held; word of it at the hearth, and a view of the other polity, are step two's. **The issue** `outsiders`: a household of the settlement saw outsiders within the year at a place its polity does not claim; that household's elder joins the week's deliberators. **The policy kind** `claim_place` (`core:policy/wild_ground`; it answers `outsiders`, asks no question and bears security +0.5 and reciprocity −0.5, design priors: a claim keeps one's own and turns from sharing): proposed, the law names every such place (`Polity.claimed`), and while outsiders are seen at places the claims in force do not name another may be proposed. A household weighs it by what it believes outsiders take a year at the places it works (the food it got at each times their share of the days there, made a yearly rate), times `claim_keeps` (0.5, a design prior), against nothing paid. **Known limits:** only food places count in that forecast, so a deposit's clay is named but weighs nothing; and a claim in force changes nobody's use of anything yet, since outsiders' use without leave becomes a grievance with step two. **The template's id sorts last** (`wild_ground` after `repeal`): opinion's monthly draws are keyed by a template's index in the catalog, sorted by id, so a template sorting earlier would have shifted every later one's draws in every world. **Measured before building** (a probe over the demo worlds, from what households remember of patches): places worked by people of two settlements within the same year, 12–77 a year from year 4 on the M5b demo's seed 9 (mostly fish), and 20–261 a year on the M5a demo's world, where two clay deposits were also dug by people of two settlements. The issue needs the same place worked on the same day, which is rarer. **The funnel, measured** (a probe, by this step's build): the M5b demo's seed-9 world lived ten years from its first save, and the M5a demo's world six years from its tenth. In both, every village with a neighbour in reach saw outsiders and claimed a place within its first two years, the settlements founded later (two households each) among them; by the end Willowford's 16 claims named 17 places and Oakholt's 21 (six households by then) named 26. **Every claim decided passed** (30 of 30 and 57 of 57), mostly by wide margins (35 to 4, 44 to 0), and most name one place (75 of 87), since each review proposes another for places newly seen; where both villages work a place both claim it. An abandoned settlement's claims stay in force with nobody to hold them. Nothing in this step makes a claim cost its village anything, which is why it always passes; step two gives a claim consequences and agreements (slice AU) a way to grant leave, so nothing is tuned for it. **Tested:** two bands of 30 that know each other, with sixty days of a shared fishing place written into their record: within forty days a gathering decided a claim naming it (seed 5: passed 10 to 2, 6 abstaining). The forecast moved each stance by 0.07–0.20 points and what people hold dear by −0.60 to +0.76, so values decided it; whether claims pass is mostly a matter of values until a claim has a consequence. A village alone keeps its places and sees no outsiders. **A world of one settlement lives as before:** a saved village of 1,000 lived forty days by the previous build and this one gives the same digests in every section but content, places (the new record) and the polity, opinion and factions sections; with the new template set aside those three match byte for byte, so they differ only by the policy-id dictionary each saves. 883 against 891 ms a day. Saves schema 62 (a schema-61 save has no places on record and nothing claimed), content API 59; the wire is unchanged until the observer's relations view (step three).
- **Trespass and views of a polity, as built (2026-10-10, M5c slice AT, step two, ADR-0020 §3–§4):** no new ADR. **Who saw it:** the day's work now names who did it, and the day's end returns where people of more than one settlement met at one place. **Trespass:** someone who worked a place their polity's claim in force names, and knows the claim, holds a grievance against each household of another settlement seen working it that day (`Wrong::Trespass`, over extraction, under the claim; its harm the food that household got there in days of the holder's household's need, so a day's fishing, about a quarter day of food, is felt too little to tell, and days of it on end are told). Nobody of the other settlement is bound by the claim; if their own polity claims the place too, as the funnel found is common, they hold the same against the claimant's household. A grievance against outsiders is no reason to leave home: moving and founding leave it out. **Views of a polity** (`civ_agents::views`), shaped like a tie: evidence for and against in three domains (keeps its word, harms us, helps us), each from a prior of 1 (α = β = 1, ADR-0020 §3) and fading back to it with a half-life of five years (1,826 days, ADR-0020 from 13-01 §3.3); a view faded below a hundredth of evidence, or held by someone no longer here, is let go. Written so far only by trespass: `seen_trespass` (1) for each day one saw it, `heard_trespass` (0.5) on first hearing a trespass grievance told at the hearth, when the household blamed lives in another settlement than the listener's (both design priors). Nothing reads a view yet; stances on agreements will (slice AU), and the relations labels and panel show them (step three). **Tested:** in two villages whose gatherings both claimed a fishing place, one who knew the claim and fished it beside an outsider held a grievance against the outsider's household under the claim and a view that the other polity harms theirs, nothing else of the view moved; the outsider held the same only under their own polity's claim; after fifteen days of it someone who was not there had heard; and all of it saves, loads and goes on alike. **A world of one settlement lives as before:** forty days of the village of 1,000 give the same digests as step one's build in every section but content and the new relation section (empty), at the same speed (887 against 891 ms a day). Saves schema 63 (the day's work names who did it; a new section, `relation`, holds the views; a schema-62 save has no views and nobody named for the day's work), content API 60.
- **Relation labels and the relations view, as built (2026-10-10, M5c slice AT, step three, ADR-0020 §1); slice AT complete:** no new ADR. **The labels** (`civ_sim::relations`, a pure classifier beside the regime labels, which nothing in the world can read): each lived-in polity's standing toward each other one is worked out from its own people alone, so the two sides can differ: *unknown* when none of its households knows the other's settlement and nobody holds a view of it; *wary* when at least a tenth of its adults hold a view leaning past 0.6 toward "harms us" (and no fewer than lean toward "helps us"); *friendly* likewise toward "helps us"; otherwise *known*. The reasons say how many households know the other, how many adults hold a view and which way, how many hold a trespass grievance against its households, and how many of its claims the other claims too. The thresholds are display conventions; a single account heard (half a sighting) is not enough to lean, two are. *Under agreement* and *tributary* come with slices AU and AV. Computing every label and the government panel daily leaves every saved section unchanged (tested). **The observer** (wire 1.57): each polity in the government panel lists its standing toward its neighbours, with the reasons, under "Toward its neighbours". **Word of another settlement's gatherings** already crosses only with travellers: company at a hearth passes word of a gathering ahead to whoever is there, and a hearth gathers only its own people and visitors (slice AM); word of another polity's laws, which nothing would read yet, waits for agreements (AU), whose sponsors need it. **A fix from measuring step two:** each outsider household seen was a grievance of its own, and a person holds at most eight, the least keenly felt giving way, so a fisher meeting several outsider households daily could crowd out grievances against their own gathering, which factions and petitions read. A person now holds one trespass grievance for each other settlement's people, raised again whoever of them is seen. **The funnel, measured** (the M5a demo's world lived six years from its tenth by this build): by year 2 Oakholt and Sedgebrook were wary of each other, each having claimed the other's fishing places; by year 6 8 of the 12 ordered pairs among the four lived-in villages read wary and 4 known, the two sides of a pair often differing (Oakholt wary of Ashford, Ashford's people only knowing Oakholt). From 27 to 39 of each larger village's people, and 18 of Oakholt's 22, held a trespass grievance; at most one person in a village held eight grievances. On the M5b demo's seed-9 world (by step two's build) the smaller Willowholt grew aggrieved against the larger Willowford (22–27 of its 43–46 people) far more than the reverse (2–22 of about 100), its people meeting Willowford's many fishers at the shared places more often. **Nothing reads any of it yet:** the labels and views wait for agreements (AU), whose stances will weigh views as regard for a sponsor is weighed, and leave to use a claimed place answers the trespass.
- **Claims that bind outsiders' choices, as built (2026-10-10, M5c slice AU, step one, ADR-0020 §2, §5):** no new ADR; it gives §5's "outsiders may use it only by leave" a mechanism. **Why first:** through slice AT a claim changed nobody's choices, so outsiders kept working claimed places, the claimant's forecast of what a claim keeps never came true, and leave to use would have granted nothing. **Word of a claim** crosses only with travellers: at the hearth, someone who knows a claim their polity's law in force makes tells a companion from another settlement with `share_claims` (0.15, inside 09-16 §2.2's 0.05–0.25 for routine news), and someone whose household heard of another polity's claim tells companions whose households have not (unless it is the listener's own polity's); the listener's household then holds every place that law claims, and lets them go once no law in force makes the claim. Laws themselves stay known only to those they bind (the M5a rule and its test). **The choice:** when choosing where to gather or dig, a household that heard of another polity's claim on a place weighs it at `claimed_worth` (0.5, a design prior; 13-05 §1.1 counts exclusive control, seasonal access and fees among the outcomes over a resource) of what it would yield, so it goes there only when nothing else is half as good, and is held to trespass if seen; a place its own polity claims too it holds as its own and weighs whole (first built without that exception, the M5a world's two villages that both claimed their shared fishing places each abandoned them). **Tested:** an elder of one village who knows its claim, with kin living in the other, is visited and the other's households hear of the claim (about two months in); and of two lives of one world, the one in which the outsiders' households had heard of a claim on the place they fished most saw them not go there in a month, the other 105 household-days. **Measured** (the M5a demo's world, six years from its tenth): every household of the villages with neighbours in reach heard of another's claims within the first year. Where both villages claim the places they share (Oakholt and Sedgebrook), both keep working them, and both stay wary, 48–82 of Oakholt's people holding a trespass grievance; Ashford, which claims little, keeps off what the others claim, stays merely known to both, and held no trespass grievance until year 6. The first claims failed at their gatherings (Rushford passed 3 of 7). **A world of one settlement lives as before** (forty days of the village of 1,000: the same digests but for content). Saves schema 64 (`RelationsSave.heard`; a schema-63 save has none), content API 61.
- **Agreements between polities, as built (2026-10-10, M5c slice AU, step two, ADR-0020 §6):** no new ADR. **The record** (`civ_agents::agreements`), one for both sides: the two polities (side 0 the seeker's), the two who met, its clauses in order, its term, the day made, each side's law, the day each gathering passed it and the day each side heard of the other's decision, and its state: offered; in force since a day; failed on a day (no terms; turned down, by a vote or a tie; too few came; nobody put it to a gathering in time; never answered, each naming its side); or ended (its term ran out, or a law of one side ended its own). **The issue** `claimed_from_us`: a household heard of another polity's claim on a place it worked within memory, which its own polity does not claim and which gives it no leave it knows of. **Seeking terms:** when a polity's weekly review puts nothing to its gathering, the elder of such a household who may propose seeks terms with the one of the claimant's body they know best, unless an agreement between the two polities is open or in force or one failed within `vote_memory_days`, and only when some package is one they would sponsor by their own weighing; one meeting a review. **Packages:** leave to use the places one side claims, the other's, or both (if it claims any), for each of `terms_days` (365 and 1,825), at most `packages` (8). **Weighing**, as a review weighs a move (ADR-0013 §5): a household's forecast is `against_gain` of what leave from the other would give back (the food it got at the other's claimed places it heard of, as a yearly rate, times `claim_keeps`) against what leave to the other gives up (the other settlement's share of the days at the places its own polity claims and it works, times the food there and `claim_keeps`). A negotiator scores a package as support × `w_gain` × ((their own forecast + `w_followers` × those who regard them) × the term in years + what the template does to what they hold dear) − `propose_cost`, the support predicted among those of their body they know. The two agree on the package each side expects to pass (support above a half) and to be worth sponsoring (a score above nothing), the most to the two together, or part with none. **Ratification:** the seeker proposes it at once as a law of `core:policy/word_given` (kind `agreement`, answering `claimed_from_us`; bears reciprocity +0.5 and security −0.5, authoring judgements), each household's stake its forecast of the agreed package; the other's negotiator proposes it at their polity's midnight once its gathering is free and they still may propose, under the issue `terms_sought`; each gathering decides it by its own custom, every stance kept. **Word:** someone who knows their polity's gathering passed it tells a companion at the hearth who lives in the other polity, and that side has heard; it is in force once both passed it and both heard. A side not put to a gathering within `answer_days` (120, a design prior) of the meeting, or not hearing within `answer_days` of the later decision, fails it. **Leave in force:** a household one of whose people knows its own polity's law deciding it no longer discounts the other's claimed places, and a witness who knows their own law holds no trespass against the other's people and takes no view from it. An agreement failed or ended lapses both its laws. **Chosen here, against ADR-0020 §6:** leave is the only clause (a gift and a recurring transfer need AV's carriers, and come with them), so clauses are a code list until there is more than one kind; the meeting takes no time (nobody walks to it); and a side's law lapses when the agreement fails even before word of the failure reaches it, a record nothing reads (failure recorded on each side once heard is step three's, with views weighed in stances). **A fix from measuring:** first built so that anyone holding the issue sought terms, three meetings in the M5a world's first two years parted with none, one sought over a clay pit, which the food forecast weighs at nothing (AT's known limit); seeking terms is a move like any other, so a seeker now goes only with a package they would sponsor. **Also fixed:** a claim's proposal was told before its places were named, so the chronicle said "a claim on 0 places". **Tested:** two villages, each claiming by law a place only the other's people work, whose households heard of the other's claim: within a month someone sought terms, the two agreed to give each other leave for five years, each gathering passed it, and once kin visiting each other's hearths had carried word both ways it came into force; then one of the claimants who knew their own law worked the place beside the others and held no trespass; all of it saves, loads and goes on alike. Where only one village claims a place both worked over a year ago, whose people still hold what the others took there, the meeting parts with none, and nobody seeks terms again while it is remembered. **The funnel, measured** (by this build: the M5a demo's world six years from its tenth, the M5b demo's seed-9 world six years from its first save): **no agreement was made in either.** Every village counter-claims the places it shares within weeks of seeing outsiders there, and every claim passes, so households hold the shared places as their own and the issue lasts only for a while: on seed 9 no household held it at any year's end, while 2–21 households a village worked only places both villages claim; in the M5a world it was held at year ends by 0–5 households a village, against up to 33 working only places both claim. The elders of 12 of the 21 households holding it knew someone of the claimant's body who may propose, yet only one meeting followed, in year 5 (Rushford with Oakholt), and it parted with none; a review that put a claim to its gathering that week, a failure remembered, and no package the seeker would sponsor each stop one, and the probe did not tell them apart. Leave between two villages that both claim a place grants nothing either does not already hold, so nothing here gives contested claims a cost. Nothing was tuned: what would bring agreements is a cost to contested claims (views weighed in stances, step three; force, M6) or something to give for leave (gifts, AV). **A world of one settlement lives as before:** forty days of the village of 1,000 by step one's build and this one, both worlds saved and re-encoded by this build, are the same in every section; the polity, opinion and factions sections differ as saved only by the policy-id dictionary each carries. Saves schema 65 (`LawSave.agreement`, `RelationsSave.agreements`; a schema-64 save has none), content API 62.
- **Agreements in the observer, and views in stances, as built (2026-10-10, M5c slice AU, step three, ADR-0020 §1, §3, §6); slice AU complete:** no new ADR. **Views weigh in stances on agreements** (ADR-0020 §3): a view of a polity has a *warmth*, how much more it leans toward "helps us" than "harms us" plus how far it leans past even toward "keeps its word" (−1 to 1, nothing at the prior), and at a gathering deciding an agreement each person's stance adds `w_regard` times their warmth toward the other polity, as regard for a sponsor is added; it is kept with the stance (`StanceRecord.view`, saves schema 66). A negotiator weighs their own view the same way; their prediction of support at home does not see others' views (nobody sees another's). **The label** *under agreement* (wire 1.58): a polity whose own law deciding an agreement in force with the other stands is under agreement with it, whatever its people's views, which stay among the reasons beside the terms and how many agreements await a gathering or word and how many failed or ended. **The observer:** each relation in the government panel lists the agreements between the two, newest first: the terms, where it stands, and each side's law history side by side (who proposed it and when, how its gathering decided with the tally, when that side heard of the other's decision, and whether its law lapsed). **Tested:** an agreement in force names each side's relation *under agreement*, and both sides' histories show the decision and the word heard. In the same two villages, with everyone of one believing the other's people harm theirs (ten sightings each), the wary village's negotiator still agreed, but its gathering turned the agreement down, every stance there keeping what its view took away (13-01 §5: "some attractive packages fail authorization"). A schema-65 save loads with no view weighed in any stance. **Not built:** failure recorded on each side once heard; a failed agreement's laws lapse at once, a record nothing reads. **A world of one settlement lives as before** (forty days of the village of 1,000, re-encoded: the same). Saves schema 66, wire 1.58.
- **Goods for leave, and payments carried, as built (2026-10-10, M5c slice AV, step one, ADR-0020 §7):** no new ADR. **Why:** through AU, leave was the only clause, so one-sided leave (one village claims what the other works) had nothing to be traded for, and the AU funnel saw no agreement made. **The clauses** (`gift`, `transfer`, appended to the clause codes, each with a good, kilograms and, for a transfer, days between payments): a polity whose common store is in force (`common_store`) and holds something may give, for leave to use the places the other claims, a gift once (`gifts_kg`, 100 or 400 kg) or a transfer every `transfer_days` (`transfers_kg`, 100 kg a year) of the good its store holds most, to the other only when the other keeps a store too (13-02 §1.2 B: a transfer is the polity's, paid from its store, not the households'). Up to `packages` (now 16, inside 13-01 §3.3's 8–32) are weighed. **Weighing:** a household's forecast adds its share of what its store gives or gets (one household's of its settlement's, a design prior: the store is everyone's levy): a transfer a year, against or toward what leave gives back, over the term; a gift once, apart from the term, both through `against_gain`. **Payments** (`civ_agents::agreements::Due`): a gift falls due when the agreement comes into force, a transfer then and every `every_days` after while in force. Each midnight the paying store sets aside what it holds of what is owed, up to the whole, into the payment, a ledger holder of its own on the appended `agreement` channel, which spoils in the open (goods are conserved: the payment's holdings and flows count in the totals); nothing is taken from households for it. **Carrying:** the one named to carry it is the keeper of the paying store (`keep_store` in force), else the one of the paying side who agreed to the terms, while they live there; for them alone, in daylight that lasts the walk there and back, the appended `carry` behaviour (`core:activity/walk_owed_goods`) is an option: walk to where the store is kept (the keeper's home, or the hearth), take it up, walk to the other settlement's hearth (at most `max_walk_minutes`, 120, each way, as a visit) and hand it over into the other's store, then walk home. It is worth `carry_points` as a duty against the walk (`w_walk_hour`, 2 points an hour). Nobody else is told of it; nobody is made to. **Missed:** a payment not handed over within `deliver_days` (30, a design prior) is missed, its cause kept in the truth layer (the store held none; nobody of the paying settlement could carry it; nobody carried it in time), and what it still holds goes back to the paying store; the chronicle tells both handing over and missing, with the amounts. **Four accounts** (ADR-0020 §7): the store's levy (its own compliance account), what was set aside, what was owed and what arrived, each kept on the payment. **Chosen here, against ADR-0020 §7:** a payment is its own record, not an ADR-0015 obligation (those are imposed by a finding on a household, and their answering is a household's choice; a polity's payment is answered by its store and a carrier); nothing holds a payment back by a decision yet (no move to remit, suspend or repeal reads it: step two and later); and the carrier's load is not on their back (the payment holds it, and spoils as goods in the open do); a carrier hands over one payment a trip. **Tuned once, a design prior:** `carry_points` was first 3, and in the test villages (an hour and a half's walk each way, about 6.5 points of walking) the keeper never chose to carry and every payment would have been missed; it is 12, enough to outweigh the longest walk allowed (two hours each way, 8 points) by about a morning's useful work. **Tested:** an agreement in force owes a gift of 400 kg and 100 kg a year; at the next midnight the store sets aside both, the keeper is named, and within the month the keeper carries each to the other hearth, the other's store holding what arrived (grain keeps: nearly all of it), every kilogram accounted for; a transfer every five days from an empty store with no keeper falls due again on its day and is missed a month after for an empty store, the chronicle saying so; both save, load and go on alike; a household's forecast of a gift weighs once and a transfer each year, toward the receiver and against the giver, by its share. **Measured in a test world, not tuned:** the AU test world where one village claims a place the other works and the two parted with none was run again with both keeping stores, the seeker's holding 1,000 kg of grain: the goods packages were offered and weighed, and the two still parted with none. What the counterpart holds dear (their values, creed and view of the other village together) weighs 0.76 points against the agreement whatever the term, their household loses no food by leave, and its share of 400 kg (about 27 kg) does not outweigh that; the seeker's people opposed giving 400 kg, and the 100 kg gift and the yearly transfer passed the seeker's side only. Goods for leave help where leave's cost is food; they do not buy past what people hold dear. **A world of one settlement lives as before**: no payment is ever owed, and the carry option is never offered (forty days of the village of 1,000 by step three's build and this one, both saves re-encoded by this build: the same in all 49 section digests); the ten smoke worlds pass. Saves schema 67 (`AgreementSave.clause_good`, `clause_kg`, `clause_days`; `RelationsSave.dues`, `goods`; a schema-66 save has no payment and no clause that moves goods), content API 63. **Not built (step two):** views written by performance (keeps its word when a payment is met or missed, helps us when one is received, for those who know), word of a miss and its cause crossing with travellers, the tributary label and both burden ratios (13-02 §2.1: T/Y and T/(Y − subsistence)), and payments in the observer.
- **Performance seen, the tributary label, and payments in the observer, as built (2026-10-10, M5c slice AV, step two, ADR-0020 §1, §3, §7); slice AV complete:** no new ADR. **Views written by performance** (ADR-0020 §3: keeps its word is "obligations due to one's polity or household, met or missed"; helps us is "gifts and relief received"; research 13-01 §1.5: update "only after a relevant opportunity", and a payment falling due is one): when a payment is handed over in full, each of the receiving polity's people living there who knows its own law deciding the agreement takes `performance` (1.0, as much as a trespass seen; a design prior) of evidence that the paying polity keeps its word, and for a gift as much that it helps; when one is missed, as much that it does not keep its word. The view acts are appended (`payment_met`, `payment_missed`, `gift_received`; no save change, as act codes were already saved), so warmth, and with it stances on later agreements with that polity (AU step three), now answer to performance. The paying side's own people take nothing from their polity's payments. **The label** *tributary* (13-02 §2.1): a polity paying another a recurring transfer under an agreement in force is tributary to it, whatever else its people hold, and its first reason says what it pays a year and its burden both ways: T, the food energy of the transfers a year; Y, what its households' fields bring in an ordinary year as their records say, less the seed (as a household's outlook reckons it); C, a year of its people's food; T/Y and T/(Y − C), or, when Y is no more than C, the subsistence shortfall instead of the second ratio; gifts, paid once, are not counted. The receiving side stays under agreement and says what it receives. Nothing in the world reads either (ADR-0020 §1). **The observer** (wire 1.59): each agreement in the government panel lists its payments, oldest first: what was owed from whose store and from when, what was set aside and what arrived, and whether it is still owed (by when, and who is to carry it), met or missed (and why). **Tested:** in the step-one test villages, now with each side's law known to its adults, B's people hold views of A leaning toward keeping its word and helping once both payments are met, and A's people hold none; A's relation to B is *tributary*, its burden given as a share of its fields' ordinary yield and of their yield beyond need; B's is *under agreement*, receiving 100 kg of grain a year; the panel's lines show both payments met. Where a transfer from an empty store is missed, everyone of B who knows the agreement holds a view of A leaning against its word, its reason the miss, and the panel's line says nothing was set aside and why it was missed. A classifier unit test covers the label and the shortfall wording; a views unit test covers the new acts. **Measured, the demo worlds with goods for leave (step one's build, six years each, nothing tuned):** in the M5a demo's world the three first villages kept a common store in force with a keeper, Ashford's and Oakholt's holding 4.5 t of grain and growing to about 7.5 t, Sedgebrook's falling to 0.6 t after most of its households left, while Alderholt and Herondale passed a store but never had a keeper or anything in it; five meetings followed (all Sedgebrook's people seeking terms with Oakholt's or Ashford's, in years 3–5), and all five parted with none, so no payment was ever owed; in the M5b demo's seed-9 world only Willowford kept a store (to 10.9 t), no household ever held the issue, and nobody sought terms. Goods for leave did not unblock the funnel; what stops it is upstream (counter-claims make the issue rare, and what negotiators hold dear outweighs a household's share of a gift), as AU and step one recorded. **Not built, recorded:** the cause of a miss told by travellers (the paying side knows it; the receiving side takes every miss at full weight, which 13-01 §1.6's "explanation" would soften: it needs a remembered telling per person, and nothing reads it until a move answers a miss); moves that answer a miss (remit it, suspend the clause, or repeal the agreement because of it; a law of either side may still end an agreement as in AU); and failure recorded on each side once heard (AU). **A world of one settlement lives as before:** no payment is owed and no view is written by one (forty days of the village of 1,000 by step one's build and this one, both saves re-encoded by this build: the same in all 49 section digests); the ten smoke worlds pass, and the government and settlements e2e pass with this host. Content API 64 (`[relations] performance`), wire 1.59 (`AgreementLine.payments`).
- **Crossings over water, step one: the crossing and its physics (2026-10-10, M5c slice AW, ADR-0004 §7, ADR-0009 §9):** amends ADR-0004 (the walking ground changes with crossings) and ADR-0009 (a crossing's condition and failure), as the M5c design said. **Why first:** small rivers are waded at a quarter of dry ground's speed and rivers above `ford_max_discharge_m3s` (3 m³/s) cannot be crossed on foot (`civ-world` nav), so a bridge either saves wading or opens a route; nothing could build one until the ground people walk on could change. **Bridge systems as content** (`kind = "bridge"`, content API 65; research 11-07 §2.1, §2.3, §2.4): the clear spans a system may be proposed for, its members (how many side by side, the diameters they may be cut to, their good, which must have timber strengths, and its density), a bearing on each bank, its deck's walking factor, labour per metre of member, rot a year, the margin builders size to, and the chance a fall kills. The first, the log footbridge (`core:bridge/log_beam`): 2–8 m of clear span (11-07 §2.1's single log), two logs of 20–50 cm, 1 m bearings, walked at 0.6 of dry ground's speed, 8 hours a metre of log (two 7 m logs: 112 hours, about 14 person-days, inside 11-07 §2.3's 5–30 for a 5 m log footbridge), rot of 5 % of the section a year, sized to a margin of 3, a fall killing with 0.3 (as a ruin, ADR-0009 §5); all design priors where the research gives ranges. **The record** (`civ_land::crossings`, saved in its own section, schema 68): its system, its banks, the water cells it spans in order, its clear span (the channel's width from hydraulic geometry), its members and their diameter, their quality and the share of their section lost, its owner (a household or a polity), its labour, when begun, and its state (being built, open since a day, failed on a day by its own weight or under a walker). **What it carries** (`civ_agents::bridge`): its logs as a simply supported beam over the span and a bearing, `M = wL²/8 + PL/4` under their own weight and 1 kN a walker at mid-span (11-07 §1.3, §2.2), against the wood's bending strength over `n·πd³/32` of section times what rot has left cubed (11-07 §5.5: a tenth of the diameter gone leaves 73 %) and their quality; builders take the smallest diameter, in 5 cm steps, that gives the margin with one walker on new logs, none outside the span envelope. Two new 20 cm logs over 6 m carry a walker 19 times over; rot of about 63 % of their section takes them to failure, which at 5 % a year comes after about 12 years over the longest span (11-07 §2.4: untreated log bridges last 10–20 years). Only bending is a mode yet. **The day and the step:** each midnight rot takes its daily share of each open crossing's logs, and one that cannot carry its own weight gives way with nobody on it; whoever sets off on a walk over an open crossing steps onto it, and one that cannot carry them gives way under them and they do not go (11-07 §6.2). **Failure:** whoever is on it falls into the water and dies with the system's chance (a new cause of death, *a fall when a crossing gave way*, saved as `Fell`); the rest scramble out onto the nearer bank; everyone whose walk would cross it stops where they are and decides again; the chronicle names what gave way, why, and who fell (a new entry kind, `crossing`). **Routing** (ADR-0004 §7): the walking grid is built with the open crossings' decks laid over the water, and laid again whenever a crossing opens or gives way, after the step that changed it; routes, the landmarks that bound their search, and each settlement's travel field are kept against one routing revision, the paths' survey and the crossings' together. **Tested:** a log over a river too big to wade, by the hearth: the river cell becomes walkable and the route between its banks crosses it, sooner than the way round; someone walks across on new logs; with its logs rotten past their own weight a minute before midnight, it gives way at midnight, the river is as it was, and no route crosses it; with rot that leaves the logs their own weight but not a walker, it gives way under the one who steps onto it, who either died of the fall or stayed on the bank; both save, load and go on alike, the grid laid alike. **Found while testing:** with rotten logs left open through a day, a villager whose walk crossed it stepped on first: people do plan their routes over a crossing once it is open. **A world without crossings lives as before** (the grid as the map made it, the routing revision the survey's alone: forty days of the village of 1,000 by AV's build and this one, both saves re-encoded by this build, are the same in all 50 section digests); the ten smoke worlds pass. **Not yet:** anyone building or keeping a crossing (step two), public works and longer spans (step three), the observer's view, rot at the bearings, and floods (M6).
- **Crossings over water, step two: households build log footbridges (2026-10-10, M5c slice AW, ADR-0004 §7, ADR-0009 §9):** **Measured before designing** (a scratch probe routing each household's recorded places, slice AT's patches and deposits, from home with and without a log at each candidate site within an hour): every river too big to wade is wider than a log's 8 m (cells are 8 m), so logs only ever cross wadeable streams, where they save wading, not a detour; in the village of 1,000 after a year, no household's places lay across any 2–8 m stream; in the M5a demo's world in year 12, 22 of 49 households had candidate sites, and the best routed saving was 0.01 hours a year against 67 hours of work. Across the first sixteen 768-cell river valleys, eight have no log-spannable wadeable stream at all and only two have one within 1 km of the hearth. **The record** (`civ_agents::fords`, saved with the crossings, schema 69): each walk counts, for the walker's household, every river cell it wades and does not walk on a deck, fading by the half-life of what households hold of the places they work (180 days), so a household weighs the trips its people actually make, every one of them; cells below half a wade are let go of. **The review** (research 11-07 §1.1's lifetime cost, as the settlements brief §1.7 put it: *the walking its own recorded trips would save ... in hours of its own work*): on its own day of the year a household weighs a crossing at each cell it wades, for each bridge system someone in it knows the technique of that spans the channel there (the reach's width, within the envelope; land either side; its nearer bank within the work's walk of the hearth): the wades a year it holds there, times the seconds a wade takes over walking a deck (the walking grid's own step into the cell, of which only the ground factor changes: about 13–22 s for an 8 m cell, on a worn trail and off one), times the years the crossing would last before rot leaves it unable to carry a walker (its margin falls with the cube of what is left, so `(1 − m^(−1/3)) / rot a year`, at the quality its people's average building skill would lay it on average, `1 − σ√(2/π)`), against the hours it takes; it begins the one that most exceeds its work, one at a time, never where a crossing stands or is being built, but taking over one begun by a household that is no more. No time preference discounts the saving: the brief says lifetime; a prior from 08-09 §1.3 or 10-02 §3.3's 1–5 year payback would only make building rarer. **The work** (a new behaviour, `bridge`, and activity, `core:activity/work_on_crossing`, appended so every older index stands; content API 66 also gives bridge systems the `skill` their builders practise): heavy work with an axe as building a hut is, 1–4 hours a session in daylight, at the nearer bank within half an hour's walk, open only to those whose household is building one; each hour of a capable adult's work worth `w_walk_hour` times the hours of walking it saves the household (a new reason, *the walking a crossing would save*). Its builders' hours weighted by their skill are kept (`skill_h`, schema 69); once its labour is done its logs' quality is drawn from their average, as a building group's (ADR-0009 §6), it opens, the walking grid is laid again, and the chronicle says whose household finished it. The inspector says *building the log footbridge north of home*. **Tested:** in world 4 (a log-spannable stream 623 m from the hearth), a household remembering five wades of it begins nothing; remembering 1,500 (about 2,100 a year), it begins a log footbridge, begins nothing more while building it, and its people finish it in five days: 70 hours over a 2.4 m channel, two 20 cm logs, its builders' average skill 0.51, quality 0.95 (11-07 §2.3: one to six working days for a small crew); the route between its banks then crosses it, and it saves, loads and goes on alike; unit tests of the wade record, the life of a crossing (its loss at the end of life leaves a margin of exactly 1, 10–20 years for new logs), and the expected quality against 2,000 draws; a schema-68 save loads with no wade remembered. **Measured after:** forty days of the village of 1,000 by AW step one's build and this one, both re-encoded by this build, are the same in all 50 section digests: nobody in it waded anything. Two years lived on by this build: in the M5a demo's world 40 of 51 households waded, but 1,546 of 2,127 wades were of brooks under 2 m, below any authored system, and the most waded log-spannable cell held 250 wades of all its households together; in the seed-9 demo world 21 of 30 households waded a 3.1 m stream about 1,900 times, spread over 23 cells, the most waded holding 490 (about 690 a year): at 13–22 s a wade that is 2.5–4 hours a year against a log's 70 hours over about 15 years, so no household built one in either world, and none was nudged to. The ten smoke worlds pass. **Not yet:** a household giving up a crossing it began, mending one before it fails (it is built again only once it has fallen and its site is waded again), the places a crossing would bring within reach (the brief's second term; on the measured maps a log reaches nothing a wade does not), wades along a stream counted toward a site beside them (a detour of one cell costs about what a wade does), and stepping stones for the brooks under 2 m, which would be content (11-07 §2.1). **Measured again after the walking fix below** (walkers no longer step between a stream's cells dry; two years lived by that build): in the seed-9 demo world 25 of 30 households waded, 9,836 times at log-spannable cells (five times as often as measured above), the most waded cells holding about 1,600 wades of all their households together, about 2,250 a year, some 12 hours of walking a year: more than a log's 70 hours over its life for the village together, but no one household's share; in the M5a demo's world all 51 households waded, 65,674 of 84,552 wades at brooks under 2 m (one brook cell held 15,600). Still no household built a crossing in either world: what a stream costs a village falls on many households, none of whom alone would repay the work (11-07 §4.1's *household or village cooperation*), which is step three's to weigh.
- **Fix: walkers stepped between a river's cells (2026-10-10, found designing M5c slice AW step three, ADR-0004 §7):** **Found:** a probe of what the trunk river cuts off found villages 24 m from a river too big to wade with no land across it out of reach. The walking grid let a diagonal step pass between two land cells whatever stood at the corner, so a river running corner to corner was stepped past in one step: on the M5a demo's map 522 places where a walker crossed the river too big to wade in 13 s, and 2,648 where a wadeable stream was crossed without wading; on the village of 1,000's map 1,202 and 2,310. Every slice since M1 walked that way, so the rule documented since M1, that rivers above `ford_max_discharge_m3s` cannot be crossed on foot, did not hold wherever a river ran diagonally, and the wade record of step two counted few wades because few were made. **The rule:** a diagonal step passes through one of the two cells at its corner, so it is walked no faster than the better of them allows (and no faster than its destination) and not at all when neither can be walked; route searches, travel fields and landmarks cost every step so, a straight line drawn to straighten a route is held to the same where it passes from one cell to the next by a corner, and the cells a walk is traced over (the wades it makes, the crossings it steps onto) include the corner cell it went by. Wear is traced as before. Tested on a river running corner to corner: too big to wade, no step, route, travel time or straight line crosses it; small enough, the diagonal step costs four times dry ground, as wading does, and a step alongside it past one wet corner is as dry. **What it changed** (behaviour, not saves: no schema change): the M5b demo's seed-9 world's two villages (Willowford and Willowholt) can no longer reach each other at all, and 17 % of the land within 1.5 km of Willowholt is out of four hours' reach; near the village of 1,000's hearth, 61 % of the land within 1.5 km is now over twice its straight walk away (across the river, round its end); in the M5a demo's world 12 % near Ashford is, and its villages still reach each other. A year of the village of 1,000 ends with the same 1,062 people and 238 buildings (241 before), but 308 fields, not 649: the ground households break is on their own bank. It costs 1,070 ms a simulated day against 725 (390 s a year, mostly in the first weeks after loading a save made before, while households give up places now across the river; days 31–40 ran at 732 ms). A ten-year quick look at the dashboard (five worlds of two founding groups) passed seven rows, its population green (96–114 people at year 10 from about 80 founders), food prices, firms, structural failures, accounts and regimes green; *moves between settlements* is amber because in two worlds the founding groups were placed on opposite banks (ADR-0018 §6 places groups by their land, never by whether they can walk to each other) and never met: no visits, marriages or moves. **Tests changed:** the tests of visits, buying from a neighbour, fetching to resell and diffusion that used world 3, whose two camps are now three hours apart round the river, use worlds 9 and 10, whose camps are on one bank; the test that nobody fetches what is not worth the walk keeps world 3, where that is truer still. **Not re-run:** the M5a and M5b demos and every measurement in this log before today ran with walkers stepping between river cells; their records stand for the build they ran on. Re-living them is the M5 demo's (slice AX). In worlds whose villages the trunk river parts, only a crossing joins them: slice AW's step three, a polity's crossing beyond one log, is now what could.
- **Crossings in the observer (2026-10-10, M5c slice AW, step three, first part):** no new ADR. Nothing but the chronicle showed a crossing, so a village's crossing could not be watched being built. **The wire** (1.60): the snapshot carries a crossings revision (a hash of each crossing's state, work done, day opened or failed, rot and owner; 0 with none), and `GetCrossings` lists every crossing in the order begun: its system's name, its banks' centres, its clear span and its members' length (the span and a bearing on either bank), how many members and how thick, its state, the labour it takes and has had, its quality and the share of section rot has taken, its margin over one walker now when open, its owner (a household or a polity), and the kernel's words: *Ada's household's log footbridge, being built: 25% of 72 hours of work*, *..., open since year 2: two members 20 cm thick over 2.4 m, sound; it carries a walker 19.0 times over*, *..., gave way in year 9 under someone stepping onto it*. **The map** draws each crossing's members their length, centred between its banks and along the line from one to the other, as wide as its members side by side and never thinner than about two pixels; one being built is fainter the less of its work is done, an open one greys as rot takes its section, and one that gave way is a dark wreck; the pointer readout gives the words of the one under it. The web fetches the list again only when the revision changes. **Tested:** a host test lists none in a new world, then a log being built by the first household, laid by hand, with its revision, owner, banks, members' length and words; web unit tests of the decoder, where the members lie, which crossing the pointer is on (the nearer of two, none off the members' ends), and how each state is drawn. No e2e of its own: no world builds a crossing within an e2e's time, and nothing can place one yet; the observer and earthworks e2e pass with this host. No save change; a world without crossings sends a revision of 0 and nothing else changes.
- **Crossings built together (2026-10-10, M5c slice AW, step three, second part; ADR-0013 §3, ADR-0009 §9):** no new ADR (ADR-0009 §9 gains a polity's crossing). **The issue and the law** (content API 67, `core:policy/works_crossing`): the issue `fords` is present where a household of the settlement wades a stream at a site a crossing of a system its people know could span, within the work's walk of the hearth, with no crossing standing or being built there or a cell either side (a step aside to a deck costs about what a wade saves), and whose wades, all the settlement's households' together, would repay the work over the crossing's life (step two's household rule, for the village). One weighing moves may propose a crossing at one of the three such sites their own household wades most (research 09-05 §3.3: a few packages per decision). Each household forecasts it as the food a year the walking it would save could bring, less what its equal share of the work, spread over the crossing's life, could, an hour worth the food an hour of the household's own work brings at its own costs (M3a; research 11-07 §2.3: communal labour priced at the production it forgoes): in the units of every other forecast. The template bears on autonomy (−0.5: a share of every household's hours is asked, as a common store asks a share of every harvest). A polity builds one at a time. **Passed**, the polity begins the crossing, its own; each household is asked an equal share of its work, labour asked and labour given kept apart (research 09-06), and nothing follows a share not given (as with a curfew's keeping, 09-06 §1.5; ADR-0015's labour-days obligation waits until a default can mean something). **The work:** someone who knows the law in force may work on the polity's crossing as on their household's, weighing the walking it saves their own household and, while their household has not given its share, what keeping to the gathering's word is worth to them, weighed as a levy's payment is (the norm that the gathering binds, their stance on the law, their regard for its sponsor; a new reason, *the work the gathering asked of their household*). The hours each household gave are kept with the crossing (saves 70), its members' quality drawn from its builders' skill as any crossing's, and the chronicle says the village's people finished it. Households, too, no longer begin a log a cell beside a crossing that stands or is being built. **Found while measuring, before committing** (two-year runs of the seed-9 and M5a demo worlds): (1) the forecast first priced an hour of walking at what it is worth in a choice made now (`decision.w_walk_hour`, 2 points) for every hour of a year, making an hour a year worth a fifth of a year's food in the forecasts' units: Willowford put a crossing to its gathering most weeks, its two-thirds custom turning nearly all down, and passed two; (2) a polity could begin crossings while others stood unbuilt, and sites a cell apart each counted: in the M5a world Oakholt passed about 50 and began about 40, most never worked; (3) with the hour priced at foregone production, no household's stake in a crossing reaches a stance (a few hundredths of a point against the 0.25 margin), and the template's first bearing, reciprocity +0.5, decided every vote: Oakholt passed 42 and finished 41 in two years, Willowford 22 of 22. That bearing misread the core value (reciprocity is "that what one household gives or takes from another is given back"), and was corrected once the measurement showed it decisive: NUDGE, the template now bears on autonomy alone; (4) even so, with stakes this small a crossing is proposed when the deliberator's own spread brings it up (a softmax at 0.5 points over a 0.5-point cost) and passes on regard for its sponsor, so Willowford still built 22 in two years, one at each site its people waded, until the issue was held to sites whose wades the village would repay. **Tested:** in world 4 with every household but one wading the stream about 400 times a year, no household begins a log, the village has a site there, a law to build it brings each wading household more than the one that wades nothing (whose forecast is a loss); passed by hand, the polity begins it, and its people give their shares until it opens: several households' hours, together its labour; the chronicle says the village finished it; nobody begins one beside it, and it saves, loads and goes on alike; a schema-69 save loads with no site named and no share kept; the reason codes stay unique; the full suite, clippy and the ten smoke worlds pass. **Measured (two years each, by this build):** in the seed-9 demo world Willowford (21 households) proposed two crossings, on days 241 and 269 of the run, both passed with nobody against (29–0 and 35–0), and each was built within three days by 15 and 13 of its households, most giving their share (about 4 hours) and some up to 11; no other site there repays its work, and Willowholt (9 households) had none. In the M5a demo world Oakholt (17 households) built four: three in the run's first month, each passed by about 30 to 4 and built within six days, and a fourth a year later (31 to 5); Ashford and Sedgebrook proposed none. **Not built, recorded:** a crew limit (11-07 §2.3: about five workers for a log footbridge over one to six days; here as many work at once as choose to, as on buildings, so 82 hours are done in a day or two); the trestle over the trunk river and the places reachable only across a crossing (the brief's second term), where a crossing's stakes would be large; giving up a stalled crossing (a polity's would block its next); mending one; and any consequence of a share not given. The deliberator reckoning its chance of passing by the support it expects rather than by the share its custom requires (ADR-0013 §5) is noted for the deliberator, not changed here.
- **Crossing crews (2026-10-10, M5c slice AW, step three, third part; ADR-0009 §9):** no new ADR. A bridge system names its `crew` (content API 68): at most that many choose to work on one crossing in a day, and anyone else who would is told it is full (a new reason, *as many as there is room for are already at work on it today*; research 11-11 §1.3: progress is limited by the usable work front). The core's log footbridge takes 5, the crew research 11-07 §2.3 states for a simple log footbridge's one to six working days. The day's count is kept with the crossing (saves 71). It is counted as each person chooses the work, not as they arrive: **found while measuring**, counting arrivals let everyone who chose in the morning, before the first of them got there, work, and Willowford's second crossing still took its 82 hours in two days. The chronicle's sentence for a crossing that gives way now says whose it was (the village's by its name, a household's by its elder's) and is kept with that settlement. `civ-host neighbours` now reports, each year, how each polity stands toward the others, the claims, agreements and crossings its gathering decided, and every agreement and crossing in the chronicle, and at the end every agreement with both sides' law histories and how many crossings were begun and stand open. **Tested:** with the village's crossing's crew held full four days running, no work is done on it, which fails with the limit removed (4 hours are done); no day's crew exceeds five; it took at least as many days as its crew allows; a household's crossing that gives way is named as its household's; a schema-70 save loads with no crew kept; the reason codes stay unique; the full suite, clippy and the ten smoke worlds pass. **Measured (two years each, by this build):** in the seed-9 demo world Willowford built the same two crossings (82 hours each), each finished on the fifth day after its gathering agreed to it, where before one was finished the next morning and the other the same morning; in the M5a demo world Oakholt built the same four (67 hours each), finished on the fourth to sixth day, where before three of them were finished by mid-morning of the day agreed and one on the seventh. Both now fall within 11-07 §2.3's one to six working days; the votes and sites were as before.
