- **The M5a and M5b demos lived again on today's walking (2026-10-10, slice AX, the M5 demo's first part):** both demos' worlds lived their thirty years again after the fix to walking between a river's cells, on the build of slice AW step three's second part (before crews held; they would add a few days to each village crossing). **The M5a demo** (`civ-host neighbours`, seed 1, three founding groups of 40): 120 founders became 249 people (Ashford 72, Oakholt 95, Sedgebrook 82), every year's checks passed. The observer's wave of ten households (49 people) came in year 11 and founded Alderholt, which was abandoned within the year, its people moving to Sedgebrook; 25 of them lived there at the end, 16 died and 8 left the map. People moved between settlements 63 times with their households (the 48 from Alderholt among them) and 5 times on marrying, nobody to found one: no coalition gathered, where the first record had 13 households leave Oakholt to found Fernfield. Oakholt and Sedgebrook, whose people visited each other some 9,600 times and made 73 purchases between them, came to hold each other wary within thirteen years; Oakholt and Sedgebrook claimed places, 18 claims in six of the years, all passed. **No agreement was made in thirty years** (the relations report lists none), so none reached ratification. Oakholt built 14 village crossings (15 crossings begun in all, 6 standing at the end); from year 16 their logs gave way to rot, under walkers nine times, and one fall killed (Sela, year 16), the village building again at the same sites. **The M5b demo's seed-9 world** (`civ-host twin`, 30 years with trade and without): as the fix predicted, Willowford and Willowholt never met (no contact between them in either life), so neither took the other's way of roofing: the M5b demo's finding does not hold on today's walking in that world, and in its second world the villages cannot reach each other either. Instead each village splintered on its own bank: six new settlements with trade and ten in the twin, and the 35 purchases between settlements with trade were between Willowford and the villages its own people founded. Every check passed in both lives. **What it leaves the M5 demo:** M5's fourth part, a treaty failing ratification, needs an agreement to reach a gathering; none has in any run since slice AU (the funnel: villages counter-claim the places they share, so leave between them grants nothing). The time box is for that, not for enclosures.
- **The agreement funnel, and need over a claim (2026-10-10, slice AX, the M5 demo's second part; time-boxed):** no new ADR. **Why no agreement reaches a gathering:** a household holds a place its own polity claims as its own even when another polity claims it too (slice AU step one, chosen because without it two villages that both claimed their shared fishing places each abandoned them). Villages counter-claim what they share within weeks of seeing each other there, so after that neither household loses anything to the other's claim, leave between them grants nothing, the issue *claimed from us* ends, and seeking terms over shared ground never opens; agreements can come only from one-sided claims, which are rare. In M5 nothing makes a contested claim costly (force is M6's), so this is the rule's honest consequence, not a bug in the move. **Tried and not adopted:** without the exception (a place another polity claims weighed at `claimed_worth` though one's own claims it too, and the issue held for it), a test pair of villages that both claim the place they gather most agreed on mutual leave within a month, and the M5a demo world from its year-11 save saw 15 meetings in six years (none in the same years unchanged), one agreement in force (Oakholt gave Birchfield 100 kg of grain for a year's leave on its places, carried by its keeper) and a second offered. But in that life Sedgebrook, about 90 people after taking in Alderholt's wave and twice short of food, emptied into Oakholt in year 14 and by year 17 both old villages were abandoned; with need also overriding claims (below), everyone but a few ended in Ashford (172 people). From the year-21 save the change brought no meeting in six years. **A fragile village, not a measure:** the same save lived with need overriding claims alone also emptied Sedgebrook in year 14 (77 to 9 within a season, its people to Oakholt and Ashford), so three of four lives from it did, each differing only in how a household weighs claimed places, and only the unchanged life kept Sedgebrook (about 69 people). A small change tips it, and which life it gives says little about the change. The exception stands on plausibility: with no cost to contesting ground, each side holding its own claim good is likelier than both keeping off; contested claims become costly with force in M6. **Kept:** a household some of whose people are going hungry (drawing on their body's reserve) weighs no other polity's claim when choosing where to gather or dig (research 04-09 §5.3: what a breach is worth turns on the agent's needs and alternatives). Tested: two lives of a world whose outsiders' households have no food and are drawing on their reserve, one of which heard of a claim on the place they gathered most, work it alike over five days; without the rule, the one that heard works it less. **What it leaves the M5 demo:** M5's fourth part, a treaty failing ratification, has not emerged in any run since slice AU, because no terms are agreed between villages that share ground. The prior the plan offered to nudge (a negotiator's caution about thin gatherings) acts only after terms are agreed, so it cannot help; nothing was nudged. The fourth part is shown in a test world instead: two villages each claiming a place only the other's people work, where most of one village's people (all but the three the other's people know) saw the others trespass these past weeks. A negotiator predicts support by members' forecasts and regard, not their views (nobody sees another's view), so whoever of that village meets the seeker, one of the three, agrees to five years' mutual leave; the seeker's gathering passes it 7 to 2 (17 of 19 came), and the other turns it down 3 to 8 (22 of 23 came), those against all holding that the first village harms theirs. The chronicle and the observer's agreement view say so with both law histories side by side ("passed on 10 March of year 1, 7 for, 2 against, 17 of 19 came; lapsed" beside "turned down on 11 March of year 1, 3 for, 8 against, 22 of 23 came"). A ratification failing on its own in a lived world waits for contested ground to cost something.
- **The fortification kit moves to M6, and M5 is closed (2026-10-10, slice AX):** no ADR (the M5c design's amendments to ADR-0009 and ADR-0010 for enclosures are not made). **Why:** what an enclosure does without war, in the relations brief (§1.8), is that outside takers must cross at a gate or climb, slower and more often seen; but in M5 people take only from their own settlement's stores (ADR-0020 §6 leaves takings between villages to M6), so a line round a village keeps out nobody who takes, and its only effects would be its costs (the walk round to a gate, the hours, the mending). Every household's forecast of an enclosure law would be a loss, so nobody would build one and its design would go untested by use. Its purpose arrives with M6's force and sieges (research 11-11 §1.4: entry, endurance, relief and negotiation as competing processes), which should shape it: built now it would likely be rebuilt then. **Carried to M6 as designed:** an enclosure as a line of sectors 10–25 m long (11-11 §2.2), each with a ditch (an ADR-0010 earthwork, its spoil a bank inside), a palisade of ADR-0009 parts rotting at the foot by wetness, and gates; walls laid on the walking grid as decks are, by the least of ground and climbing (climbing slow and seen); a polity's law naming the line, how work is raised and whether the watch keeps a gate, households outside the line paying without its cover; and the security dilemma (13-05 §1.5), which needs force to fear. **M5 closed:** M5a (several settlements), M5b (trade and diffusion) and M5c (relations and works) are implemented: claims over wild ground, agreements ratified by each side's own custom and performed by people carrying real goods, log footbridges built by households and by villages with crews. The trestle over a trunk river, other bridge systems (rope, truss, the stone arch) and per-polity currencies are not built. Its shared demo: on the corrected walking the M5b worlds' villages no longer meet (the earlier diffusion record stands for its build), and a treaty failing ratification is shown in a test world, not lived (above).
- **M6 design (2026-10-10, plan §7):** from four briefs (`docs/briefs/m6-health.md`, `m6-fire.md`, `m6-war.md`, `m6-observer.md`), the citations ADR-0021 and ADR-0015 §8 rest on checked against the reports: 12-01 §2.1 (10–30 L a person a day for hand-carried supply, where evidence is absent) and §3.1 (oak-lined wells in the early Neolithic); 12-02 §1.1 (do not "spontaneously generate a particular infection merely because a midden becomes large") and §5.3 (groundwater as sparse, directional connections); 03-02 §1.4 (a well needs construction depth, inflow and stored water), §2.2 (geological priors, "not measured universal ranges") and §5.1 (64–128 m groundwater cells); 05-03 §1.1 (three clocks; infection is not illness), §4.3 (a closed world may lose a pathogen for good, and nothing recreates it), §7.2 (exposure integrated, not arbitrary daily rolls) and §7.6 (mortality calibrated in lived runs); 05-01 §1.3 (exceptional deaths as excess over the baseline, "not another copy of ordinary disease mortality"); 05-05 §2.2 (organized supportive care at a relative risk of 0.95, a low-confidence prior); 03-07 §1.3 (an explicit origin, natural or intervention, so interventions do not contaminate records); 12-07 §1.1 and §1.8 (the response chain; never receiving help is its own outcome); 15-01 §3.5 and 15-02 §1.1 (nearness in time is not a cause; `caused_by`, `enabled_by`, `responded_to`, `part_of` and `occurred_after` are not interchangeable). **M6 is split in three** (§7), as M5 was, because its contents are three kinds of work, each with its own part of the demo: M6a, water and sickness (the cholera outbreak); M6b, fire, masonry and floods (the great fire and its masonry code); M6c, war and conquest (the siege). **In that order** because each uses the one before: floods rise from M6a's rivers by the day, a siege's endurance turns on wells and stores, and the fire and the siege are watched in the Incidents panel M6a builds; war comes last because the fortification kit and raids need force, and force should be shaped by what a siege does to water and food (the kit's move, above). **One ADR now,** [ADR-0021](decisions/0021-water-and-sickness.md) (water and sickness), which amends ADR-0009 and ADR-0010 for wells, and an amendment to ADR-0015 (§8: incidents of every kind, episodes, typed links), as the observer brief suggested, since the incident record generalizes ADR-0015's; M6b and M6c are designed when reached, one ADR each expected. **Four slices for M6a** (§7): AY (rivers by the day, groundwater and wells), AZ (sickness), BA (what people see and do), BB (the Incidents panel and the M6a demo). **The briefs' open questions, answered.** *Health brief:* (1) the plague tool offers a menu of authored diseases (cholera and bacillary dysentery first); "plague" is the tool's name, and plague proper waits until rodents and fleas can carry it (05-03 §1.2: transmission must follow a plausible route). (2) No world gets a declared introduction on a fixed day, which would be a scheduled event; the dashboard's epidemics row is grey unless an introduction happened, until imports from off the map exist (M9). (3) Well rights: a household's well serves its own people and its kin's households, a polity's well its members, and a household short of water may draw at any source it knows (need over another's word, as with claims, above). (4) Founders know well-digging and lining (12-01 §3.1). (5) Forecasts price a believed death as `w_death` days of work, a design prior; values still weigh on top. (6) The drought tool holds the weather's slow anomaly at a dry value for 3–24 months, keeping wet-day persistence and temperature coherent. (7) Fords stay on mean discharge in M6a; M6b designs floods and changing fords together. (8) Disease deaths are excess over the Siler baseline until a disease is measured endemic in lived worlds. (9) Suspicion is held per person, a tally of their own records. (10) Tallies of contact with the sick, and quarantine, wait until a lived outbreak shows what people would need them for. (11) Blessings and curses (ADR-0016 §5) touch an episode only through its severe-stage death draws. *Observer brief:* (1) the Takings panel becomes one Incidents panel. (2) Coverage ships *could reach* and *did come*; probes on copied worlds come later. (3) The chronicle is the observer's and may tell truth, as it tells takings (ADR-0015 §7), but an outbreak's entries count cases and deaths and never name a source as their cause: what fouled a source is in the episode page's truth column, beside what people know. (4) The plague tool targets a person only: a source as its target would make "traced to a well" the observer's doing. (5) Satisfaction is dropped from plan §5.4's dashboards: nobody in the world states one, and grievances against a provider are already ADR-0016's. (6) Destructive tools are always offered, each use an `Influence` record marked as the observer's wherever it shows. (7) ADR-0015 is amended (above). (8) Charts stay SVG, as the market panel draws them, until a panel needs more. (9) Flood water on the map and (10) the siege page are M6b's and M6c's. Weather visuals and fake interiors go to the Unreal track (§7). **Bench, not yet clean:** a year of 3,000 people in three settlements of 1,000 at Max took 1,461 s on this build, against slice AL's 1,179 s, but it ran while four agents built on the same container; slice AY measures it again alone before adding daily water work.
- **Water under the ground and rivers by the day, as built (2026-10-10, M6a slice AY, step one, ADR-0021 §1–§2):** no new ADR. **Measured first:** the 3×1,000 bench alone on the container (`bench_village --people 1000 --groups 3 --size 1024 --seed 2 --known --max`): a year in 1,286.5 s, against slice AL's 1,179 s on the same arguments, about 9 % slower after M5's additions; the ten-minute budget stays unmet, as recorded at AL. The design's 1,461 s ran on 768 cells while four agents built, so it compared with neither. **What the soil cannot hold:** the reference soil's daily surplus, until now clamped away, is kept (`DayWater.surplus_mm`), with its long-run mean over the landscape's 300 climatology years (`Climatology.surplus_mm_per_day`: 0.52 mm a day, 191 mm a year, in the temperate valley). **The aquifer** (`civ_land::water`): one head per habitat patch (128 m, inside research 03-02 §5.1's 64–128 m), on 03-02 §1.3's storage equation: recharge, exchange with the four neighbouring patches at the harmonic mean of their transmissivities, and exchange with the river, lake or sea in a patch at its transmissivity times twice its channel's length over the patch's side, at most four (a design prior: the water lies about half a patch from the middle); a patch of water alone is held at its water's level, which is the mean of its river beds, lake levels and the sea's; a head that reaches its patch's lowest land cell stops there, the rest seeping out. Each day is lived in explicit sub-steps sized from the patch that empties fastest (1–3 on the measured worlds). **Units of ground** (content API 69, the land profile's `[groundwater]`): first match by habitat, each drawing its conductivity evenly in its logarithm and its specific yield evenly once a world, within 03-02 §2.2's priors: alluvial sand under water, wet margins and floodplain (0.1–100 m/day, 0.15–0.30, 10 m thick, from 03-02 §1.4's worked well), silty slope deposits under the lower-slope woodland (0.001–0.1, 0.03–0.15, 10 m), weathered rock under the rest (0.001–10, 0.005–0.05, 15 m), as the health brief §1.2 proposed; the thicknesses are design priors. Half the surplus recharges, the rest runs off at once (`recharge_share`, a tuning value: the research gives no partition for this landscape). **Settled, not spun up:** when a world is made (before the year a new world lives first) and when a save from before schema 72 loads, the heads go by projected over-relaxation to where the mean recharge would hold them for good (100–307 sweeps, under 0.03 s for 4,096 patches). ADR-0021 §2 said "spin up over some years of the landscape's climatology"; the steady state under the mean is what such years average toward, at a fraction of the cost, and the year a new world lives first gives it a season. **The runoff store:** what ran off, what groundwater gave the water net of what the rivers lost to it, and what seeped out enter one store for the world, which lets go `1 − e^(−1/3)` of what it holds a day (`recession_days` 3, tuning); its outflow against the mean surplus is the day's flow factor (`Land::flow_factor`), which scales every reach's mean discharge, derived from the saved store. **Springs** are derived from the heads: a patch off the water whose head stands at its lowest ground, where what flows to it from the patches round it at today's heads is at least `spring_min_m3_day` (0.5 m³, a design prior: 25 people's 20 L), rising at that lowest cell. **Saved:** the heads (doubles) and the store in the land section (`Land.water`, schema 72); the aquifer is derived from the map, the content and the seed. **Tested:** 03-02 §4's storage test (50 mm of net recharge raises a closed aquifer of specific yield 0.2 by 0.25 m); every cubic metre accounted for day by day on a wet slope over a river; settled heads hold under the mean and the store then lets go the mean; a dry spell lowers heads and flow, a storm's runoff passes at once; a spring where recharge from a high patch meets a low one; open gravel (1,000 m a day) stepped stably; a new world's water table under its ground; splitting an advance changes nothing. **Measured** (probes on the river-valley preset at 1,024 cells): over thirty years of seed 1's weather the accounts close (in 376,660,763 m³ against out 376,263,424, the aquifer +516,002 and the store −118,664), and the mean flow factor is the surplus's against its mean (0.988 against 0.99; seed 2, 0.919 against 0.92); single years run from 0.16 to 2.2 of the mean flow with their weather. The rivers are flashy: below a quarter of their mean on 172–355 days a year, because the silty lower slopes (42–73 % of the patches on the seeds measured) hold almost nothing and give their recharge back as seepage within days. Homes on the valley floors stand above a water table 0.7–3.6 m down at the median and at most 2.2–5.7 m, on four seeds, steady through the year where a river holds it (03-02 §2.3: a Chilean sample of dug wells ran 1.48–10.1 m, prehistoric Yangtze wells mostly under 3 m). Springs are plentiful: 500–1,560 a map, 8–24 a square kilometre, where heads meet the ground in valleys too small for a mapped river. A day's water costs 0.03–0.08 ms. **Nothing in the world reads it yet:** forty days of the village of 1,000 lived on from one schema-71 save by the build before (in its own worktree, with its own content) and by this one are the same in every section but the content and the land. Fords stay on mean discharge (§9, "M6 design"). **Not yet:** sources people choose and use that flexes (step two), wells (step three), the observer's view (step four).
- **Sources people choose, and the water they use, as built (2026-10-10, M6a slice AY, step two, ADR-0021 §1):** no new ADR. **Measured first:** with walks taken from each settlement's hearth (the routing field is the settlement's since slice AL), founding bands settle 0–89 m from a river or lake on eight river-valley seeds, and a spring could rise nearer than the river at only two of them, so springs nearer than the river will be rare until something besides the walk weighs (suspicion, slice BA). **Sources:** the settlement's routing field keeps, besides the nearest cell at fresh water, every patch's lowest ground off the water that it reaches nearer than that cell, nearest first; when a person weighs fetching water, the nearest of those whose patch is a spring today (`Water::spring_at`: the head at its lowest ground, at least `spring_min_m3_day` flowing to it) and has a load left today is the place, else the river or lake. **A spring gives what flows to it in a day:** loads drawn at each spring are counted for the day (`SpringDraws`, saved in the places section, let go at the day's turn), and once less than a load is left nobody sets off for it; someone already on the way still draws. **Draws are uses:** every load is logged as a use of its source (`Place::Source`, the cell people stand on), so households know where they draw and whom they meet there; no polity may claim a source (`Place::claimable`), so it raises no issue of outsiders and no trespass. **Use follows the walk** (content API 70, the people profile's `water_use_by_walk_min`): each household keeps the litres each of its people use a day, set when water is brought home from the walk to where it was drawn, interpolated on `[[15, 20], [30, 10]]` (one-way minutes, litres), a tuning curve within research 12-01 §2.1's prior of 10–30 L for carried water and its benchmarks (up to 20 L at 5–30 minutes of collection, often under 5 L beyond); every use of a household's water rate reads it. Saves 73 (`Household.water_use_l`, 0 for the profile's; the places section's draws). **Tested:** a spring made nearer than the hearth's river (seed 6) gives its 500 L to the first 33 loads and the rest of the village goes to the river, households then using 20 L, and the next day it gives again (the draw cap checked by breaking it: 690 L were drawn); a spring whose head stands a metre below its ground is left for the river; draws at a spring save, load and go on alike for a day; the curve's interpolation; sources are never claimable. **Measured:** forty days of the village of 1,000 lived on from one schema-71 save by step one's build and this one differ only in the content, the households (their water rate) and the places (draws logged), at the same speed (825 ms a day); a year of three villages of 100 drew 149,072 and 151,149 loads from rivers on seeds 1 and 2 and none from springs, and on seed 3 98,957 loads from three springs and 49,972 from rivers; no household anywhere walked far enough to use less than 20 L. **Not yet:** wells (step three), the observer's view (step four).
- **Wells, as built (2026-10-10, M6a slice AY, step three, ADR-0021 §1 and §3):** no new ADR. **A well is its own record** (`civ_land::wells`), as a crossing is: the ground its digging took (no plot, field, pit or heap may be laid over it), its depth and target, the labour and skill put into it, its lining's quality and the share rot has taken, the water standing in its shaft and when, what was drawn from it since midnight, and its state (being dug, open, given up, fallen in); saves 74 (a wells section; target kind 15, chronicle kind 45). **Content API 71** brings well systems as a content kind: the core's timber-lined well is dug 1.5 m across and lined 1.2 m inside (research 12-01 §2.2), the digging at the profile's 8 h a cubic metre (ADR-0010 §2; 14 h a metre) and the lining at 16 h a metre (a design prior: some 19 m of split plank a metre at about a metre an hour), dug a metre below where its diggers expect water and at most 10 m deep (03-02 §2.3: dug wells of 1.5-10.1 m, prehistoric Yangtze wells mostly under 3 m), refilling over a 50 m radius of influence (03-02 §1.4), hauled up at 0.15 minutes a metre of lift (a design prior), its lining rotting 0.05 a year on ground of wetness 1 (a design prior beside 11-07 §2.4's 10-20 years for untreated log bridges), with room for two to work. Founders know well digging (12-01 §3.1: timber-lined wells in the early Neolithic), learned at the age of the work; work on a well is gated by it, so the young learn beside a knower. *Found while measuring:* nobody knew the technique at first, because founders learn each technique at the age of the work it gates and nothing yet counted a well system's work; `Catalog::work_age` now does, and a technique only a bridge or well system names gates work (E3005). **The yearly review** (on each household's keyed day, one well's work at a time): beside its home plot, on the nearest claimable ground in front of it, a well a metre below the water level its people expect, from the water standing in the nearest well of its settlement, or below the floor of one given up dry (its depth becomes what others expect), or else the water they draw from now (the lowest water beside the bank, or the spring's ground); begun when the walk from home to that water and back, and its lift, less the walk to the well and its lift, for every load its people use, over the years its lining would last at the quality its people would lay it, is more than the hours to dig and line it. A household whose lining rot has taken half of what its quality left weighs relining it the same way (the lining's hours), and its quality is drawn again. **The work:** a session as on a crossing, but each hour weighed by the walking a year of the well would save for it. Counted over the lining's whole life, as for crossings, a session of digging outscored hunger (40-120 points against hunger's 20 at most), and in a probe people dug while their stores ran down; a year's return sets it beside useful work (a design prior, with the store review's three-year horizon as precedent). At its target a shaft meets water where the water table stands above its floor, its lining's quality drawn from its diggers' average skill (ADR-0009 §6), or it is dug a metre deeper, or at the deepest given up; the chronicle tells each. **Water:** an open well's column refills toward the water table of its patch at Thiem's rate over the column (03-02 §1.4); a load takes from the column, or what it holds of one; people draw at the well that takes least time there and back with its lift, among their own, their close kin's households' and, while their household holds less than half a day's water, anyone's (ADR-0021 §1, rights v0; the half day is a design prior), when that is no longer than the spring or bank; what each well gave is taken from the water table under it at midnight (patches of open water excepted). **Rot:** monthly, by the habitat's wetness and the month's rain against its usual, as for posts' feet; a lining whose rot has taken all its quality left falls in. Wells pass with a household's plots and buildings. **Tested:** the column's refill, its never falling below the floor and 03-02's 2.26 m³ in 2 m; a household beside its home plot weighing and beginning one, then busy, its people digging it in sessions no larger than its crew; meeting water where the table stands above the floor; a dry shaft deepened to the deepest and given up, after which every well begun expects water below its floor; drawing at one's own well, and a twin with nothing drawn holding the water table exactly that much higher at the next midnight and the same elsewhere; rights for strangers, the short of water and kin; relining past half and falling in; saves exact before and after a day; the session's weight in a decision unit test; the content kind's ranges. **Measured** (three villages of 100 on the river-valley preset, two years, seeds 1-3): 44, 72 and 62 wells begun by about 70 households each, 25, 59 and 55 of them open by the second year's end, none given up or fallen in yet (the river's level, which households expect with no well to go by, lies below the water table under their homes); open wells 1.0-8.5 m deep, medians 5.3, 4.3 and 1.5 m (03-02 §2.3's sample: 1.5-10.1 m, mean 4.8), 54-115 hours of work each on average; wells gave a sixth, a half and a half of all loads drawn over the two years. One of seed 1's settlements, its homes 28-30 m above its river, digs none: its people expect water too deep. Without the lift, and with each session counting the lining's life, it was 45, 72 and 71 wells. *Not established:* seed 2's Stonebank has a famine in its second spring with or without wells; on six runs without them it had 71-96 people at day 511 (mean 84), on five with them 62-94 (mean 72; identities differ run to run). Its harvest, farm hours and the day its food ran short were the same in both, and well work was about 1 % of hours, so the difference is not labour taken from food; it is to be watched in longer runs. **Speed:** forty days of the village of 1,000 lived on from the same schema-71 save as step two took 855 ms a day, against 806 ms by step two's build on the same machine (+6 %; wells give it 0.022 hours of work a person-day by then). Choosing a well first passes over others' wells that could not beat the household's other water at the fastest walk, before anyone's kin is looked up; that changed no choice (the same digests in every section) and took little off. The rest is mostly new trips, to wells, to route. **A flake found on the way, older than wells:** `a_watcher_chooses_once_what_to_do_with_a_taking_they_saw` (`tests/crime.rs`) fails about once in 30-40 runs (1 of 40 on step two's build, 1 of 30 on this one) at `law.watch.cases == brought` (5 against 6). Its world draws a fresh identity each run (`Sim::create`), so only some histories reach it; the watch's count of the cases it brought can miss one its officer brought. Open: find the cause in the watch's bookkeeping. **Not yet:** the observer's view of sources and wells (step four); covers, curbs and silt (slice BA, with contamination); a polity's well by law (slice BA); pits that met water as knowledge.
- **The observer's view of water, as built (2026-10-10, M6a slice AY, step four, ADR-0021 §1-§3):** no new ADR. **Wire 1.61:** the snapshot carries a water revision, which changes whenever a well is begun, dug deeper, opens, rots, is relined, given up or falls in, and with each day lived while the world has wells or ground where a spring may rise (0 when neither), so the observer reads the water again when a well changes and otherwise once a day; `GetWater` lists every well (its system, centre and dug radius, state, depth and target, the water standing in its shaft now and how far down, its lining's quality and rot, its household, and all that in words: "Ada's household's timber-lined well, open since year 2: 4.1 m deep, 1.8 m of water standing 2.3 m down; rot has taken a tenth of its lining"), every spring flowing today with what flows to it and what was drawn there today, the cells at a river's or lake's edge drawn at today with how many times people came there, and the rivers' flow today against their mean; the person inspector gains where their household went for water today, from the day's logged draws ("Their household went for water 6 times today: 4 times to its own well, twice to the water's edge; each of them uses 20 L a day, and it holds 85 L"). The water standing in a well is read through `Land::ground_water`, the one rule the kernel's wells use, so the observer cannot drift from the world. **The map** draws each shaft at its dug radius (never under about two pixels): fresh earth fainter the shallower it still is against its target, blue while water stands in it, brown once given up dry, dark once fallen in; springs as small blue dots, brighter when drawn at today; the places drawn at the water's edge as pale dots by how often; the readout names the one under the pointer, and over a river or lake says how it flows today ("flowing at 40% of its mean today"). Nothing is drawn as coverage: the Water lens, with *could reach* and *did come*, is slice BB's. **The dashboard's water row** is grey, reported and not graded: loads drawn over the run by the kind of place (counted as drawn, not saved: `Population.water_draws`), wells at the end by state, and the litres people use a day; what each uses follows the walk on a tuning curve set inside research 12-01 §2.1's prior of 10-30 L, so that prior cannot judge it. **Tested:** the host reads the water with no world (an error), a new world's (no wells, a flow), and a well and draws set by hand (the well's state, system, water standing and words; two trips at one bank cell; the inspector's line in order of trips); the web decodes the water and builds its query, and draws, finds and words wells, springs and banks; end to end, a world lived 60 days serves the water to the map and the inspector's line to a selected person; the row's words and totals. **Measured:** the dashboard lived its five worlds two years at 512 cells (a quick look, not the dashboard): 432,333 loads, 37 % from wells (2 % of them short of a load), 3 % at springs, 60 % at the water's edge; 71 wells open and 19 being dug at the end, none given up or fallen in; every household's people using 20 L a day, as step two found (nobody walks far enough to use less); the dashboard passed in 72 s. **Slice AY is complete.** **Not yet:** coverage of water per home (the Water lens, slice BB); what people know of others' draws and suspect of a source (slice BA).
- **The save-and-load flake, found (2026-10-08):** a rare failure of the save-and-load continuation tests (about 1 run in 100, first seen in `tests/values.rs` during slice AH) was reproduced by living one world (river valley, seed 3, 512 cells) forty days from 200 fixed identities: two continued differently a day after a load, and only in decision receipts. Traced, one step at a time: the chosen option and every listed total matched, but the chosen option's probability did not; of all the options, breaking new ground scored differently, at another site. A household's candidate new ground is found once a day and kept in a cache the save does not hold. A world lived straight on reused the site found earlier in the day; one loaded found it afresh after a neighbour had marked out a field or plot, so its search saw other land. The cached site is now kept only while what the search reads (the fields, plots and earthworks laid out, and the homes and hearth it keeps clear of) is unchanged. A household therefore also no longer holds to a site that a neighbour's new field has since covered. Afterwards 600 identities (400 of seed 3, 200 of seed 4) continued alike. **Recorded, not changed:** the cache of the building a household would begin (`home_sites`) has the same shape and also reads the household's stores, which change all day; no world showed it, and keying it on its inputs would undo the cache.
- **Revolts, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AI step one, ADR-0017 §4):** no new ADR (ADR-0017 §4 decided that a revolt replaces the body through officeholders' own choices and has no roll). **The program:** a faction whose members blame the gathering holds one: of the bodies the content's amendment templates offer that differ from the custom and admit its organizer, the one under which the decisions its members' households were at, under the present custom, would have gone most their way, at each household's stake in each decision (slice AF's test, research 09-02 §3.7; a faction with nothing to gain from any body has no program). Unlike an amendment it may be any body, not only one change away. **Calling** (ADR-0017 §4): at the organizer's monthly review, when neither a petition nor a refusal is called, and when nothing of the faction's own waits or stands, no revolt stands at its settlement and none of its own was called within `petition_days`, its organizer weighs calling on everyone to stand with that body in place of the gathering's. It is worth what a petition is, less `revolt_cost` 0.75 and less what the norms the organizer holds weigh, since the custom itself is what it breaks. **Word** goes from the organizer to its members, then by word of mouth (a fifth claim kind, `revolt`, let go when it ends), and the chronicle records the call. **Sides** (research 04-10 §5.3, 09-11 §2.5: in a crisis people decide daily): each midnight, each adult of the settlement who has heard of it takes a side: with it, with the gathering, or with neither, from their grievance against the gathering, belonging to the faction or else regard for its organizer, the share of those they know who stood with it the day before (for some, more adds less), less what the norms they hold weigh and `w_exclusion` 1.0 for one the new body would leave out (09-11 §1.6); a side needs the points past the gathering's stance margin. **Holding** (09-11 §1.5, §2.2): it holds once the store's keeper and the watch, if any, stand with it and more adults stand with it than with the gathering, for `hold_days` 7 together; then its body decides from now on, a version of the custom *taken* (its `seized_by` names the organizer, no law made it), any amendment in force is superseded, and the chronicle says those who stood with the organizer took the deciding from the gathering, not by its procedure. It comes to nothing when its organizer or faction is gone, or after `revolt_days` 60. Losers keep their wealth, ties and grievances; nothing else changes hands. **The first prior was wrong, as the refusal's was:** with `revolt_cost` 1.0 an organizer all of whose members held the grievance fully, where nobody held the norm, was 0.65 against a threshold of 0.78; at 0.75 (above a refusal's 0.5) it is 0.82. **A finding from the test:** the first test village, with a decision lately gone against the faction, amended its custom by its own procedure (to two-thirds) before the organizer's review, and the old decision then no longer counted, so no revolt was called: the legal route came first, as it should. The test instead gives the village an elders' custom, under which no body one change away would have decided otherwise; there the organizer called a revolt, every one of the 27 who heard of it stood with it, and it held a week later, making a gathering of all the adults, half of whom must come. A second test lets a revolt's time run out: it comes to nothing and the elders' custom stands. Both save and load exactly. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds** on the first run, with no revolt called: the gathering binds is held about 0.55 on average there, and of the three villages with factions, all petitioned (four petitions, all granted). **Boundary:** saves schema 44 (each custom version's `seized_by`; revolts with their sides in `factions`; 43 still loads), wire 1.43 (each polity's revolts in the Government panel, in kernel words; a version of the custom taken is told so in its history), content API 48 (`revolt_cost`, `revolt_days`, `hold_days`, `w_exclusion`). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** founding after a revolt (the new body deciding which laws and offices stand: step two), a watch of several and coups (step three), and force, damage and violence (step four); an organizer weighing whether others will stand with it before calling (they weigh only their faction's grievance and belonging).
- **M4b design (2026-10-08, plan §7):** from the M4 crime brief, its citations checked against 04-09, 12-04, 09-07 and 09-09. One ADR, [ADR-0015](decisions/0015-incidents-cases-obligations.md): incidents (the kernel's truth), cases (what a polity knows) and beliefs (what people know) are kept and saved apart, and no choice reads an incident; taking is a scored choice behind a moral filter; every sanction is an obligation with a debtor, a beneficiary and a default rule. Four slices, AA to AD (§7). The brief's open questions, answered: **a summary decision by the gathering or an office counts as "no courts"** (plan §7; cases are shaped so that M7 can put a forum between identified and sanctioned). **No corporal punishment or execution** in v0 (settled in the M4a design). **The watch is an office named by a law**, as the storekeeper is: its holders keep watch unpaid, since office pay is not built; a rotating household duty waits for it. **Releasing the store's grain** is relief, built in M4a. **People take only from other households' stores** in v0: not from their own household, standing crops or workshops; whether kin are spared is left to the objection and regard, not a rule. **Sanctions are weighed in days of the household's food**, the scale `Ask` and the levy already use. **A prohibition needs no new good:** a law can close a place or an activity, such as a grove for firewood (AD). The briefs are now kept in the repository (`docs/briefs/`), since the scratch copies were the only ones.
- **Theft and what was seen, as built (2026-10-08, M4b slice AA, ADR-0015 §1–§3, §5, §7):** **Taking** is `Behavior::Take` (appended; `core:activity/take_food`, from 12), weighed only when the household is short, like `Ask`: the food a load would carry against the walk, the person's objection (20 points for a full one), the chance they believe a taker runs of being seen times 10 points (scaled by `exp(−0.3 × risk)` for their risk-taking) and 5 points for full regard for the household's elder. An objection of 0.8 or more rules it out before anything is weighed, with its own exclusion in the receipt (04-09 §5.3). **Objection** is a per-person value, its log-odds drawn as N(2.5, 1.2) for founders (about one in six falls below the filter) and pulled toward the parents' for a child with heritability 0.5, each by a keyed draw of its own so a birth's other draws are unchanged. **The perceived chance of being seen** starts at 0.5 and moves as p̂ ← (1 − α)p̂ + αs: α = 0.3 from one's own attempts (seen or turned back 1, unseen 0) and 0.1 from what one hears (word of a taker 1; a loss found that nobody saw 0). Research 04-09 §5.4 gives no figures for any of this: all are tuning values. **The target** is found as asking finds a giver: the households of the settlement within reach from which a load would carry the most food it wants, the nearest after a walk weighed against regard. **Arrival settles guardianship** (04-09 §1.1, 12-04 §1.4): a member of 10 or more home and awake turns the taker back; so does anyone of 10 or more within 30 m whom the taker notices (0.7 each); a grown sleeper at home wakes with chance 0.25 and the taker flees, seen; otherwise they carry off what they can on a `take` channel, and those about whom they did not notice, and younger children, see them. One who turned back or fled waits 6 to 18 hours (drawn) before weighing it again. **Incidents** (the truth: who, from whom, when, how it ended, what was carried off, who saw) are saved and no choice reads one. **Beliefs** are saved per holder with their source (saw, told, found the loss) and the witness the account began with, so several people repeating one witness are one source. A household finds a taking at the next midnight; a witness tells the household taken from unless they are of the taker's household or regard the taker more than its elder (12-04 §1.2); members share what they know; and at the hearth people tell what they know of takers outside their own household. Beliefs are kept two years. Four tie acts (`took_from_us`, bad provision 3; `took_from_others`, 1; `restored`, good word 1; `refused_restitution`, bad word 2). **A household refuses the asks** of someone it believes took from it, or from a household whose elder a member regards at 0.5 or more. **Responses:** a household that learns who took from it lets it go or demands the food back (a logistic: a point per day of its food taken, less 3 for full regard for the taker); a demand is an obligation due in 30 days, which the taker's household answers once (a logistic: 4 for the share of its settlement's households that believe the taker took, 2 for regard for the household owed, less 0.5 a day of its own food paying costs) and pays from food beyond three days' need each midnight, on a `restitution` channel. It ends met, refused, unpaid when due (the rest an arrear; an award never creates goods) or lapsed. The chronicle tells a taking or an attempt someone saw, and how a demand ended, plainly (06-10 §4). The Takings panel (wire 1.30) shows the latest 60 incidents as what happened beside what people believe and chose, in separate tables joined only by the incident's number. **Deviations from ADR-0015, recorded:** an objection is not yet moved by what the person lives beyond their upbringing (04-09 §1.1's social learning beyond the household waits for M4c's opinion and norms); telling is a rule of ties, not a scored choice, and reporting waits for slice AB, so a household taken from chooses only between letting it go and a demand; and a loss is found at the next midnight rather than at the next use of the store. **Found on the way:** in the first lean-spell probe the taker turned back only for a member at home, so takings made in the evening, with the household at the hearth 20 m off, were "seen" by 33 to 38 people, and the same few people went back to a guarded store dozens of times a day. Research 12-04 §1.4 (an offender may notice a guardian and abandon, or change their timing) and 04-09 §1.1 (capable guardians) gave noticing, the wait and the guardian's age. With a fixed 12-hour wait every retry fell at dawn and in the evening, when households are up, and none of about 170 attempts in 30 days succeeded; the wait is now drawn. **Measured:** in a test's shared shortage (river valley 4 at 512 cells: half the households with nothing, the rest six days' food, every other hungry adult below the filter), one 30-day run saw about 190 attempts by 8 people and at least 4 takings, the rest turned back; takings were seen by neighbours and children; 3 households learnt who took and 2 demanded the food back, unpaid because the takers' households had nothing to spare. The integration test checks the moral filter, that goods are conserved, that every belief naming a taker traces to someone who saw it, that a demand meant to be paid is paid from what can be spared, and an exact save and load. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds**, twice. On the final build seven had no attempt to take. On the coast, seed 2's village (24 adults) saw 18 attempts by 3 people and 5 takings (82 kg), each seen and known to the household taken from: 5 demands to give it back, 4 met, and 1 ask refused to a taker. Seed 3's 10 attempts by 4 people brought one taking of 3 kg, seen and demanded back; and seed 5's struggling village (11 adults) made 26 attempts by 6 people, none taken. An earlier build's run, with the same rules, had 6, 9 and 17 attempts on three coast worlds and no taking (worlds draw their own identity each run, so runs differ). Gate B passed (20 runs in 1.6 minutes), with no attempt to take in either fixture's third year; takings are reported by run and not graded, since a mean's relative tolerance cannot hold a rare event. The notables' gate passed with the same figures as at M4a. **What it does not show:** the fed villages of the smoke and the gates scarcely take, and the chain from a taking to a demand paid has been seen in one smoke world; whether a lean year in a village of 1,000 makes takings common, or merely makes attempts common, is open.
- **Cases and their decision, as built (2026-10-08, M4b slice AB, ADR-0015 §4–§5):** **The law against taking** is a policy template (`does = "against_taking"`, `core:policy/against_taking`) answering a new issue, `takings` (a household of the settlement found food taken from its store within the year), with sanction bundles people propose: compensation to the household taken from and a fine to the common store, each in days of the taker's household's food, and exile (research 09-07 §1.2 keeps restitution, compensation and a fine apart; §6.2: a bundle, never one severity number; §2.3: burdens in days of household consumption). The core pack has four: restitution alone; three days' compensation; three and a three-day fine; and that with exile. All amounts are tuning values. **The forecast reads no incident:** a household weighs what it would recover (what it lost in the past year to takings whose taker one of its members knows, and the bundle's compensation for each) against what it would owe (what its members took, with compensation, the fine and, for exile, 60 days of its food for the member it would lose, `exile_days`), at the mean chance its grown members believe a taker runs of being seen; the gain is the common store's log-utility forecast of the one less the other. For this a taker now knows what they took (a belief with the new source `did`, which is no account of it) and a household knows what it found missing or brought home (`KnownAmount`, saved). **Households taken from since the last review join those a review reaches** (ADR-0014 §4 extended, recorded here: being taken from reaches a household as running short does), so their elders may propose it. **Bringing a case:** where such a law is in force and the household's chooser knows of it (09-07 §4.1: knowing of a forum is one of access's barriers), a household that learns who took from it chooses by a softmax among letting it go (0 points), a demand (slice AA's points) and a case, worth p̂ × V − C less slice AA's 3 points for full regard for the taker: p̂ = 1 − 0.5^accounts, the chance it believes the gathering would find, from the distinct witnesses its members' beliefs come from (12-04 §1.5: several people repeating one witness are one observation); V a point for each day of food to recover, the bundle's compensation included; C 1 point. Without the law the choice is slice AA's, draw for draw. One whose demand was refused, or unpaid when due, weighs a case after, against letting it go (09-07 §1.1: negotiation can fail and the dispute go on to a forum). The choice now reads what the household found missing and what its members believe, not the incident (slice AA's read the incident's taker and amount, which then always equalled what the household knew). **A case is what the polity knows:** who brought it, whom it accuses, the loss as the household found it, the witnesses behind it, its stage, and the hearing whole; it never reads the incident it is about. **The hearing:** a case goes before the gathering already called, or one called on cases alone for the next evening. Each who came stands for a finding or against it: the one who brought it for, the accused against; everyone else by what they believe (1 point toward a finding if they believe the accused took; otherwise the accounts told there, 2 × (1 − 0.5^leads) − 1 points, so one witness is an even chance and none counts a point against), what their household stands to gain or lose (a point a day of its food, the scale of a demand), and the polity's `w_regard` (1) for the one who brought it less that for the accused, against the 0.25 margin. The body's rule decides: found; not found (turned down or tied); unheard (too few came); or lapsed if a party was gone before it sat. A wrong decision is recorded as made and never repaired. Those who came to a finding then believe it (told, by the one who brought it, from its first witness). **What a finding imposes:** restitution (the loss less what a demand already brought), compensation and a fine to the polity's store, obligations on the accused's household due in 30 days, answered once for the whole finding (slice AA's points for paying, and 2 more for the custom that the gathering binds) and paid in that order from food beyond three days' need, on the `restitution`, `compensation` and `fine` channels; an award never creates goods. Under a bundle that exiles, the one found leaves the valley at once, a migration on their record, not a disappearance (ADR-0015 §5); their household stays and owes what was imposed. **Deviations from ADR-0015, recorded:** the gathering hears every case (no law yet gives the power to an office); labour-days are not built, since no polity work exists to owe; refusing or failing to pay what a finding imposed has no sanction beyond the arrear and the tie acts until a watch exists (slice AC); and exile is carried out by the gathering that found, enforcement by the coalition 09-07 §1.1 says it needs, rather than an obligation the exiled may refuse. **Found on the way:** the hearing first put each household's stake in the common store's forecast, which is flat below a year's subsistence, so in a lean spell every party stood to gain or lose nothing; it is now days of food at a demand's scale, which matters most to those with least. The slice AA integration test had failed about one run in 24, when a lean spell brought no taking in 60 days (guardianship works: one probe saw 58 to 94 attempts and at most one taking in 120 days); both crime tests now set their situation explicitly through a test hook on the rules (the AA test: a taker notices nobody about; the cases test: nobody is a capable guardian either, and a household taken from brings a case), and passed 12 runs of 12. **Measured:** in the cases test's situation (river valley 4, 512 cells, its harshest bundle), two runs saw 5 and 6 takings in about 60 days, all seen and all brought as cases; the gathering, which 13 to 17 of the village's adults came to, found against every accused it heard (10 to 14 for, 2 to 4 against, the opposition the takers' own households), sent each from the valley, and their households refused, or began to pay, what was imposed; a second case against one already exiled lapsed. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds**, three times. In the first, no attempt to take succeeded anywhere (7, 12 and 28 attempts on three coast worlds), so nothing of this slice arose. In the second, three coast villages each saw one taking, seen and demanded back (one met, two refused), and each passed one law more than its common store and storekeepers account for; the report did not yet name laws against taking, and now does. In the third, three coast villages passed a law against taking after a seen taking, two with restitution alone and one with three days' compensation and a three-day fine. In seed 2's village (26 adults) three takings were seen and demanded back (two met) and one was brought as a case, found, and its restitution paid; in seed 5's struggling village (4 people at the end) one of two takings was brought as a case and found, and the household paid restitution, compensation and the fine; in seed 3's, the household taken from let its one known taking go. No world proposed exile. **Gates:** Gate B passed (20 runs in 1.9 minutes, no attempt to take in either fixture), and the notables' gate passed with its M4a figures (on the coast fixture 3.8 proposals with the tier against 4.0 without), with households taken from now among those a review reaches. **What it does not show:** whether exile is ever chosen, and how often cases come where takings are common (a lean year in a village of 1,000); the smoke's villages take a few times a decade, so each step of the chain has been seen in one or two worlds.
- **The watch and corruption v0, as built (2026-10-08, M4b slice AC, ADR-0015 §6):** **The watch is an office named by a law**, as the storekeeper is: a `keep_watch` policy (`core:policy/keep_watch`) answering `takings`, whose sponsor names the adult they regard most (themselves at full regard). A household weighs it by half of all it found missing in the past year, whoever took it, back to it, against as much of what its own members took (`watch_guard` 0.5, a tuning value; research 12-04 §1.3: households set up a specialization when they expect it to protect their stores). A law now carries its template's kind, set when proposed or loaded and not saved, so the storekeeper's office and the watch are told apart without the catalog; any office lapses when its holder dies or leaves, and the chronicle says which. The office is unpaid (office pay is not built) and has one holder. **Rounds are a chosen activity** (`core:activity/keep_watch`, behaviour `watch`): only for the holder, only in the dark, a set path through the settlement's homes in id order beginning where the last round ended, a 15-minute stand at each of six (12-04 §2.3's 11–15 minute visits, a modern patrol finding, used only as a scale), worth 6 points for the night's first round and less for each since, up to three a night (12-04 §2.1: one account, medium-low confidence). Sleep pressure ends a watcher's night: a test village's watcher walked about 1.7 rounds a night. **Standing at a home the watcher is someone about**, so the guardianship slice AA built does the rest: a taker who notices them turns back, and one who does not is seen. Nothing else makes a watch effective (12-04 §1.4: a patrol affects the places it visits and those who observe it; §2.3: no linear patrol effect). The watch's rounds, hours and the cases it brought are kept on its law, saved, and shown in the Government panel; nothing in the world reads them (12-04 §2.4: capacity is a reported measure). **A watcher who sees a taking chooses once what to do with it** (research 09-09 §1.3: ΔU = B + K − C − M − p̂(F + V)), by a softmax: saying nothing is 0 points; bringing it before the gathering under a law against taking they know of (or, with none, telling those taken from) is 2 points of duty and slice AA's 3 points for full regard for the household taken from, less as much for regard for the taker; and, under such a law, asking the taker's household for three days of its food (at most what it can spare beyond three days' need) to say nothing is worth a point a day of the watcher's own household's food, less 20 points for a full objection to taking what is not theirs (never weighed above the moral filter: asking is taking) and 10 for a certainty of being found out, the same p̂ they hold of takers being seen. The household asked pays (4 points against half a point a day of its food) or refuses, and is then brought before the gathering. A payment moves on a `bribe` channel; a watcher who looked away or was paid keeps their belief to themselves, at home and at the hearth; an unexposed payment lowers their sense of being found out (09-09 §1.5: learning). What a watcher chose is kept as truth (`sightings`, saved) and shown only in the Takings panel's "what happened" layer. **Deviations from ADR-0015 and the brief, recorded:** the watch is one person, not a roster with a backlog (cases wait for the gathering, and the open ones are its backlog); a watcher's silence is never exposed (no audit, rival or victim learns of it in v0, so p̂ only falls), and no office has a removal rule; the collector's diversion (the brief's second corrupt office) is not built; and asking is open only under a law against taking, since without a case there is nothing to spare the taker. **Found on the way:** with no objection to taking, a test's watcher also took, once their household had given its food away to hungry neighbours, and was found and sent from the valley, which ended the watch; the payment test keeps its watcher on watch with a bundle that does not exile. A starving taker's household has nothing to pay with by the midnight a watcher chooses, so a payment is weighed only where it can spare food; and a watcher who has heard of many takings believes being found out likely, so even one without objection usually says nothing rather than ask (about −2 points against 0 in one probe). **Measured:** in the cases test's situation with a watch and a law against taking, the watcher saw 2 to 4 takings and brought each before the gathering (asking scored about −12 for a watcher of ordinary objection); the payment test (a watcher without objection or fear of being found out, and takers' households kept with two days' food) asks, is paid on the `bribe` channel with every good accounted for, or is refused and brings the case; 10 runs of 10 for the six crime tests. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds**. In one (coast seed 3) the village passed four laws: a common store, a storekeeper, a watch and a law against taking with three days' compensation and a three-day fine. Its watcher walked 4,924 rounds over the decade, 7,386 hours of standing watch (about 1.5 rounds and 2.2 hours a night), saw the one taking that succeeded there, and brought it before the gathering, which found against the taker; nothing it imposed was paid by the decade's end. No other world named a watch. Run times were as before (137 to 192 seconds a world). **Gates:** Gate B passed (20 runs in 1.1 minutes, no attempt to take in either fixture) and the notables' gate passed with its M4a figures. **What it does not show:** whether a watch changes how often takers succeed: in the one smoke village that named one, a single taking came in its years of rounds.
- **Curfews, the crime row and the M4b demo, as built (2026-10-08, M4b slice AD):** **A curfew is a prohibition in the law pipeline** (the M4 crime brief §1.6: "nobody may be off their own plot in set hours"): a `curfew` policy (`core:policy/curfew`) answering `takings`, at the hours its template offers (21:00 to 5:00 and 23:00 to 4:00, tuning values), each a level a sponsor weighs. A household weighs it as a watch, at `curfew_guard` 0.3 of all it found missing in the past year back to it and as much of what its own members took (smaller than a watch's 0.5: nobody stands at the stores), less `curfew_cost_days` 0.25 of its food a year for each hour the curfew runs and each grown member it keeps at home (tuning values; the research gives no figure for what a curfew costs or stops). **Keeping it is each person's choice** (research 09-06 §1.5): under a curfew in force, someone who knows of it weighs any option that would take them off their home's plot (a walk beyond 20 m of home, or work where they already stand that far off; a walk home never) in its hours against keeping it, with the levy's terms: the custom that a gathering binds (`comply_base` 1.5), where they stood on it and their regard for its sponsor, never below nothing. The watch at its rounds and those at a gathering are exempt. The law counts the times it was broken by those who knew of it and by those who did not, saved and shown in the Government panel (wire 1.33). **Deviations, recorded:** breaking a curfew carries no sanction in v0, and the watch neither stops nor reports those out at night (the brief's enforcement): a case for a curfew's breach would need the gathering to hear more than takings. **Closing a place** (a grove for firewood, a fishing ground; §9's M4b design) **is not built:** fallen wood grows at a constant rate in the content, so resting a grove yields nothing more, and for game and fish, which regrow logistically, a household could not forecast a closure without a record of what it gathers, which is not kept; a template nobody could weigh would be inert vocabulary. **The dashboard's crime row** (plan §4.7, "crime correlates positively with poverty"): every attempt to take now records where the taker's household stood by food when they came, its days of food and the share of its settlement's other households holding more (ties counting half), saved with the incident (schema 34; NaN in older saves) and never read by any choice. The row pools every attempt of the five worlds and grades the mean share by direction only, green above one half (the poorer take), red at or below, grey under 30 attempts (tuning: about a standard error of 0.05); takings, those seen and those brought as cases are shown beside it and not graded, since better detection raises recorded crime (12-04 §4.B). The smoke reports the same share for each world. **The Takings panel** now carries every taking and every attempt someone saw before the newest unseen attempts that turned back (60 in all): a lean spell brings dozens of those, which had pushed the takings that came to a case out of the panel. **The M4b demo** (`web/e2e/m4b-demo.spec.ts`, with `TCE_DEMO=1`): civ-sim's `theft_world` example makes river valley seed 4 at 512 cells, lives its band a week, then empties the food stores of every other household by id and leaves the rest six days of food (test scaffolding, as `workshop_world` is; everything after is people's choice), and lives it until a case has been found and all it imposed is settled, or 240 days. Five runs of one world gave one such finding (in 51 days); the rest saw 63 to 74 attempts, nearly all turned back, and at most one taking. So the example makes up to twelve worlds (each draws its own identity), saves the first where a finding settled, or the last, and says which. In the recorded demo the third world did, after 53 days: of 60 attempts one taking succeeded, Wendel's of 11 kg of meat, seen by eight people; the household taken from demanded it back; Senna proposed a law against taking with three days' compensation, which the gathering passed 2 for and none against, 23 of 23 adults coming and the rest abstaining; Hal brought the taking before the gathering on two witnesses' word, and it found against Wendel 6 to 2 with 8 of 9 adults there. The demand went unpaid when due, and the restitution and compensation the finding imposed lapsed when the household taken from was no more. The same village also passed a watch, a common store at a twentieth and a curfew from 23:00 to 4:00 (2 for, 1 against). **Measured:** a curfew everyone knows keeps people in: run through a working day (9:00 to 17:00, a test's hours, since in the template's night hours nearly everyone is asleep at home), a village under it had 175 samples of someone away from home against 662 without, and 103 breaches by people who knew of it; in the template's hours a village of seed 5 had 5 away against 5, all at the hearth when the hour struck. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds.** Three coast villages (seeds 2, 3 and 5) each passed a watch, a law against taking and a curfew from 21:00 to 5:00 after seeing takings; their curfews were broken 720, 1,655 and 1,218 times over the decade by people who knew of them, nobody unaware. The breaches are likely summer dawn work before 5:00 and summer evenings at the hearth past 21:00, but which activities they were was not checked. Their attempts came from the poorer: 0.68, 0.80 and 0.73 of the takers' neighbours held more food (8, 6 and 28 attempts; 0.73 over the 42 together); no other world tried to take. The watches walked 4,780 to 5,860 rounds and saw no taking. **The fifty-year dashboard** (on the build with the crime row and before the curfew, 32 minutes on four cores): population, food prices and firm sizes green, the Gini of goods amber (0.23-0.31); **crime and poverty grey: not one attempt to take in five valley worlds over fifty years**, whose villages stay fed; **structural failures red**, 11 in 4,484 building-years (2.45 per 1,000 against at most 2). Over the seven recorded runs the counts were 11, 5, 7, 9, 2 and 11 (1.65 per 1,000 pooled), so the threshold sits inside the row's spread by chance; nothing was tuned or rerun. **Gates:** Gate B passed (1.2 minutes) and the notables' gate passed with M4a's figures. **Found on the way:** a pre-existing flaky test (`a_band_sows_reaps_and_threshes_its_first_harvest`) failed about 1 run in 12, on the build before this slice as on this one (2 of 24 each), claiming that every household keeps the seed to sow all its fields. Since slice V a household may break ground after the harvest for next year, planned against the seed for what it means to crop (it may rest some), and seed moves with those who leave a household for another; the failing household had cropped 0.75 ha, broken 0.75 ha more and kept 135 kg of seed. The model was right and the claim too strong: the test now claims that the village keeps the seed to sow again the ground it cropped (48 runs of 48). Keeping a household's field seed from those who leave it was tried and dropped: a new household then had fields and no seed for them. **What it does not show:** whether curfews change how often takers succeed (the smoke's villages took once a decade with or without one), and the crime row's grade in the dashboard's own worlds, which do not go hungry.
- **M4c design (2026-10-08, plan §7):** from the M4 factions brief, its citations checked against 04-06 §1.7 and §5.3, 09-02 §3.5–3.7 and 09-11 §1.6. Two ADRs, [ADR-0016](decisions/0016-grievances-claims-opinions.md) (grievances, claims and opinions, and the interventions that touch them) and [ADR-0017](decisions/0017-factions-episodes-regime-change.md) (factions, episodes and changes of regime), and six slices, AE to AJ (§7). **Amending the custom needs no ADR:** ADR-0013 §2 already gives the gathering the power to amend its body and create offices; slice AF adds the templates, and comes early because differing regimes need a way for a village to change who decides. The brief's open questions, answered: **winners rebuild by bargaining** from the inherited constitution (09-11 §1.6), their programs (plan §5.2's "according to its ideology") being what they bring to it. **Ideology axes are the slow values**, not the whole political state: positions on issues are (06-04 §1.1). **Contacts are ADR-0014's ties**, whose number is already a content cap; no second network. **Grievance memory has two layers**, a durable claim kept while unresolved and an activation that fades and is raised only by reminders (04-06 §5.3), which reconciles the reports' 30 days to 5 years. **Whispers are true** and **an agitator is a newcomer** (both settled in the M4a design). **Bless or curse** shifts only the target's own keyed draws for material purposes (work quality, spoilage, illness), for a stated period, imperceptibly; never a decision, a tie, detection or punishment. **Coups need an office of force:** a village whose watch is one person, or none, cannot have one, recorded rather than worked around; slice AI adds a watch of several. **Leaving is already a scored choice** (M4a slice Z). **Gate B** reports factions, episodes and attendance by run and does not grade them: a rare event's mean has no stable tolerance (as for takings, slice AA). **A faction's least founding size** is a tuning value, chosen in slice AH. **What would be plot, and is not built:** no issue weighs toward a faction, an episode or a regime; no timer brings a revolt; and if fewer than two regimes arise in the demo's five seeds, that is reported (§7).
- **Claims and grievances, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AE, ADR-0016 §1–§3, §6):** **A gathering is heard of by word of mouth.** Its call is a shared claim (the settlement, the day it meets and the law before it, if any), made once; the sponsor, or whoever brought its cases, hears it first. At midnight each member of a household tells each other member of a gathering ahead with `share_home` 0.8, and companions at the hearth tell each other with `share_urgent` 0.6 (research 09-16 §2.2: 0.4–0.9 for urgent, personally relevant news); only those who heard may come. Each hearing record keeps the claim, the first and last day heard, who told and the one the account began with; a call is let go once its day has passed. Draws about a call are keyed by its settlement and day, never by the claim's number, which other claims move (ADR-0016 §6; the first build keyed them by number, and a grievance told shifted every later call's draws). Older saves give every adult word of a gathering still to sit, as before. **Grievances** (ADR-0016 §2) arise only where harm meets a law the person knows, and name the party: a household short of food that finds the common store empty holds it against its keeper, or with none the gathering (Subsistence), raised again at most every `remind_days` 7 while it stays empty; a levy taken in a lean year (the settlement's food short now or within the year, as its people have seen it, the polity's own test of a food issue) that leaves the household below a year's food, against the gathering (Extraction; 04-10 §1.1, Scott's subsistence ethic, and 04-10's "a tax may be burdensome yet accepted as customary"); a finding against one of the household whom they do not believe took (Treatment), or no finding, or too few to hear the case, of a household that believes it was taken from (Collective), against the gathering; and a finding's obligation refused, against the household that refused (Collective). The harm is in days of the household's food; relief from the store makes good grievances over its emptiness and its levy, and settles them when nothing is left. Activation fades by the issue's half-life (30, 30, 90 and 60 days; 04-06 §2.2's 7–90 for severe events) and is raised only by a reminder: new harm, or hearing it told (`reminder` 0.1). A person holds at most 8, the least keenly felt giving way. One held at `tell_floor` 0.2 or more is told at the hearth with `share_routine` 0.15 (09-16 §2.2: 0.05–0.25) and stays news `news_days` 60. **Tuned once:** `full_harm_days`, the harm felt fully, was 30 days and is 10 (tuning value): at 30 an empty store's five days of relief were felt at 0.17, under the telling floor, so no such grievance could ever be told. **Measured, and changed:** the first build's levy grievance had no lean-year test, and at year 10 of the smoke 18 to 34 people in every world held one: households seldom hold a year's food in store, so an ordinary levy counted as a wrong, which ADR-0016 §2 forbids (harm alone makes none). With the lean-year test, the ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds; only the three hungry coast villages (seeds 2, 3 and 5, which gave 209 to 328 kg of relief) held grievances at the end, 45 to 65 among 25 to 28 people, about half over food and half over the levy, none over a finding or a wrong unanswered; the valley worlds, which stay fed, held none. 92 to 99 % of the members came to gatherings on average and none lacked a quorum (an earlier run of the same build bar the lean test had 76 % and 3 without a quorum in one coast world: each run draws its own identity). Nothing reads a grievance yet: factions and episodes do (slice AH). **Not built, recorded:** news of a law passed or turned down as claims, because who knows a law already travels by contact on the law itself since M4a (households at midnight, the hearth) and copying it would move saves for no new behaviour, as ADR-0016 §3 already keeps takings' beliefs apart; news of a finding, which stays in ADR-0015's beliefs; and relief refused from a store that holds food, since the store gives what it holds and nobody refuses. **Boundary:** saves schema 35 (a `word` section; 34 still loads), wire 1.34 (a person's grievances and what they have heard, in the inspector only, ADR-0016 §7), content API 39 (the `[word]` table). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed on the build before the lean-year test, which changes only grievance records (no choice or draw reads them).
- **Amending the custom, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AF, ADR-0013 §2, ADR-0017 §1):** no new ADR: ADR-0013 §2 already lets the custom amend its body and ADR-0017 §1 asks for versions. **The body's fields became amendable values:** membership (every adult; each household's elder, its first member of an age to keep one; or the adults of households holding a field, nobody where the settlement holds the fields), the share who must come, and the rule (more for than against, or two of every three who take a side; a tie fails either way) (research 09-05 §1.4, §3.9). **The template** (`core:policy/amend_custom`, `does = "amend_body"`, content API 40) offers adults, elders or landholders, a quarter or a half, and either rule (tuning values); a sponsor weighs only the bodies one change away from the present custom and that keep them a member (09-05 §2.3: a few alternatives; §3.9: reform changes one gate at a time). **Who may propose:** the move answers a new per-person issue, `overruled`: someone who came within memory (`vote_memory_days`, 365) to a gathering that decided against where they stood, or who holds a grievance against the gathering (slice AE). **The forecast** (09-02 §3.7: institutional change is proposed for an expected benefit; 09-05 §1.2): a household judges a rule by how it would have decided the laws and cases it saw decided, re-deciding each under the new body from the stances recorded there (open acclamation: those who came saw them), at the stake the household had in each, and counting only those the new body admits now. The sponsor weighs it from what they saw; each household's stance at the gathering comes from what its own members saw. **Measured, and changed:** the first build let anyone overruled weigh four amendments of no gain, and at temperature 0.5 the softmax would pick one about three times in five (four moves at −0.5 points against none at 0, worked out, not measured): in the government e2e world a village amended its custom to landholders, 24 to 0, though every household held land and nothing changed. An amendment is now weighed only when it would have served the sponsor's own household (a positive forecast); no other move is filtered so. **Once passed** (decided by the present custom), the body's members alone come to its gatherings, stand and count toward its quorum, propose and are weighed for support; anyone may still hold an office it creates. The amendment law stays in force until a later one supersedes it (status 4, appended); the custom keeps its versions with the amendment that made each; the chronicle tells it as an amendment (a new entry kind, appended), apart from the law's decision; the label reads the share of adults the body admits. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds, and no village amended its custom**: its gatherings are nearly unanimous at about 50 people (92-100 % came, at most 2 without a quorum in one world), so few are overruled, and for them no single change would have decided otherwise what they saw. This is recorded, not tuned: amendments are vocabulary people use when the record gives them cause. **Boundary:** saves schema 36 (35 still loads: the founding custom is its only version), wire 1.35 (the custom's versions and how many may decide, in the Government panel), content API 40. **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed on the build before the positive-forecast filter, which only removes moves of no gain. **Not built:** amending who may propose (anyone in the body may), seats filled by a procedure, and unanimity or consensus rules (09-05 §1.4); and the bodies' memberships beyond the three named.
